# Engine ↔ app contract (M1)

Version 1, 2026-10-14. Covers milestone M1 of `../DESIGN.md` §10. DESIGN.md decides intent; this file decides names, shapes and rules. Change it only when all three developers agree. Work split: engine A builds project, ledger, comments, render, state, api, cli and rpc. Engine B builds texdiff and anchor. The app (SwiftUI) talks to the engine only through RPC.

## 1. Hard rules
- The engine is the only writer of `ledger.json` and of every PDF. The app loads PDFs with `PDFDocument(data:)`, never calls `write`/`dataRepresentation`, and draws marks as in-memory annotations or overlay views.
- The engine never writes into the student's folder. It reads that folder once, into the read-only `rN/src`.
- Only the advisor closes a comment (verdict `verified` or `wontfix`). Nothing auto-resolves. Orphans are listed, never hidden. Students install nothing and are not expected to reply (replies are M2).
- Python 3.9. Dependencies are pymupdf and the stdlib. TeX runs as subprocesses (`latexmk`, `synctex`), with `/Library/TeX/texbin` prepended to PATH because processes launched from the GUI lack it.
- All files are UTF-8. Notes may be Chinese. UI and output text is English.

## 2. Review folder
```
<paper>/
  ledger.json      the authoritative record, written only through ledger.edit()
  .lock            flock target for ledger.edit()
  rN/src/          snapshot of the student's source, files chmod 0444           ingest
  rN/build/        latexmk output: <stem>.pdf, <stem>.synctex.gz, <stem>.log, aux   ingest
  rN/analysis.json {"schema": 1, "changes": [Change…]}, rounds ≥ 2 (cache)       ingest
  rN/final.pdf     summary page(s) + build PDF + real annotations               finish
  rN/email.txt     comment list for the email                                   finish
```
`<stem>` is the main file's name without `.tex`. The engine keeps no global list of papers; the app remembers paper folder paths. If an `rN/` folder exists but its round is missing from the ledger, it is left over from a crashed ingest, and the next ingest deletes it. These are M2, not M1: prose.json, crops/, review.pdf, read state.

## 3. Coordinates and offsets
- **page** is a 0-based index into `rN/build/<stem>.pdf`, in the API, ledger and state. People see `p.{page+1}`. synctex pages are 1-based.
- **rect** is `[x0, y0, x1, y1]` in PDF points, origin at the **top-left** of the page, y pointing down, x0<x1, y0<y1, rounded to 0.01. These are PyMuPDF's page coordinates, and also what `synctex` prints.
- **PDFKit** page space has its origin at the **bottom-left**, y up. The app converts with `cb = page.bounds(for: .cropBox)`: rect ↔ `CGRect(x: cb.minX + x0, y: cb.maxY - y1, width: x1 - x0, height: y1 - y0)`. For LaTeX output cb = MediaBox = (0, 0, W, H), so this is `y_top = H - y_bottom`. Pages with /Rotate ≠ 0 are not supported in M1.
- **start/end** index the source file's text as a Python `str` (code points; decoded UTF-8, errors="replace"), with end exclusive. **lines** are 1-based and inclusive. `file` is relative to `rN/src` and uses `/`. The app never indexes source text.

## 4. ledger.json, schema 1
```json
{"schema": 1,
 "paper": {"title": "…", "student": "Lin Zhou", "main": "main.tex", "engine": "pdflatex", "created": "2026-10-14T10:00:00+08:00"},
 "rounds": [{"n": 1, "created": "…", "source": "/abs/path/v1", "closed": true, "closed_at": "…", "last_seq": 7,
             "build": {"status": "ok", "pdf": "r1/build/main.pdf", "log": "r1/build/main.log", "pages": 4, "errors": [], "warnings": []}}],
 "comments": [{"id": "R1-03", "created_round": 1, "created": "…", "source": "app", "kind": "strikeout", "note": "多余，删掉。",
               "rounds": {"1": {"anchor": Anchor, "anchor_state": "new", "confidence": 1.0, "method": "selection",
                                "verdict": null, "verdict_note": "", "verdict_at": null},
                          "2": {"anchor": Anchor, "anchor_state": "gone", "confidence": 0.86, "method": "diff",
                                "verdict": "verified", "verdict_note": "", "verdict_at": "…"}}}]}
```
Anchor = `{"file": "sections/method.tex", "start": 1234, "end": 1269, "lines": [27, 27], "quote": "…", "prefix": "…", "suffix": "…", "page": 1, "rects": [[72.0, 336.1, 223.2, 346.1]], "text": "The expected cost of a pool of size"}`
- Times are ISO 8601 local time with offset, to the second. `paper.engine` is pdflatex, xelatex or lualatex. `paper.main` is relative to src. `round.source` is the absolute path given to ingest.
- `build.status` is `ok`, `errors` or `failed` (§5). Its paths are relative to `<paper>`; pdf and log are null when missing. `errors` is `[{"file", "line", "message"}]` (file relative to src or null; line an int or null). `warnings` is a list of strings, deduplicated, in log order.
- `last_seq` is the highest sequence number ever used in that round. `id` is `R{n}-{seq:02d}`, assigned by the engine when the comment is written and never reused.
- `source` is `app` or `import`. `kind` is `highlight`, `strikeout` or `note`. `note` is the advisor's wording and may be "".
- `rounds[str(n)]` holds one record for the creation round and one for each later round the comment was carried into. The creation record holds the original anchor.
- In an Anchor, `quote = src[start:end]`, and `prefix`/`suffix` are up to 40 source characters on either side. `text` is the PDF words at the rects, joined by single spaces. When the range is not in the PDF, page is null and rects is []. For **gone**, `start == end` at the deletion point and quote is "". For **orphan**, anchor is null.
- `anchor_state` is `new` (creation round), `unchanged`, `changed`, `moved`, `gone` or `orphan` (§5 anchor). `confidence` runs 0–1, and below 0.75 is low (DESIGN §3). `method` is one of `selection`, `import`, `line`, `diff`, `exact`, `fuzzy`, `none`.
- `verdict` is null, `verified`, `reopened` or `wontfix`. It is set only by the advisor, only in the open round, and only on a comment carried into that round (`created_round < n`). `verdict_note` is free text; for `reopened` it is the reopen note (since UI v2 any verdict's note has a meaning: amendment "UI v2 integration").
- **Carrying.** A comment is closed after round n if `rounds[n].verdict` is `verified` or `wontfix`. A comment with a round-n record and a verdict there of null or `reopened` is carried into round n+1, and starts it with verdict null.
- Readers ignore unknown keys, so M2 can add read state under the same schema version.

## 5. Engine modules
`paperole/__init__.py` defines `class EngineError(Exception)` and `__version__`. Every expected failure raises it, with a message the advisor can read. Paths may be `str` or `pathlib.Path`.

**project.py**
- `snapshot(source, dest) -> [warning]` copies a folder or .zip into dest, which must not exist.
  - It skips symlinks, dot-files, `__MACOSX` and build products (`*.aux *.log *.out *.toc *.lof *.lot *.fls *.fdb_latexmk *.synctex.gz *.blg *.xdv`), and keeps `.bbl` and PDFs.
  - For a zip it rejects absolute and `..` members, and unwraps a single top-level folder.
  - It sets files to chmod 0444.
- `detect(src) -> {"main", "engine"}` picks main from the top-level `.tex` files that have an uncommented `\documentclass`. One such file is main. If there are several, `main.tex` wins; otherwise EngineError lists the candidates. engine comes from `% !TEX program = X` in main's first 20 lines, else `xelatex` if main loads fontspec, xeCJK, polyglossia or unicode-math, else `pdflatex`.
- `compile(src, build, main, engine, progress) -> {"status", "pdf", "log", "pages", "errors", "warnings"}` (absolute paths) runs, in cwd=src, `latexmk -norc -e '<Perl>' -pdf|-xelatex|-lualatex -f -synctex=1 -interaction=nonstopmode -file-line-error -no-shell-escape -outdir=<build> <main>`.
  - Before running, it mirrors every subfolder of src inside build. latexmk 4.79 does not create them, and without them `\include{chap/a}` fails with "I can't write on file chap/a.aux" (tested).
  - The timeout is 300 s. Output is captured, never inherited. `-norc` matters because a student's latexmkrc is Perl.
  - The fixed `-e` Perl (`project.SAFE_AUX`, no student string in it) makes latexmk run bibtex, biber and makeindex as its own `internal` commands that call one sub, which runs the program as an argv list, never through `/bin/sh`. latexmk otherwise builds those command lines from auxiliary-file names the student's LaTeX chooses and runs them through the shell, where a backquote or `$(…)` in the name would run (amendment "Shell escape per paper", Security).
  - Status is `failed` when there is no PDF or the run timed out, `errors` when the log has `file:line: …` or `! …` lines, and `ok` otherwise.
  - Warnings are the `LaTeX`/`Package X`/`Class X Warning:` messages with continuation lines joined. Over/underfull boxes are not warnings.
- `log_tail(log, n=40) -> str`.

**ledger.py**: `SCHEMA = 1`.
- `create(paper, title, student) -> dict` raises EngineError if a ledger exists.
- `load(paper) -> dict` takes no lock. It raises EngineError if the ledger is missing or `schema != 1`.
- `edit(paper)` is a context manager that yields the dict. It holds an exclusive `fcntl.flock` on `<paper>/.lock` for the whole read-modify-write. On a normal exit it writes `ledger.json.tmp`, fsyncs it and calls `os.replace`; on an exception it saves nothing.
- `open_round(lg)` returns the round with closed false (at most one) or None. `latest_round(lg)` returns the last round or None. `get_comment(lg, cid)` raises EngineError for an unknown id.
- `new_id(lg, n)` increments `last_seq` and returns `R{n}-{nn}`. `carried_into(lg, n)` lists the comments open at the close of round n−1, in id order.

**comments.py** works on the dict from `ledger.edit`, enforces §4 and §6, and raises EngineError.
- `create(paper, lg, page, rects, text, note, kind, source="app") -> comment` anchors with `anchor.locate` and adds a round-n record `{anchor, "new", confidence, method, verdict null}`.
- `edit(lg, cid, note)` and `delete(lg, cid)` work only on comments created in the open round. A deleted id stays used.
- `set_verdict(lg, cid, verdict, note="")` works only on comments carried into the open round. None clears the verdict.
- `import_pdf(paper, lg, pdf) -> {"ids", "skipped"}` takes an annotated copy of this round's build PDF.
  - The page count and page sizes must match, otherwise EngineError "This PDF is not round N's build PDF".
  - It runs `annots.extract`. Kinds map highlight/underline/squiggly → highlight, strikeout → strikeout, and everything else → note.
  - Rects are the quad boxes (or the rect) as `[x0, H-y1, x1, H-y0]`. Each comment goes through `create(..., source="import")` in annots order (page, top, left).
  - It skips a duplicate of a comment from this round (same page, kind and note, rects within 1 pt). Threads are ignored in M1.

**texdiff.py** (engine B)
- `diff_trees(old_src, new_src) -> TreeDiff` compares every `*.tex` file; other files are ignored in M1.
- `TreeDiff.hunks` lists Hunks in (new_file, new_start) order. A Hunk is `{"kind": "insert"|"delete"|"replace"|"move", "old_file", "old_start", "old_end", "new_file", "new_start", "new_end", "old_text", "new_text"}`.
  - An insert has `old_start == old_end` (the insertion point) and a delete has `new_start == new_end`.
  - For a file that exists on one side only, the other side's file and offsets are null. The texts are raw source.
- **Tokens** come from the regex `\\[A-Za-z@]+\*?|\\.|\w+|[^\w\s]`, run on the text with comments blanked (an unescaped `%` to the end of the line), plus one paragraph-break token per whitespace run containing a blank line. So re-wrapping is no change, and a change inside a `%` comment is no change.
- **Hunks** are the non-equal opcodes of `difflib.SequenceMatcher(autojunk=False)` on each file pair's tokens.
  - Hunks with fewer than 5 equal tokens between them, and no paragraph break, merge into one replace.
  - Where the alignment allows, a pure insert or delete is slid to start at a word rather than at punctuation.
- **Moves**: a delete and an insert (in any files) that share a run of ≥ 8 tokens become one `move` hunk over that run. Leftovers are ordinary hunks, except leftovers made only of punctuation or paragraph breaks, which are dropped.
- `map_offset(file, offset, bias="left") -> (file, offset) | None`
  - Equal and moved tokens map exactly; whitespace maps to the matching token boundary.
  - An offset inside a hunk maps to its new_start (left) or new_end (right).
  - The result is None if the file is gone and the offset is not in a move.
- `kept_ratio(file, start, end)` is the share of the range's old tokens that lie outside every hunk, or inside a move (1.0 if the range is empty). `hunks_touching(file, start, end)` returns the hunks whose old range touches `[start, end]`, ends inclusive.
- `sentence(text, start, end)` widens a range to whole sentences. A sentence ends after `.`, `?` or `!` followed by whitespace, at `\\`, or at a blank line. `paragraph(text, start, end)` widens to blank lines or the file ends. Both return `(start, end)`.

**anchor.py** (engine B). `synctex view` prints boxes as h (left), v (bottom), W and H, so a rect is `[h, v-H, h+W, v]`. `synctex edit -o {page+1}:x:y:pdf` takes the same top-left points. Input names look like `<src>/./sections/method.tex`; the part after `/./` is the path relative to src.
- `locate(src, pdf, page, rects, text) -> (Anchor, method, confidence)` builds a new comment's anchor from the PDF words whose centres lie in the rects.
  - `synctex edit` at each rect centre gives the file and line.
  - The words are searched for in the source within ±3 lines, ignoring LaTeX markup (commands, braces, math). A match gives the exact range, method `selection` and confidence 1.0.
  - Otherwise the anchor is the synctex line, method `line`, confidence 0.5.
  - The page and rects are the input's. `text` is the PDF words, or the app's `text` when no words are found.
- `to_pdf(src, pdf, file, start, end) -> (page | None, rects, text)` searches the synctex line boxes of the range's lines for PDF words matching the range's words (markup ignored).
  - Matched word boxes, merged per line, are the rects. If no words match, the line boxes are.
  - For an empty range (a deletion point), the rect is 2 pt wide and sits after the PDF word just before the point; this is best effort, and the line box is the fallback.
  - If synctex has no box, the result is `(None, [], "")`.
- `carry(old_src, src, pdf, diff, items) -> (records, changes)`. Each item is `{"id", "anchor": round n−1 anchor or None, "last": last non-null anchor}`. `records` maps each id to `{"anchor", "anchor_state", "confidence", "method"}`. R is the old range in file f, and S = `sentence(R)` in the old text. The first rule that holds wins:
  1. **gone**: R has at least one token containing a letter or digit, and all such tokens are inside delete hunks. The new range is the deletion point.
  2. **changed**: a hunk other than a move touches S (a replace in R, an insert at its edge, an edit elsewhere in its sentence).
  3. **moved**: R lies in a move hunk.
  4. **unchanged**: otherwise.

  For rules 1–4 the method is `diff` and confidence is `kept_ratio(paragraph(R))`. The new range is `[map(start, left), map(end, right)]`, widened over the new side of hunks that touch R itself.

  If R cannot be mapped, or anchor is None, `last` is searched for instead:
  - a unique exact quote match in any `.tex` file (prefix and suffix break ties) gives `moved`, method `exact`, confidence 0.9;
  - otherwise the best fuzzy token match with ratio ≥ 0.6 gives `changed`, method `fuzzy`, confidence = ratio;
  - otherwise the comment is `orphan`, method `none`, confidence 0.

  `changes` has one Change per hunk (§8), numbered `c01`, `c02`, … in hunk order.
  - Page and rects come from `to_pdf` of the new range (the point, for a delete).
  - `attached_to` is the sorted ids of carried comments whose S the hunk touches; for a move, those whose R it contains.

**render.py**: `final_pdf(paper, lg, n, out)` and `email_text(lg, n) -> str` (§9). **state.py**: `build(paper) -> State` (§8), from `ledger.load`, `rN/analysis.json`, the log tail and `email.txt`. **api.py**: one `fn(params: dict, progress: Callable[[str], None]) -> dict` per RPC method, and `METHODS = {name: fn}`. cli.py and rpc.py only translate to and from it.

## 6. Round lifecycle
- `new` creates a paper with no rounds. `ingest` creates round N = latest + 1, which requires the latest round to be closed (or no rounds yet).
  - Exception: if the open round has no comments created in it and no verdicts, ingest replaces it with the same N. This covers a re-submission and a retry with main/engine after a failed build.
- Ingest runs these steps:
  1. snapshot.
  2. In round 1, or when main/engine is given, detect or overwrite `paper.main`/`paper.engine`. Later rounds reuse them.
  3. compile.
  4. Unless the build `failed`, and only for N ≥ 2: texdiff of r(N−1)/src against rN/src, then `anchor.carry`, then the round-N records go to the ledger and the changes to analysis.json.
- A `failed` build leaves the round open with no carried records.
- `comment`, `edit_comment`, `delete_comment`, `verdict`, `import` and `finish` need an open round with a build PDF.
- `finish` writes final.pdf and email.txt, then sets `closed`/`closed_at`. A closed round is frozen, and round N+1 may now be ingested. In M2, closing round 1 will mark all text read; M1 has no read state.

## 7. CLI and RPC
**RPC** (`paperole rpc`) speaks JSON lines over stdin/stdout, one UTF-8 object per line.
- A request is `{"id": 7, "method": "…", "params": {…}}`. The reply is `{"id": 7, "result": {…}}` or `{"id": 7, "error": {"message": "…"}}`.
- While a request runs, the engine may send `{"event": "progress", "id": 7, "message": "Compiling with pdflatex…"}`.
- Requests run one at a time, in order. EOF exits with 0. stdout carries protocol lines only; diagnostics go to stderr.
- An unexpected exception replies `"Internal error: <Type>: <text>"`, writes the traceback to stderr, and keeps serving.

**CLI** is `paperole = paperole.cli:main`, or `python -m paperole.cli` (the package was `papercollab` until 2026-10-27). It prints `result` as indented JSON and exits 0, or prints the error to stderr and exits 1. Progress goes to stderr. Pages are 0-based here too.

`paper` is always the absolute path of the paper folder, and every result contains `"state"`.

| method | params | result | CLI |
|---|---|---|---|
| new | paper, title, student | {state} | `new P --title T --student S` |
| ingest | paper, source, main?, engine? | {state} | `ingest P SRC [--main F] [--engine E]` |
| comment | paper, page, rects, text, note, kind | {id, state} | `comment P --page 0 --rects "x0,y0,x1,y1;…" --text T --note N [--kind highlight]` |
| edit_comment | paper, id, note | {state} | `edit P R2-01 --note N` |
| delete_comment | paper, id | {state} | `delete P R2-01` |
| verdict | paper, id, verdict (verified/reopened/wontfix/null), note? | {state} | `verdict P R1-03 verified\|reopened\|wontfix\|clear [-m NOTE]` |
| import | paper, pdf | {ids, skipped, state} | `import P FILE.pdf` |
| finish | paper | {state} | `finish P` |
| state | paper | {state} | `state P` |

## 8. State JSON (what the app renders)
```json
{"schema": 1,
 "paper": {"path": "/abs/paper", "title": "…", "student": "…", "main": "main.tex", "engine": "pdflatex"},
 "rounds": [{"n": 1, "created": "…", "source": "…", "closed": true, "closed_at": "…", "status": "ok"}],
 "round": {"n": 2, "closed": false, "created": "…", "closed_at": null,
           "build": {"status": "ok", "pdf": "/abs/paper/r2/build/main.pdf", "pages": 4, "warnings": [], "errors": [], "log_tail": "…"},
           "final_pdf": null, "email": null},
 "comments": [{"id": "R1-02", "kind": "highlight", "note": "…", "source": "app", "created_round": 1, "carried": true,
               "verdict": null, "verdict_note": "", "anchor_state": "changed", "confidence": 0.92, "method": "diff",
               "page": 0, "rects": [[411.2, 661.0, 540.0, 671.0]], "text": "Both approaches reason about …",
               "old_quote": "Both approaches decide when to keep a\nsingle instance alive.", "new_quote": "…",
               "file": "main.tex", "line": 77, "changes": ["c02"],
               "history": [{"round": 1, "anchor_state": "new", "verdict": null, "verdict_note": ""}]}],
 "changes": [{"id": "c01", "kind": "replace", "file": "main.tex", "line": 41, "old_text": "simple", "new_text": "static",
              "page": 0, "rects": [[181.7, 391.0, 209.4, 401.0]], "attached_to": ["R1-01"], "unrequested": false}]}
```
- `round` is the latest round, open or closed, and is null before the first ingest. Paths are absolute. `final_pdf` and `email` are set once the round is closed. When the build failed, `build.pdf` is null and comments and changes are [].
- `comments` are this round's comments: the carried ones and the new ones (`carried = created_round < round.n`). They are sorted by page (null last), then by the first rect's top, then left, then id.
  - `old_quote` is the previous round's quote (the last known one if it was an orphan there), or null for new.
  - `new_quote` is this round's quote: "" when gone, null when orphan.
  - `file`/`line` come from this round's anchor and are null when orphan. `changes` lists the attached change ids.
  - `history` holds the earlier rounds' records, oldest first.
- `changes` comes from analysis.json and is [] in round 1. `file`/`line` refer to the new side, or the deletion point for a delete. The texts are whitespace-collapsed. `unrequested` is `attached_to == []`.

## 9. Final PDF and email (render.py)
The words, groups and colours below are M1's. final.pdf is now in the margin style and uses the student-facing words of UI v2: amendments "Final PDF in the margin style", "final.pdf without annotations" and "Student-facing words" (its follow-up sign: "UI v2 review fixes").
REPLY_RULE (one line, used by both): `No need to list what you fixed. Reply only for comments you did not change (say why), fixed elsewhere (say where), or have a question about: one line each, starting with the ID, e.g. "R1-05 …".`
- **final.pdf**: summary page(s), at the size of page 0, inserted before the build PDF's pages.
  - The summary opens with a header `Paperole · Round N review · <date>` (PaperCollab until 2026-10-27), the heading `Review of Round N`, the title, and `Student: S · source received <round.created> · reviewed <date> · page numbers are the paper's (this summary is an extra first page)`.
  - Then REPLY_RULE and `To do for Round N+1: <open ids>`.
  - Then the groups Reopened, Still open, New comments, Verified (ids on one line) and Won't fix. Each entry shows the id, `p.X` (or `location not found`), the quote (≤ 60 chars), the note, and `Round N: <verdict_note>` if set.
  - The summary is laid out as HTML (`page.insert_htmlbox` or `Story`) so Chinese renders through MuPDF's built-in CJK fallback (checked with PyMuPDF 1.26.5).
- **Annotations**: every comment of round N that has a page gets a real annotation on page `page + summary_pages`.
  - highlight → Highlight and strikeout → StrikeOut, with quads from the rects. note → Text at (x1, y0) of the first rect. A **gone** comment is always a Text at the deletion point.
  - `/Contents` is `[R2-01] <note>` for a new comment, or `[R1-03 · reopened|still open|verified|won't fix] <note>` for a carried one, plus `\nRound N: <verdict_note>` when set. Colours are as in §10.
- M2 adds unrequested changes and build health to the summary, the review margin, XMP, and render numbers.
- **email.txt** (also `state.round.email`). The groups appear in this order and empty groups are omitted, as is an empty Closed line. The quote is the anchor's `text`, whitespace-collapsed and cut to 60 chars with `…`. Note lines are indented by two spaces.
```
Round 2 review: <title>

<REPLY_RULE>

Reopened (2)
R1-02  p.1  "Both approaches reason about the lifetime of one instance…"
  这句不准确：Hybrid 也决定预热窗口。
  Round 2: 还是没说清楚和 Hybrid 的区别。
…

Still open (1)
…
New comments (3)
…
Closed: R1-01, R1-03, R1-04 (verified); R1-07 (won't fix)
```

## 10. Colours and app notes
M1's table and keys. The app's status colours and keys are now those of UI v2 (amendments "Thread cards" and "UI v2 integration"); the unrequested colour is slate teal (amendment "App, 2026-10-16").
| meaning | colour |
|---|---|
| new comment | blue `#2563EB` |
| carried, no verdict yet ("still open") | amber `#F59E0B` |
| reopened | red `#DC2626` |
| verified | green `#16A34A` |
| won't fix | grey `#6B7280` |
| unrequested change | purple `#7C3AED` |
| attached change (app only) | light green fill `#86EFAC`; a delete is drawn as a small × |

- The app launches `<repo>/engine/.venv/bin/python -m paperole.cli rpc` once, and restarts it if it exits, with the engine's folder first on `PYTHONPATH` (the official app's snapshot, else the checkout's `engine/`; mac/README.md, Engine path).
- In an open round with a PDF, a verdict is allowed when `carried`, and edit/delete when not.
- A comment needs attention when it is `orphan`, `confidence < 0.75`, or `page` is null.
- V/R/W send verified/reopened/wontfix; pressing the current verdict's key again sends null.
- A new comment's rects are `PDFSelection.selectionsByLine()` bounds converted as in §3; its `text` is `selection.string`.

## Amendments
Engine A (core), 2026-10-14. Each item is the smallest choice where the contract was silent or had to change.
- **New comments are located in comments.py.** The M1 task gave the PDF → tex step to engine A, so `comments.create` calls `comments.locate(src, pdf, page, rects, text)`, which has the same signature and return value as §5 `anchor.locate`. `anchor.locate` is not called. Integration should keep only one of the two.
  - synctex gives the file and lines at each rect's centre. The PDF words are then searched for within ±3 lines in de-TeXed source: comments, macro names, braces and non-text arguments (`\label`, `\cite`, `\ref`, …) are dropped, and only letters and digits are compared after NFKD and case folding, so hyphenation, ligatures, accents and `\%` do not matter. An exact match is tried first, then the cheapest fuzzy alignment with at most one edit per 4 characters. Either one gives `selection` with confidence 1.0; otherwise the anchor is the synctex lines (`line`, 0.5).
  - Math keeps its letters (Greek and `\log`-style macros are mapped), but math is atomic: a range that cuts into `$…$`, `\[…\]` or an equation-like environment widens to the whole span. A selection inside a display equation therefore anchors to the whole `\begin{equation}…\end{equation}` (DESIGN §5).
  - If synctex finds no position inside src (for example a BibTeX `.bbl`), the words are searched for in every `.tex` file. If they are not found, the comment is refused with an EngineError.
- **Anchor has 3 more keys**, which readers may ignore: `section`, `env` and `position_guessed`.
  - `section` is the title of the innermost `\chapter`, `\section`, `\subsection` or `\subsubsection` at or before the start, or null.
  - `env` is the innermost environment that contains the range, other than `document`, or null.
  - `position_guessed` is true when no PDF word's centre lies in the rects. The comment then anchors to the source line of the nearest text line, with method `line` and confidence 0.5.
- **A note at a click point** is sent as a zero-size rect `[x, y, x, y]` with text "". Rects may have x0 == x1 and y0 == y1.
- `project.detect(src, main=None)`: when `main` is given (`--main`), it is checked and only the engine is detected.
- `new` refuses a folder that has no ledger and contains anything other than dot-files, so a mistyped path cannot write into a student's folder.
- When `ingest` replaces an open round, the round keeps its `last_seq`, so deleted ids stay unused.
- Compile sets `max_print_line=10000`, so log lines are not wrapped.
  - An error in a file outside src has `file` and `line` null, and its message starts with `name.ext:line:`.
  - A failed build whose log shows no error gets one error that holds the timeout, or the last lines of latexmk's output.
- `import` result: each `skipped` entry is `{"page", "kind", "note", "reason": "duplicate of R1-02"}`.
- State: an orphan has `text` "" (with `page` null and `rects` []).
- Email and summary quote: when the round's anchor `text` is empty (gone, or a note at a point), the most recent earlier non-empty text is used. When there is none, the quote is left out. The summary shows only the date part of `round.created`.
- **final.pdf** is made by inserting the summary pages into the build PDF. `insert_pdf` of the build into a new document drops hyperref's named-destination links. Fonts are subset (the CJK fallback font is several MB otherwise).

App (SwiftUI, `mac/`), 2026-10-14.
- Changed text is drawn as a band (3 pt) along the bottom of each rect, not as a full fill: light green `#86EFAC` when attached, purple `#7C3AED` when unrequested. A fill mixed with the comment highlights' verdict colours where both overlap. A delete is still a small ×.
- A selection that crosses a page break becomes a comment on its first page, with only the lines on that page (§4 allows one page per comment).
- A note at a click point is sent as the zero-size rect from engine A's amendment above, with kind `note`.
- `--fixture <state.json>` (debug, no engine) resolves a relative `round.build.pdf` against the fixture's folder; real state always has absolute paths.

Engine B (texdiff, anchor), 2026-10-14.
- **Tokens.** A line that holds only a `%` comment is not a blank line (as in TeX), so it is no paragraph break. A student's `% R1-03` line inside a paragraph is therefore no change.
- **Diff.** Paragraphs (runs of tokens between paragraph breaks) are diffed as units first, and only the differing regions token by token, each with `SequenceMatcher(autojunk=False)`. A flat token diff took 3.3 s on a 30k-token file and 57 s on a 90k-token one; this takes 0.06 s and 0.83 s. The fixture gives the same hunks.
- **Sliding.** Among the valid positions of a pure insert or delete, the engine prefers one that starts at a word, then one that ends at punctuation, then the smallest shift. So a deleted sentence is `It is cheap.`, not `is cheap. It`, and a comment on it becomes `gone`.
- **Points.** An insert's `old_start` and a delete's `new_start` are just after the last token before the hunk, not counting paragraph breaks. So a sentence inserted after another one touches that sentence. In `map_offset` a paragraph break counts as whitespace. In `carry` an empty range (a `gone` comment that is carried again) maps with the right bias, so it keeps its point.
- **Leftovers of a move** that hold only punctuation or paragraph breaks are not in `hunks`, but their tokens still map to the deletion point.
- **TreeDiff** also exposes `old_texts`, `new_texts`, `old_tokens`, `new_tokens` (`{file: text}` and `{file: Tokens}`) and `tokens_in(file, start, end)`. anchor uses them.
- **A whole deleted file**: a comment in it cannot be mapped, so `last` is searched for (it may have moved elsewhere). Otherwise it becomes `orphan`, not `gone`, because there is no deletion point.
- **Fallback search.** The exact match compares tokens, so re-wrapping and comments do not matter. Ties are broken by how many prefix and suffix tokens match. A quote that still ties, or a fuzzy window that ties with one at another place, gives `orphan` rather than a guess.
- **anchor.locate** (not called while comments.locate is used; see engine A's note): the selection is aligned letter by letter against the whole page, so a short word lands on the right occurrence. A selection, or a synctex line, inside a display-math environment anchors to the whole environment with `selection` and confidence 1.0. Punctuation that ends the app's `text` is included when the source has it. When synctex gives no file under src, it raises EngineError "No LaTeX source was found for this spot on page N.", as comments.locate does.
- **to_pdf.** A range inside display math gives one rect: the union of the environment's line boxes. When the range's own lines match fewer than half of its letters, the lines of its whole paragraph are tried, because synctex puts a macro argument that spans lines (such as `\caption{…}`) on the line where it ends. A range over two pages gives rects on the page that holds more of it.
- **Change.** It also has `unrequested` (= `attached_to == []`). For a hunk in a deleted file, `file` is the old file, and `line` and `page` are null. A record's confidence is rounded to 3 decimals.

Integration, 2026-10-14.
- **One locator.** `anchor.locate` is removed. `comments.locate` (engine A's note above) is the only PDF → source step, so read §5 `anchor.locate` as `comments.locate`. Engine B's locate tests now run against it. Two of engine B's rules were carried over:
  - synctex gives display math the line where it ends, so a match inside the display formula that holds that line counts as on that line. A word selected in Eq. (2) no longer lands on the same word in the next line.
  - A selection of only symbols in display math (`=`, `(2)`) anchors to the whole environment with `selection`, 1.0, instead of to the `\end{equation}` line.
- anchor's `synctex view` calls use `project.tex_env()`, so `/Library/TeX/texbin` is set in one place.
- Anchors made by `carry` have no `section`, `env` or `position_guessed`; only a comment's creation-round anchor has them.
- App: changed text is drawn as a 2 pt band just below each rect, which replaces the 3 pt band along its bottom (App note above). PDFKit composites annotation drawing over the page, so the multiply blend had no effect and the band hid the descenders.
- App: `--debug-open-comment <phrase>` selects the phrase in an open round's PDF and opens the new-comment popover.

Fix round, 2026-10-14 (after the engine and UX reviews).
- **texdiff moves.** The deleted and inserted sides of replace hunks are move candidates too, so a sentence moved next to an edit is a move. What a move leaves of the hunks it was cut from is rebuilt: an old and a new piece between the same moved tokens (or beside one and of a similar size: the larger at most 2× the smaller + 5 tokens), or left of one replace, become a replace there, so a word edited inside a moved paragraph is a replace at its new place. A piece between two moved tokens that stay neighbours goes between them, punctuation beside a moved token goes with it, and any other piece keeps its hunk's position. Paragraph breaks at a piece's ends are left out. An edit in a renamed file is now one replace.
- **carry.**
  - `moved` is judged on word tokens, so a sentence whose final `.` was left out of the move is still moved.
  - Before `gone`: if the deleted text (its whole sentence when all of it was deleted, else the range; at least 3 words) was inserted again verbatim, by an insert or replace, exactly once, the comment is `moved`, method `exact`, 0.9, and that hunk is attached to it. If it was inserted several times, or at least half of it was inserted again as one run, the comment is `gone` with confidence at most 0.5.
  - A point range (a `gone` comment carried again) stays `gone`, or is `changed` when a hunk touches its point; it is never `unchanged`.
  - The new range is widened only over touching hunks whose new side is inside it or next to it. A mapped range of more than 2 × (the range's tokens + those hunks' new tokens) + 8 tokens is not trusted, and the quote is searched for instead (§5 fallback).
  - An item may have `"guessed": true`; api sets it when the comment's creation record has method `line` (a click-placed note, or a selection that fell back to its synctex line). Its confidence is then at most 0.5 in every later round, so it keeps needing attention.
  - A `move` change is attached to the comments whose range it overlaps (was: contains), since an edit splits a move into pieces.
- **comments.locate.** Among equal matches, one that starts and ends at source word boundaries wins, then the one whose source around it agrees furthest with the 3 PDF words on each side of the selection, then the one nearest synctex's lines. When fewer than 6 letters around the match agree, every `.tex` file is searched for an exact match, and one with at least 6 agreeing letters and more than the first replaces it (synctex can name the wrong file near inline math, and `\maketitle` prints the title far from `\title`). When synctex names the build's `.bbl` and no file in src, the comment anchors to the first `\bibliography{…}` or `\printbibliography` line, method `line`, 0.5, `position_guessed` true; it is no longer refused.
- **Interrupted ingest.** Every ledger-locked call, and `state` when an `rN.old` exists, first restores a round that a killed re-ingest left in `rN.old` (when the ledger has round N), or deletes the `rN.old` (when it does not).
- **State.** `old_quote` is the most recent non-empty earlier quote, so a comment that was `gone` last round shows the text it was on.
- **App.**
  - A card shows each earlier round's verdict note as `Round N (reopened): …` in that verdict's colour.
  - A note being written is kept until it is saved or discarded. The new-comment popover does not close on a click elsewhere while its note is changed, Cancel or Esc asks once more, and Save keeps it open until the engine has saved the comment. A new comment's note is edited in a sheet.
  - A request sent while another runs waits for it instead of being dropped, so switching papers or adding one during an ingest works. A reply older than the state shown is not applied.
  - Debug: `--debug-note <text>` (with `--debug-open-comment`) and `--debug-switch-to <folder>`.

Review-mode fixes, 2026-10-14 (a paper in ICLR's review mode, three rounds).
- **Margin line numbers.** On a page, digit-only words of which at least 5 share a left or right edge, run down at least half the height of the page's words, and lie left of every other word on the page or right of every other word, are line numbers in the margin (lineno, or the ruler that ICLR and NeurIPS review modes draw at shipout). They are not words anywhere: not in a selection's picked words or `text`, not a click note's nearest line, and not in `to_pdf`'s matching or `text`.
- **Click notes.** The nearest line is the nearest by height, then by horizontal distance, so a click beside a line of the other column or of a wrapped table gets its own column's line. When synctex names only lines with no text outside `%` comments (where a paragraph ended), the anchor is the nearest line with text above, else below. A click note's range is never empty, so it is never taken for a deletion point (`gone`).
- **Reference lists.** The `.bbl` that TeX read is a source file. It is `rN/src/X.bbl` when that exists (TeX reads its working folder first), else the one BibTeX wrote, `rN/build/X.bbl`, which is still named `X.bbl` as if it were in src. So `file` may name a file of `rN/build/`.
  - `comments.locate`: a selection in the reference list is searched for in that file (`selection`, 1.0); a click gives its line (`line`, 0.5). The `\bibliography` line is used only when the file is missing.
  - `texdiff.diff_trees(old_src, new_src, old_build=None, new_build=None)` compares every `*.tex` file and these `.bbl` files, when they hold `\bibitem` (biblatex's `.bbl` holds entry data, not the printed list). api passes both rounds' build folders. So a student's `.bib` edit is a change exactly where it changes the printed reference list; reordered entries and unprinted fields are no change.
- **to_pdf.**
  - A display's page is the one where most of its letters match the words in its line boxes (was: most words), ties going to its last line's page. Its rect is one box around the matched words and the line boxes that hold them, or around the line boxes when nothing matches. When a page breaks just before a display, synctex also gives its first lines the previous page's shipout boxes.
  - When fewer than half of a range's letters match inside its line boxes (and its paragraph's), all words of those boxes' pages are tried. synctex gives the inside of a `\resizebox` or adjustbox its unscaled place.
  - When no word of a non-empty range matches (the range prints nothing, such as `\bibliography{…}` in an older anchor), carry's last known PDF `text` is looked for (letters and digits, at least 4) from the page of the range's boxes outwards, nearest those boxes. `to_pdf(src, pdf, file, start, end, hint="")` takes it as `hint`.
  - Line boxes that start at the page's edge (pictures drawn at shipout) are never rects, and every rect is clipped to the page.
- **PDF text** (an anchor's `text`, and so the quotes): a word that ends a line with a hyphen after a letter is joined, without the hyphen, to the next line's first word when that starts in lower case.
- **Render.** When round N's record has no verdict note, the latest earlier one is shown as `Round K (reopened): <note>` (email, summary and annotation), so a comment reopened earlier keeps its reason. Highlight annotations in final.pdf have opacity 0.35, so the words under them stay readable.

Drag selection, 2026-10-15 (some text was hard to select completely).
- **`build.text_x`**, in the ledger's round `build` (beside `pages`) and in state's `round.build`: for each page of the build PDF, `[x0, x1]`, the horizontal extent (contract x, rounded to 0.01) of the page's words other than margin line numbers (the ruler rule of the review-mode fixes above). Running heads, page numbers and both columns of a two-column page count, so it is the union of the columns. It is `null` for a page with no such words and `[]` when the build failed. Ingest computes it (`comments.text_x`); for a round ingested before this, state works it out from the PDF once per engine process (about 6 ms a page).
- **App.** The app tracks a left-button single click, plain or with shift, itself (`ReviewPDFView.mouseDown`), because PDFView starts and ends a selection at the nearest character. Beside a review ruler, that character is a line number, and the selection then took in the ruler, the running head and the text from the top of the page. Right of a short line, it is on the line above. The app's loop reads the drag and mouse-up events with `window.nextEvent`, keeps each end on the text of its row, sets `document.selection(from:at:to:at:)`, and autoscrolls. A click without a drag clears the selection. With shift, the selection runs from the current one's first character to the mouse, or from the mouse to the current one's end when the mouse is before it. A click that starts on a link or another interactive annotation (`Link`, `Widget`, `Text`, `FileAttachment`), a double or triple click, a right-click and a click with command, control or option go to PDFView unchanged. The app no longer replaces any method at runtime.
  - The row of a point is computed from the page's PDFKit lines inside `text_x` (`Geometry.clampedPoint`). Among the lines whose middle lies within 6 pt of the point's height, the row is the one over the point's x first. Otherwise it is one of the column nearest the point (a line and the lines within 30 pt of the point that overlap it horizontally), and then the one at the least vertical distance. Lines sharing at least half its height belong to the row. A point left of the row moves 0.5 pt inside its first glyph, and a point right of the row 0.5 pt inside its last glyph, level with that line's middle. A point over the row stays. A point inside `text_x` also stays when the nearest text at its x does not overlap the row horizontally, because it is then over another column or a figure. With no line near, `text_x` decides as before (a point more than 1 pt outside it moves onto its edge). Pages whose `text_x` is null or missing are not clamped.

M2a engine, 2026-10-15 (cards with full old and new sentences, mark colours and kinds, box comments, re-anchoring; `mac/UX.md` §15). Ledger schema stays 1: readers ignore unknown keys, and records written before this have no `color` (yellow) and no `context` (state works it out).
- **Colours and kinds.** A comment has `color`: `yellow` (default), `green`, `blue`, `pink`, `purple` or `orange`, with fill/ink `#EAB308/#A16207`, `#4ADE80/#15803D`, `#60A5FA/#1D4ED8`, `#F472B6/#BE185D`, `#A78BFA/#6D28D9`, `#FB923C/#C2410C` (`comments.PALETTE`). `kind` is `highlight`, `underline`, `strikeout`, `note` or `box`. The §10 status colours no longer colour marks: status is on the app's chips and labels, and in final.pdf's `/Contents`.
- **RPC and CLI.**
  - `comment` takes `color?` (`--color`, default yellow). `kind: "box"` needs exactly one rect, the box.
  - `edit_comment` takes `note?`, `kind?`, `color?`; it changes only what is given (not null) and refuses a call with none of them ("Nothing to change: …"). `note` and `kind` are for comments created in the open round, `color` for any comment of the open round. `kind` cannot make a box or turn a box into anything else, and a note at a point (zero-size rects) stays a note: "R3-02 is a box; re-anchor it to change its kind." CLI `edit P ID [--note N] [--kind K] [--color C]`.
  - New **`reanchor`**: paper, id, page, rects, text?, kind? → {state}; CLI `reanchor P ID --page 0 --rects "…" [--text T] [--kind K]`. For any comment of the open round. The new place is located as `comment` locates it (a box when `kind` is box). Without `kind`, a box comment placed on text becomes `highlight`, and any other comment keeps its kind. For a comment created in the open round, it replaces the creation anchor (method and confidence from locating). For a carried comment, it replaces this round's record's anchor, with method **`advisor`** and confidence 1.0, and `anchor_state` `moved` when its PDF text equals the last earlier non-empty one (letters and digits compared), else `changed`. Verdict fields stay. The round's changes are then re-attached: the comment is removed from every `attached_to` and added to the changes whose `file` is its file and whose `line` lies within the lines of `texdiff.sentence` of its new range; `unrequested` is updated in analysis.json. Later rounds carry from this record. `carry`'s `guessed` (amendment "Fix round") now looks at the latest record whose method is `selection`, `import`, `line` or `advisor`, so a click note the advisor re-anchored on text is no longer capped at 0.5.
  - `method` gains `advisor` (above) and `label` (box comments, below).
- **Box comments** (`comments.locate_box`). `synctex edit` at the box's centre names a line. The innermost environment holding it whose name (without `*`) is figure, table, wrapfigure, wraptable, subfigure, subtable, algorithm, equation, align, gather, multline, flalign, alignat, eqnarray or displaymath, or a `\[…\]`/`$$…$$` display, is the anchor as a whole, from `\begin` to the end of `\end` (`selection`, 1.0, `position_guessed` false). Otherwise the box is located like a selection with the box as its one rect: the words inside it (`selection`, 1.0), else the nearest line (`line`, 0.5, `position_guessed` true). A box is never refused for having no words.
  - The anchor's `rects` is `[box]`. Its `text` is the PDF words of the environment's last `\caption` (not one of a nested subfigure), else of the environment, else the app's text.
  - A box comment's anchor has two more keys, in every round: `label`, the environment's last own `\label` (or null), and `ref`, the bounding box `[x0, y0, x1, y1]` of the PDF words of that caption (else of the range) when they are on the anchor's page (else null).
  - **Carry.** For a box anchored to a whole environment, the sentence S of §5 carry is the environment itself (an edit in the next paragraph does not touch it). When neither the diff nor the quote search finds it, the environment holding the same `\label` in the new tree is the anchor: `changed`, method `label`, confidence 0.8. A found range is widened to the innermost such environment that holds it. Every carried box (not `gone`) gets `rects = [the previous round's box moved with ref]`: the box keeps its offset from the ref's centre across, and from the ref's top when the box's middle is above the ref (a figure above its caption), its bottom when below (a table below its caption), else its centre; it is clipped to the page. Without an earlier ref, the rect is the new ref.
- **Card context.** Every round record has `context`: a Context or null. It is computed when the record is made (`comment`, `import`, carry at `ingest`, `reanchor`) from that round's build PDF and source, and stored in the ledger. For a record from before this amendment, `state` works it out once per engine process (about 0.9 s on the first `state` of a 25-page paper with 16 comments over 3 rounds; then 2 ms). Context:
  ```json
  {"display": "text", "page": 0, "rects": [[…]], "clip": null, "text": "On the first front, … boots a minimal microVM in about 125 ms, …",
   "sel": [[95, 131]], "cut": null, "changed": [[12, 19]], "against": 1}
  ```
  - Offsets index `text` as a Python str: **Unicode code points** (Swift: `text.unicodeScalars`, not `count` or UTF-16). Spans are `[start, end)`.
  - `page` and `rects` are the record's anchor's. Null context: an orphan, an anchor without page or rects, or a missing PDF.
  - **`display: "text"`**: `text` is the whole sentence(s) holding the anchor as that round's PDF prints them: PyMuPDF words in reading order, without margin line numbers, ligatures spelt out, joined by single spaces. A line-end hyphen followed by a lower-case word is dropped unless the anchor's source file has the hyphenated word (`preference-aligned` stays). Paragraphs break where the next text line starts more than 0.6 × the line height below the previous one, where it starts above it (unless it is to its right, and, when more than two lines up, at least 80% as wide: a sentence flows into the next column, but not into the text of a figure at that column's top), and before a line starting with a bullet. A sentence ends after `.` `?` `!` (before closing brackets and quotes) when the next word does not start in lower case and the word is not an abbreviation (`al. e.g. i.e. cf. vs. Fig. Eq. Sec. Tab. No. Ref. …`, `cards.ABBREVIATIONS`), an initial (`J.`) or dotted (`U.S.`), and at a paragraph break; `3.5` is one word. A caption (`Figure 3:`, `Table 1.`, …) or list item is taken whole. A sentence cut by a page or column break (a float at the column's top) is completed from the other side when the ≥ 8 letters on each side of the break follow each other in the source. At most 1200 characters are kept, centred on the marked part and cut at spaces, with `… ` / ` …` where text was left out.
  - `sel`: the spans of the anchor's words (those whose centre lies in `rects`), consecutive words merged. `[]` for a note at a point (`text` is then the sentence of the nearest word) and for `gone`.
  - `cut` (gone only): the deletion point, after the word before it; `text` is that word's sentence, plus the next one when the point ends a sentence. When the point's word ends a page or column in mid-sentence, `cut` is the end of that sentence (synctex placed the point on the page where its line starts).
  - `changed` and `against`: in a record of a later round than the creation, `changed` lists the spans of the words of `text` not in the reference round's text (the latest round after the creation and before this one in which the comment was reopened, else the creation round; `against` is that round), by a word diff with punctuation split off; `[p, p]` marks words of the reference left out at p. `[]` and null in the creation round, for images, for `gone`, and when the reference has no text context.
  - **`display: "image"`** for a box; a range (or deletion point) in display math, `tabular`/`tabularx`/`tabulary`/`longtable`/`array`, `tikzpicture`, `algorithm`/`algorithmic`/`algorithm2e`, or in a float outside its `\caption`; and a range with no PDF word in its rects. `clip` is the region to crop, in contract coordinates: the rects' bounds plus 6 pt, at least 200 × 48 pt around their centre (not for a box: as drawn), clipped to the page. `text` is the anchor's PDF text collapsed (alt text); `sel` is `[]`. **The engine writes no crop images**: the app renders `clip` from `rounds[].pdf` (UX §4).
- **State.**
  - `comments[]` gain `color` and `context` (this round's). `history[]` entries gain `page` (the anchor's, or null) and `context`, so the card's "When commented" block is `history[0].context` (or `context` for a new comment), "Reopened · Rk" is the entry with `verdict: "reopened"`, and "Now" is `context`.
  - `rounds[]` entries gain `pdf`: the absolute build PDF path, or null when the build failed.
- **final.pdf** annotations use the comment's colour: `highlight` → Highlight in its fill at opacity 0.35; `underline` → Underline, `strikeout` → StrikeOut, `box` → Square (1.5 pt border) and `note` (and any `gone` comment) → Text, in its ink.
- **Import.** Highlight → highlight; Underline and Squiggly → underline; StrikeOut → strikeout; Square and Circle → box (the annotation's rect); everything else → note. The colour is the palette entry whose fill or ink is nearest to the annotation's `/C` (yellow without one). `annots.extract` comments gain `color` (the `/C` components).
- **Margin line numbers** (amendment "Review-mode fixes"): a column of digit words also counts when, though shorter than half the page, it has at least 10 numbers counting up by one from top to bottom: ACL review mode numbers only a column's text lines, so its ruler is short beside floats (3 of 31 pages of a long ACL paper). Only words that `int()` reads count as digits.

M2a integration, 2026-10-15 (findings on an ACL two-column review-mode paper, and the app).
- **Locating a selection** (`comments.locate`).
  - **Where.** An exact match near synctex's lines (±3) first, then an exact match anywhere in the paragraph that holds them (blank lines bound it; at most 60 lines), then a fuzzy match near them, then in the paragraph. In two-column layouts synctex often names a paragraph's last line for all its lines.
  - **What \cite and \ref print** (`comments.printed`). When the PDF words are not found as they are, they are matched without author-year and numeric citations (`(Wang et al., 2026)`, `Chen and Li (2025)`, `[3, 5]`, and a citation cut by the selection's edge), without the numbers after `Section`, `Figure`, `Table`, `Eq.`, `§`, `Appendix`, … (`comments.REF_NUMBER`), and without a footnote mark glued to the first word (`1See`). The range then takes in the `\cite…` commands the selection starts or ends in. A selection of only citations is the `\cite…` commands where the words before and after it meet. `subref`, `nameref`, `vref`, `citealt`, `citeyear(par)`, `Citet`/`Citep`/`Citealp`/`Citealt`/`Citeauthor`, `parencite`, `textcite` and `autocite` print nothing of their arguments.
  - Among equal matches, fewer than 3 letters of agreement with the words around the selection count as none (chance), so the match nearest synctex's lines wins.
  - A range that starts just inside a `{…}` group that it closes widens to the group and the command before it (`\textit{Sysprompt} alone`, not `Sysprompt} alone`); one that ends just before the `}` of a group it opened takes the `}`.
  - **Words drawn in a picture.** When synctex names only a line holding one `\includegraphics` (it does for any point inside an included picture) and the words are not found, or are found on other lines (a caption with the same words as an axis label), the anchor is that `\includegraphics` command (`selection`, 1.0). Its card is an image. In carry and change attachment (`texdiff.sentence`) such a range is its own sentence, so a caption edit next to it does not touch it.
  - **Click notes.** The nearest line is taken among lines whose height is within `comments.LEVEL` (2 pt) of the nearest, then by horizontal distance (the two columns' lines are rarely at exactly the same height). A page number (a lone number that is the page's lowest or highest line) is never the nearest line.
- **Margin line numbers.** `comments.ruler_edges(pdf)`: the shared edges of the rulers found on any page of the PDF; on every page, digit-only words within 1 pt of such an edge and outside the page's other words are line numbers too, however few (a page of figures with one numbered line). `body_words(words, edges)`.
- **`build.columns`** (ingest, beside `text_x`, and in state's `round.build`; worked out once per engine process for older rounds): per page, `[x of the gap between two text columns, half the page's height]`, or null when the page is not set in two columns (at least 5 lines at least 30% as wide as its text wholly left of its middle and 5 wholly right, outnumbering such lines across it). `comments.layout(pdf)` computes `text_x` and `columns` in one pass.
- **Comment order** (state §8): by page, then, on a two-column page, a first rect across both columns in the top half, the left column, the right column, one across both columns in the bottom half; then top, left, id.
- **texdiff.**
  - **Renamed files.** A file only in the old tree and one with the same extension only in the new tree whose tokens share at least 80% (`texdiff.RENAMED`) are diffed as one file: `TreeDiff.renamed` maps old to new, and their hunks have different `old_file` and `new_file`. No whole-file moves.
  - **Replaced pictures.** For each `\includegraphics` that is the same in both trees (`\graphicspath` folders tried) but whose graphics file draws differently (equal bytes, or for PDFs the same pages' content streams and images, are the same: a plot saved again differs only in its dates and ID), `hunks` has a `replace` over the command with `"figure"`: the file, relative to src. The Change (§8) has `figure` too. Comments whose sentence holds the command (a box on the figure, a picture anchor) are `changed` and attached.
  - **`sentence()`**: a range in a `\bibitem` entry widens to the entry (`texdiff.bibitem`), so a fixed venue or year touches a comment on the title.
- **carry.** An `insert` hunk whose new text ends just before the new place of a range's sentence, with no blank line between, touches that sentence and is attached to the comment (the motivation the advisor asked for, added at the start of the paragraph). A note at a point (`"point": true`, from api) whose range prints nothing keeps its earlier page and point.
- **to_pdf.**
  - Words that a `\cite` or `\ref` at a range's edge prints are part of its rects (`(Wang et al., 2026).` after a commented sentence). A range of only such commands is drawn on the words they print. A range that prints no word of its own (`(` that replaced `\footnote{`, `\vspace{…}`) is a point after the word before it.
  - Line boxes used when no word matches are clipped to the page's text (synctex gives a scaled picture its unscaled size).
  - **Changes**: a hunk inside the argument of `\input{…}`/`\include{…}` is a point at the start of the included file's first printed line, not the boxes of everything it includes.
- **Card contexts** (amendment "M2a engine").
  - Paragraphs break at a gap of more than 0.45 line heights (`cards.GAP`; ACL headings and footnotes sit about half a line below the text before them).
  - Parts across a column or page break are joined only when no blank line, `\par`, `\item` or start or end of a non-math environment lies between them in the source (a tcolorbox's text and the paragraph after it are not one sentence).
  - A selection in two places (two runs across a column break) shows only the paragraphs that hold its words: a figure between them is left out.
  - A reference list entry shows the whole entry (the paragraphs that hold its words). A `gone` point that ends a sentence or a heading also shows the next sentence, in the next paragraph if need be.
  - `record_context(…, changes)`: at ingest, the sentences of the changes attached to the comment (not moves) that lie in the anchor's paragraph are part of `text`, so a sentence inserted before the commented one shows, underlined.
  - Image `clip`: for a formula or a picture, around the rects and the range's own place in the PDF; within the page's text, and on a two-column page within the anchor's column; a window widened to `MIN_CLIP` is moved back inside rather than cut. For a deletion point in a table, the row it is in.
  - A line-end hyphen is kept, with no space, when the source has the two parts with it, also before an upper-case word (`GPT-5.6-Luna`). `changed` splits words as the app's WordDiff does (between spaces, punctuation at their edges one character each), so `arXiv:2309.11998.` is no artefact.
- **Build log.** An error without a place (`! LaTeX Error: File … not found.`) takes the place of TeX's follow-up (`main.tex:92: Emergency stop.`), and the follow-ups (`Emergency stop.`, `==> Fatal error occurred…`) are not errors. When the build failed, warnings about undefined references and reruns are left out.

App, M2a integration, 2026-10-15.
- **Drag across columns.** On a page with `round.build.columns`, a drag from one column to the other is selected as two runs (`Geometry.columnRuns`): from the point in the left column down to the end of its column's text, and from the start of the right column's text down to the point there; a column's text runs on while the space between its rows is less than a row's height, so a float, heading or footnote ends it. PDFKit alone selects in content order, which put a figure at the top of the right column into the selection. The comment's text lists the left column's lines first (PDFKit lists them in content order).
- **Id labels** sit outside all of the page's text, or, when that leaves no room on the page (an ACL ruler at the page's edge), between the body text (`text_x`) and the ruler.
- **Change rows** with `figure` read "New image file: <file>". A `gone` comment whose context is an image (a row deleted from a table) shows the round's crop with the deletion point in its Now block.
- The engine process runs in the temporary folder, with the engine's folder first on `PYTHONPATH` (since 2026-10-27; before, a Python chosen with `enginePython` used its own installed `papercollab`).

M2a fix round, 2026-10-15 (the app and engine reviews of M2a).
- **Colours.** The second palette colour is `teal`, fill/ink `#2DD4BF/#0F766E`, in place of `green` (`#4ADE80/#15803D`): green is kept for changes and "verified", and a green mark hid the change bands and changed-word underlines under it. `comment` and `edit_comment` refuse `green`. A ledger that stores `green` (M2a) reads as teal (`comments.LEGACY`, `comments.color(c)`): state's `color` is `teal` and final.pdf draws it in teal. Import maps a green annotation to the nearest entry, teal.
- **Click notes** (`comments.locate`). synctex is asked at the centre of the nearest word of the nearest line (not the line's first word when it has others), not at the point clamped to the line's edge: at the edge synctex named another line or file for about a third of the clicks beside a line of a real two-column paper. `comments._synctex` treats a line past the end of the named file as no hit (it raised IndexError, "Internal error"); with no hit at all the call fails with "No LaTeX source was found for this spot on page N."
- **Card contexts across a column or page break** (`cards._across`, `_follows`). A break place is taken only at a full line of body text: at least 75% as wide as the widest line of its column, and not a page number (a figure's tick labels, a legend or a table row are narrower). Each side's letters are taken from its own paragraph only, up to 2 × `JOIN` of them (at least `JOIN`), with what `\cite` and `\ref` print left out (`comments.printed`), so the head of a sentence ending in a citation is found and a page number or legend word next to the break is not.
- **to_pdf for changes.** A range inside the argument of a `\cite…` or `\ref…` command (an edited citation key) widens to the whole command, so it is drawn on the citation's printed words, not on every line of the sentence. A range inside `\footnote{…}` is aligned with the footnote's own text only (synctex gives it the line of its mark). So `record_context(…, changes)` no longer pulls neighbouring sentences into a card.
- **Whole `\input`/`\include` lines** added, moved or deleted (`anchor._only_includes`, `_included`): an inserted or moved command is a point at the start of the included file's first printed line; a deleted one, at the start of the file that now follows the deletion point with nothing between, else no rects. They were drawn wherever synctex put that `main.tex` line.
- **reanchor of a carried comment.** Placed on the same source range as before (a box redrawn around the same table), it keeps its state. Otherwise it is `moved` when its PDF text is the last earlier round's, else `changed`; and `changed` when a non-move change of the round now lies in its sentence (`api._reattach` returns the changes now attached). A box re-anchored on a table whose numbers changed, or on a replaced figure, stays `changed` (was: `moved`).
- **Carried boxes** (`anchor.box_place(…, old)`, `carry(…, old_pdf)`). When the box was drawn around the picture next to its caption (each side within `FIT`, 15 pt, of the drawing's extent in the previous round's PDF), it is drawn around the new drawing with the same margins, so a replaced or resized figure is boxed whole. A drawing is the paths and images above or below the caption, across its width, following each other with gaps of at most `DRAW_GAP` (30 pt). A carried box stays inside the page's text and drawings plus `BOX_PAD` (6 pt).
- **Image clips** (`cards._clip`, `_snap`). A clip is widened to the drawings its band crosses (a plot's axes) and to horizontal rules within `MIN_CLIP`'s height (a table's), within the page. Its edges are then moved so they cut no text: text lines level with the rects that hold none of them and lie beside them (the other column, text wrapped around a table) are left out; a word of a line or row level with the rects is taken in when half of it is inside; above and below, rows are taken in from the rects outwards until a row the sides cut by more than a tenth, or less than half inside, and the edge goes midway between that row's ink and the ink of the rows taken in (`cards.INK`: a line box holds about 18% of its height above capitals and 5% below descenders), because the boxes of a tightly set table's rows overlap.

App, M2a fix round, 2026-10-15.
- **Selection outline.** Selecting a comment (J/K, a mark, a row) takes the old and new marks off their pages and puts them back, because PDFKit does not redraw a custom-drawn annotation for `annotationsChanged(on:)`; the outline used to stay on the previous mark when the PDF did not scroll. `--debug-script dump` has `pdf.selectedMarks`.
- **Id labels** are filled with the comment's own ink and show the status as a glyph before the id (`+` new, `○` open, `↩` reopened, `✓` verified, `–` won't fix); the status colours stay on the panel chips. After the PDF is shown, the PDF view takes the keyboard focus (not the papers list), unless a text field has it. In re-anchor mode a click on a mark selects nothing.
- **Cards.** Inside an underline or note mark, changed words are tinted green (the mark's own underline is there). When a carried comment's state is `unchanged` or `moved` but its Now text differs from the block above (fixed in an earlier round without a verdict), the row says "changed since Rk". A Reopened block with the same text as When commented is merged into it ("WHEN COMMENTED · R1 · REOPENED R2"). A note placed on an empty area shows that area of the page ("NOTE ON AN EMPTY AREA"); the When block never shows another round's LaTeX quote. A Now block of a comment with an attached `figure` change says "NEW IMAGE FILE <file>". A row deleted from a table is drawn as a dashed green line across the crop, at the bottom of the row before it (between that row's descenders and the next row's capitals), with a caret standing on it after the deletion point. A carried click note (its line was a guess by design) is not flagged as low confidence. In Dark Mode, card underlines and strikeouts use the lighter fill colour.
- **Comment list** is an NSTableView (`CommentTable`): section headers that float, fixed 44 pt compact rows (one hosting view each), and the expanded card in one hosting controller laid over its row, given the next card's model on J/K. It follows `PanelModel` through Combine, so SwiftUI never re-renders the list. Panel scrolls run on a 120 Hz timer (`ScrollAnimator`).

Final PDF in the margin style, 2026-10-15 (the advisor: Preview showed final.pdf's highlights but not the comment text, and a sample final.pdf with the notes in the margin looked much better). This replaces §9's final.pdf bullets, the M2a item "final.pdf annotations" and the annotation opacity in the review-mode item "Render" (the highlight is now burned in at 0.35; the annotation is at 0); the email is unchanged.
- **Pages.** The summary page(s), then every page of the build PDF widened in place (its MediaBox, so its text, links, named destinations and outline stay): `render.MARGIN` (200 pt) on the right, and on the left too when at least a quarter of the pages with text are set in two columns (`comments.layout`). Every summary page has the size of the first paper page.
- **Burned into the page content**, so every reader and print show it:
  - the margin's background and a header `Round N review, final · <title> · <date>`;
  - each located comment's mark in its own colour and kind (a highlight's fill at 0.35, underline, strike-out, box, a dot for a note, × for gone), at 0.45 opacity when verified or won't fix;
  - an ID chip in the paper's margin at the anchor's last line (a box's top), in the status colours `render.STATUS` (new blue, still open amber, reopened red, verified green, won't fix grey);
  - the comment's margin item: an open comment is a box headed `+ R2-01 · new`, `○ R1-08 · still open` or `↺ R1-05 · reopened` (and `· text deleted` when gone), with the reopen or won't-fix note first and the original muted as `Round K: …` (a still open comment: the note, then an earlier reopen note muted); a closed one is one line, `✓ R1-03 verified · <note>` or `– R1-06 · won't fix · <note>`, cut with …. Below it, struck in grey, the old text of its replace and delete changes on the page, as round N−1's PDF printed it where the change's context has it (`render._printed`), else its LaTeX source (2 lines, then `(+N more words deleted)`);
  - the student's changes on the page in grey: a faint fill under new words, a bar beside their lines (one for an attached change, two thin ones for an unrequested one, dashed for a move), × at a deletion, a frame round a replaced image; an unrequested change also gets a grey margin line `your change · <what>` with a dotted leader.
- **Placing.** On a two-column paper an item goes to the margin on its anchor's side of the column gap. Items on a side keep the order of their leaders and never overlap; every note is whole and in one size (`render.SIZE`), and what a side cannot hold goes to a band under the paper, the page taller (amendment "Notes whole, the band under the paper"). A leader runs straight from the chip to its item; past margin line numbers (a review-mode ruler) it first runs along the chip's lower edge, between two numbers, to beyond the ruler. Nothing is drawn for a page without comments or changes except the empty margin and its header.
- **Type.** Helvetica (MuPDF's built-in), and for the characters it lacks Hiragino Sans GB (else STHeiti, else MuPDF's Droid Sans Fallback), embedded and subset. Lines break at spaces and between CJK characters, not before CJK closing punctuation.
- **Annotations.** One per located comment as before (kind, colour, `/T`, `/Contents` unchanged), with a closed Popup over its margin item, and all at `/CA 0`: they list the comments in UPDF, Acrobat and Preview's sidebar without drawing the mark a second time (PDFKit draws markup annotations from their type, ignoring the appearance stream, but honours /CA).
- **Summary.** Header, `Review of Round N`, title and the student line as before; a "How to reply" box with REPLY_RULE and `To do for Round N+1 (k)` followed by ID chips; a colour key; `Earlier comments (k)` with rows for Reopened, Still open and Won't fix (chip, page, the texts of the margin item, and the quote) and the Verified chips with "Closed. No action needed."; `New comments in this round (k)`; `Your changes no comment asked for (k): I have read these` (page and `“old” → “new”`, `added “…”`, `deleted “…”`, `moved “…”` or `new image file <file>`, 90 characters, in the words the PDF prints: `render._printed`, see the amendment "Changes tab"); `Build` (pages against the last round, undefined references, new warnings, replaced images, errors). A comment whose round-N context has `display: "image"` shows its `clip` as a picture (4 px per point, at most 64 pt high: a form XObject of the page would put the whole page's text into PDFKit's search and copy) instead of its PDF text; otherwise the quote (200 characters, 3 lines), `deleted: “…”` when gone, and `(location not found in Round N)` for an orphan, whose page reads `—`. The chips link to the anchors.
- **Navigation.** Page labels `Summary` (`Summary 1`, `Summary 2`, … for several pages), then the paper's own (shifted; arabic from 1 without any). Outline: Summary, `Comments (k)` with one `R1-05 · reopened · p.2` item per located comment, then the paper's own outline with its named destinations resolved to pages.
- `render.final_pdf(paper, lg, n, out)` returns the margin items it placed, `[{"page", "id", "side", "rect"}]` (tests). A closed round's final.pdf is re-rendered with `render.final_pdf(paper, ledger.load(paper), n, out)`; there is no RPC method for it.

Changes tab, 2026-10-15 (`mac/UX.md` §16: the advisor could not tell what the tab was for, and it showed LaTeX).
- **Change context** (`cards.change_details`, at ingest after carry). Each Change in analysis.json gains `context: {"before", "now"}`: the sentence(s) around it as round n−1's PDF and round n's print them. Each side is a card Context (amendment "M2a engine") from that round's source and PDF: `sel` holds the change's own words; `cut` is where an insert went (before) or a delete was (now). A side whose rects hold no word (a `(` that replaced `\footnote{See`, a formatting command) is also shown as a point, except a replaced figure. Figures, tables and math are `display: "image"` with a `clip`, as for cards. `changed` is [] and `against` null. Now uses the change's page and rects; before uses `anchor.to_pdf` of the old range in round n−1's PDF. A side is null when it prints nothing: no page on the new side (a deleted file or `\input` line), a hunk about `\input`/`\include` on the old side, or a missing PDF.
- **`section`** (also in analysis.json): the title of the innermost heading at or before the change in its file (the new file, else the old), without macro names, braces, `$` and `~`; "References" in a `.bbl`; null when the file has none before it.
- **Older rounds.** For an analysis.json written before this, `state` works both out once per engine process (about 2.4 s for 23 changes). It does so only when the diff made again gives the stored changes' kinds and files in order; otherwise `section` and `context` are null.
- **Checked.** The ledger's round record gains `checked`: the sorted ids of the changes the advisor checked in that round. A new RPC, `check_change` (paper, id, checked? default true) → {state}, with CLI `check P c03 [--clear]`. It works only in the open round, and only for one of its changes ("Round 2 has no change c99."). An open round that is re-ingested starts with none checked.
- **State.** `changes[]` gain `section`, `context` and `checked` (a bool).
- **final.pdf** (integration, 2026-10-15). A change's `your change` line and the struck old words under a comment's margin item use its context (`render._printed`): a text side's `sel` words, or the words of an image side (a table cell or row) when they hold the source's letters and digits; the LaTeX source when a side has none (formatting, `\input`, a formula's glyphs), when its words are more than twice as long as the source (+12 letters), or when a replace prints the same words on both sides (`effort` → `Effort` on a figure's own axis label). The `checked` list does not change final.pdf.

Fix round M2b, 2026-10-16 (reviews of the M2b trial runs: a closed comment's note was cut, an accepted strike-out still read as "delete this", final.pdf opened past the summary, a change's old words were printed twice, and a comment written on an edit no comment asked for left it "unrequested" everywhere). Where it conflicts with the amendments "Final PDF in the margin style" and "Changes tab", this wins.
- **Changes asked about by a new comment.** `comments.commenters(lg, n, changes)` → {change id: sorted ids of the comments created in round n whose round-n anchor rects overlap the change's rects on its page}, each rect grown by 1 pt (4 pt for a point: a note, a deletion). It is computed, not stored, so a deleted or re-anchored comment needs nothing. `state` gives each change `commented_by` (that list, or []); `unrequested` still means `attached_to == []`.
- **final.pdf, the margin.** A closed comment is its heading line (`✓ R1-03 verified`, `– R1-06 · won't fix`) with its verdict note below, whole. A closed strike-out is drawn as a faint underline, and its annotation is an Underline. The old words of a replace or delete change are printed once per page, under the comment it is attached to or asked about by whose mark covers most of it (the lowest id on a tie); a change asked about by a new comment gets no `your change` line of its own. When another chip already sits at a comment's last line, its chip goes to the nearest line above of its own mark that has none (side by side, the second chip covered a review ruler's number).
- **final.pdf, the summary.** The student line ends "(the summary comes before its page 1)". "How to reply" adds "Next round: send the full LaTeX source (zip) again."; its To-do chips, the Verified chips and the key carry the status signs (↺ reopened, ○ still open, + new, ✓), so they read in greyscale, and the key lists only the statuses of this round. Earlier comments: a verified comment with a verdict note gets a row like Won't fix (the note, then `Round K: <original>` muted); the others stay chips. `Your changes no comment asked for (k)` is followed by one muted sentence: "I have read all of these." when each is checked (the round's `checked`) or asked about, "I have read N of them: those marked read[ or asked about]." when some are, nothing when none is; then "R2-01 and R2-03 ask about 2 of them." Each row ends with the chips of the comments that ask about it and "asks about this", or "✓ read" when checked. Rows are in the paper's order (page, the left column first, top). A move to another page reads `moved here (was on p.X) “…”`.
- **final.pdf, the file.** The catalog's `/OpenAction` is `[<summary page> /Fit]` (the build's own named the paper's page 1). Title "Round N review: <title>", Creator and Producer "Paperole" (PaperCollab until 2026-10-27). The paper's link annotations get `/Border [0 0 0]` (hyperref's green and red boxes read as marks); they stay clickable.
- **email.txt.** After the `Closed:` line, one line per closed comment with a verdict note: `  R2-02 (verified): <note>`.
