- **Anchors in captions.** `anchor.to_pdf` of a range inside a `\caption` or `\subcaption` argument leaves out the synctex line boxes that hold a picture (a drawn part at least 20 pt wide or high, `PICTURE`) when others remain: synctex gives a subcaption's line its subfigure's box too, and the plot's own axis label was taken for the caption's words.

final.pdf without annotations, 2026-10-16 (the advisor saw each note twice: in its margin box, and again as "[R2-03] …" in a reader's popup beside it). Where it conflicts with §2, §9, the M2a item "final.pdf annotations" and the amendments "Final PDF in the margin style" and "Fix round M2b", this wins.
- **No annotations.** final.pdf has no PDF annotations of its own: no markup, Text, Square or Popup per comment. Only the paper's link annotations stay (with `/Border [0 0 0]`). Every note, status and mark is in the page content (margin items, chips, burned-in marks), and the outline's `Comments (k)` with one `R1-05 · reopened · p.2` item per located comment lists and links them; the summary's chips link to the anchors. A closed strike-out is still drawn as a faint underline.
- **Why.** The transparent annotations (`/CA 0`) were for readers' comment lists, but a reader could still open their popups, a second copy of each note. Without annotations no reader can show one; this was not checked in UPDF or Acrobat, which were not run.
- **Import** is unchanged (it reads the annotations of an externally annotated PDF).

App, 2026-10-16.
- **Unrequested changes** are slate teal, not purple (§10's `#7C3AED`): `#355F6C` (Dark Mode `#9CC3CF`) for text and bars, and `#355F6C` at 70% for the PDF band (`mac/UX.md` §16).
- **Launch.** `--background` runs a test instance out of the user's way (accessory, never activated, its window behind all others; `mac/README.md`). Launch arguments are never taken as files to open (`NSTreatUnknownArgumentsAsOpen` off).

Earlier rounds and next steps, 2026-10-16 (BACKLOG F7: the app showed only the latest round; F6: the papers list showed no next step; `mac/UX.md` §17).
- **`state`** takes `round?` (an int; CLI `state P --round N`) and then returns round N's state, built as the latest round's: `round` is round N (with its `final_pdf` and `email`), `comments` the comments with a round-N record, with that round's verdicts, notes, contexts and `history` (the rounds before N), and `changes` round N's analysis.json with its `checked` and `commented_by`. A closed round is frozen, so this is the state as the round was left; only `rounds` and `summary` are the paper's now. A comment's `color` and `kind` are its current ones: the ledger keeps no style per round (a carried comment can be recoloured in a later round, or re-anchored from a box onto text). An unknown round is refused: "There is no round 9." Every other method still returns the latest round's state. About 2 ms in the engine for any round of a 25-page paper.
- **`summary`** in every state, about the paper's latest round whichever round is shown: `{"round": 3, "closed": false, "status": "ok", "to_judge": 2, "to_check": 3}` (and `answered`, `handled` since amendment "UI v4 data"). `to_judge` counts the comments carried into the open round without a verdict; `to_check` its unrequested changes neither checked nor asked about by a comment of the round (`commented_by`), the count on the Changes tab. Both are 0 when the round is closed or its build failed; `round` and `status` are null before the first ingest.
- **`summaries`** (new RPC; papers: [path]) → `{"papers": [{"path", "summary"} | {"path", "error"}]}`, in the order given, with an error (an EngineError's text, or a file error's) for a folder that cannot be read. It is the one method whose result has no `state`. It reads only ledger.json and the latest round's analysis.json (about 1.5 ms a paper), takes no lock, and does not put back an interrupted ingest. CLI `summaries P [P…]`.
- **App.** The toolbar's round label is a menu of the rounds (`Round 1 · Oct 14 · closed`); an earlier round is shown read only under a banner, from `state` with `round`. The papers list shows each paper's `summary` under its title: from its states for the selected paper, from one `summaries` call for the others at launch and whenever the app becomes active. Requests are written to the engine in the order they are sent (`EngineClient.send` on the caller's thread): written from Tasks after a hop off the main actor, two requests sent one after the other could reach the engine the other way round, and the earlier one's reply was then dropped as older.

Prose view and reading, 2026-10-16 (BACKLOG F1, F2; `mac/UX.md` §18). Ledger schema stays 1. Where §2 lists prose.json and read state as later work, this replaces it.
- **`prose.py`** (new). `build(paper, lg, n, diff=None) -> dict` writes `rN/prose.json`: a temporary file, then `os.replace`.
  - **When**: ingest calls it after carry unless the build failed, passing the round's TreeDiff, and builds round n−1's first if it is missing. The `prose` RPC builds any round.
  - **A cache**, like analysis.json: a closed round's file may be written again, and nothing the round decided changes.
  - **`PARSER`** (an int) is stored in the file. A file with another value is stale.
  - Block splitting and the text command table follow Paper Pal's `parseBlocks` and `TEXT_COMMANDS` (github.com/Claire1217/paper-pal, MIT; credited in the module header).
- **Reading order.**
  - **Body**: from `paper.main`'s `\begin{document}` to `\end{document}`, following `\input`, `\include`, `\subfile` and `\import` where they stand. Paths are relative to src, with `.tex` added. A missing file becomes a `missing` block.
  - **References**: read at `\bibliography{…}` or `\printbibliography`, from the `.bbl` that TeX read (amendment "Review-mode fixes"), one `bibitem` block per `\bibitem`. A biblatex `.bbl` gives none.
  - **Title**: `\title` gives a `title` block first.
  - **Definitions** (`\newcommand`, `\renewcommand`, `\providecommand`, `\def`, `\DeclareMathOperator`) are collected from the preamble and from every file read.
- **Blocks.**
  - **Splitting**: at blank lines, and at a line that starts a heading, an `\item`, the `\begin` or `\end` of a list or wrapper, or a one-line command.
  - **One block whatever it holds**: display math (`equation`, `align`, `gather`, `multline`, `flalign`, `alignat`, `eqnarray`, `displaymath`, all with or without `*`, and `\[…\]`); floats (`figure`, `table`, `wrapfigure`, `wraptable`, `sidewaysfigure`, `sidewaystable`, with or without `*`); `algorithm`, `algorithmic`, `algorithm2e`; `verbatim`, `Verbatim`, `lstlisting`, `minted`; `tikzpicture`, `pgfpicture`; a `tabular` outside a float; `thebibliography`.
  - **Skipped**: `%` comments, `comment` environments and `\iffalse…\fi`.
  - **Wrappers**: other environments (`abstract`, `center`, `quote`, `minipage`, `tcolorbox`, `multicols`, …) are transparent, and their paragraphs are blocks. Paragraphs inside `abstract` have role `abstract`. An environment whose content converts to no text is one `picture` block.
- **Display text.**
  - **Hidden**: `\label`, spacing and layout commands and comments map to no characters.
  - **Styles**: `em`, `bf`, `tt`, `sc`, `u`, `sup`, `sub`.
  - **Footnotes** (`fn`) keep their text in place.
  - **`\url` and `\href`** become `url`.
  - **Citations** take the printed form from the build's `*.aux` `\bibcite` entries: natbib author-year (`(Agarwal et al., 2025)`, and `Agarwal et al. (2025)` for `\citet`) or numeric (`[3, 5]`); `[?]` when unresolved.
  - **References** take the `\newlabel` number. `\Cref`, `\cref` and `\autoref` add the type name from cleveref's `@cref` entry or the label prefix (Figure, Table, Section, Equation, Appendix, Algorithm). `\eqref` gives `(3)`.
  - **Characters**: accents become combining marks; `~` becomes U+00A0; ` `` '' ` become “ ”; `--` and `---` become – and —; the control and text symbols follow Paper Pal's table.
  - **User macros** are expanded with their `#1`–`#9` arguments, up to depth 6 and 1500 expansions a block.
  - **Unknown commands** keep the text of their braced arguments. One that prints no text becomes a `tex` mark holding its source (the app's grey chip).
  - **Inline math** (`$…$`, `\(…\)`, `math`): `text` holds the TeX between the delimiters, without `\label`, under a `math` mark.
- **`prose.json`**: `{"schema": 1, "parser": PARSER, "round": n, "against": n−1 | null, "macros": {"\\R": "\\mathbb{R}", "\\tens": "\\boldsymbol{#1}"}, "blocks": [Block…], "changes": {"c04": [12, 13]}}`. `macros` are the paper's definitions in KaTeX's form; `changes` maps each change id to the indices of the blocks it touches. A Block:
  ```json
  {"kind": "paragraph", "file": "sections/method.tex", "start": 1234, "end": 1890, "lines": [27, 34],
   "hash": "9f2c4e0a5b1d7c33", "section": 5, "level": null, "number": null, "role": null,
   "text": "We boot a microVM in t_0 ms (Wang et al., 2026).",
   "marks": [[21, 24, "math"], [28, 47, "cite", "wang2026"]],
   "map": [[0, 21, 1234, 21], [21, 3, 1255, 5], …],
   "box": [4, [72.0, 301.2, 540.0, 388.9]], "clip": null,
   "ins": [[3, 7, "c04"]], "del": [[20, "quickly", [], "c04"]], "added": null, "moved": null, "before": null}
  ```
  - **Indices and offsets**: a block's index is its position in `blocks`, and the RPCs and state use it. All display offsets index `text` as code points (§3), with spans `[start, end)`.
  - **`kind`**: `title`, `heading`, `paragraph`, `item` (with `depth` and `label`: `•` or `3.`), `math`, `figure`, `table`, `code`, `picture`, `bibitem`, `deleted` or `missing`.
  - **`level`** (headings): 1 for section, 2 for subsection, 3 for subsubsection, 4 for paragraph.
  - **`number`**: for a heading, counted from the headings, starred ones null, letters after `\appendix` (`3.2`, `A.1`). For `math`, its labels' `.aux` numbers, joined by `, `.
  - **`section`**: the index of the innermost heading of level 1 or 2 above the block, or −1 for front matter. The reference list gets a heading block of its own, "References", with `hash` null.
  - **`math` blocks**: `text` is the environment as KaTeX takes it (starred, without `\label`, `\nonumber` and `\notag`); `clip` is the fallback when KaTeX cannot render it.
  - **`figure` and `table` blocks**: `text` is the converted `\caption`, prefixed `Figure N: ` or `Table N: ` from its label's number when it has one.
  - **`map`**: entries `[d, n, s, m]` pair the display text `[d, d+n)` with the source `[s, s+m)` of `file`. When `n == m` the characters pair one by one; otherwise the entry is atomic (math, a citation, a reference, a macro, a symbol). Only the engine uses `map`.
  - **`box`**: `[page, rect]`, the union of the synctex boxes of the block's lines on the page that holds most of them, or null.
  - **`clip`** (`figure`, `table`, `code`, `picture`, `math`): `[page, rect]` from those boxes, widened and snapped as card image clips are (`cards._clip`, `_snap`). The app crops it from that round's build PDF.
  - **`anchor.line_boxes(pdf)`** (new) gives `{(file, line): [(page, rect)]}` in one pass over `<stem>.synctex.gz` (0.07 s on a 25-page paper), with rects as `synctex view` gives them (§5 anchor). It replaces a `synctex` call per block.
- **Hash.** The first 16 hex digits of the sha1 of the block's kind and its texdiff tokens (comments blanked, whitespace dropped), joined by spaces.
  - **Figures**: for a block that includes graphics, each graphic's digest is added: for a PDF, its pages' content streams and images; otherwise its bytes (the comparison texdiff's replaced pictures use). A replaced figure therefore turns unread.
  - **What keeps a hash**: re-wrapping, `%` comments and moving the block.
  - **What changes it**: any token edit.
  - **Shared hashes**: blocks with the same tokens share a hash, and are read together.
  - **Not counted**: `missing` blocks and the References heading have `hash` null.
  - **Deleted paragraphs**: a `deleted` block's hash is `d` followed by its old hash.
- **Diff marks** (rounds ≥ 2, against round n−1, from the round's TreeDiff).
  - **Change ids**: hunk i is change `c{i+1:02d}`, as in `carry`. In a lazy build the diff is made again, and the ids are kept only when its hunks give analysis.json's kinds and files in order (as in the amendment "Changes tab"); otherwise they are null.
  - **Inserted text**: an insert's or replace's new side, mapped into display spans, gives `ins: [[s, e, change]]`.
  - **Deleted text**: a delete's or replace's old side, taken from round n−1's prose, is placed at the mapped point as `del: [[p, text, marks, change]]`.
  - **Within a replace**, the old and new display texts are diffed again by words (split as `cards.changed` splits them), so equal words inside the hunk are not marked.
  - **Whole blocks**: a whole old block deleted becomes a `deleted` block at the deletion point, with its old `file`, `start`, `end`, text and marks, and `"round": n−1`. A block wholly inserted has `added: change` instead of `ins`. A block inside a move has `moved: change`.
  - **`before`**: a `figure`, `table`, `math`, `code` or `picture` block that a hunk or a `figure` change touches gets `{"page", "clip"}` of the old round's block on the hunk's old side.
- **Ledger.** The top level gains `read`: `{"<hash>": <round in which it was marked read>}`, one map for the paper.
  - **Read**: a block is read when its hash is in the map.
  - **Writers**: the `read` RPC writes the map. `finish` of round 1 adds every round-1 hash.
  - **Older ledgers**: a ledger without `read` counts every closed round's hashes as read, and its first write stores them. Review folders made before this were reviewed round by round.
  - **Earlier rounds** are shown with the map as it is now.
- **RPC and CLI.**
  - **`prose`** (paper, round?) → {path, state}. It builds the round's prose.json when it is missing or stale, and round n−1's when needed, with progress "Preparing the prose view…". It refuses a round without a build PDF ("Round 2 has no build PDF."). CLI `prose P [--round N]`.
  - **`read`** (paper, blocks: [int], read?: default true) → {state}. It works on blocks of the open round only, adding their hashes to the map or removing them. Errors: "Round 2 is closed.", "Round 3 has no paragraph 999.". CLI `read P 12 13 [--clear]`.
  - **`comment` and `reanchor`** take `prose: [block, start, end]` in place of `page`, `rects` and `text`. CLI `--prose 12,40,58`.
    - **Mapping**: the span is mapped to a source range through `map`. An atomic entry it touches is taken whole, and the range is cut at the end of the block's file. `start == end` places a note on the word at that point.
    - **Anchor**: `quote`, `prefix`, `suffix`, `section` and `env` come as for a selection; `page`, `rects` and `text` from `anchor.to_pdf`; method `selection`, confidence 1.0, plus `"via": "prose"`. When `to_pdf` finds no page, the comment is still made, with page null (it then needs attention, §10).
    - **Boxes**: `kind: "box"` on a `figure`, `table`, `math`, `code` or `picture` block anchors to the whole environment, as `locate_box` does, with `rects = [the block's clip]`.
    - **Refusal**: a span whose range holds only hidden source gives "Nothing to comment on there.".
  - **`finish`** of round 1 builds round 1's prose.json if it is missing, then adds its hashes to `read`.
- **State** (for the round shown, latest or `round`).
  - **`round.prose`**: the absolute path of `rN/prose.json` when it exists and is current, otherwise null.
  - **`reading`**: `{"read": [indices of the round's blocks that are read], "count": blocks with a hash, "unread": k}`, or null without prose.
  - **`comments[].prose`**: the display spans of this round's anchor, `[[block, start, end], …]`. A gone comment gives `[[block, p, p]]`; `[]` when the anchor maps to nothing.
  - **`changes[].blocks`**: the indices of the blocks it touches (`deleted` blocks included). **`changes[].read`**: true when it is not a move, `blocks` is not empty and each of those blocks is read.
  - **Counting**: `summary.to_check` and the Changes tab count leave out read changes.
  - **Where the data lives**: ingest also writes each change's block hashes into analysis.json (`hashes`), so `summaries` stays a read of ledger.json and analysis.json only. prose.json is cached for each engine process by path and modification time.
- **final.pdf.** In "Your changes no comment asked for", a `read` change counts as read and is marked "✓ read", as a checked one is.
- **Budgets** (two papers of 44 and 20 files):
  - building one round's prose: at most 1.5 s;
  - added to ingest: at most 2 s, with round n−1 built too;
  - `state` with prose: at most 0.15 s;
  - `read`: at most 0.1 s;
  - prose.json: at most 1 MB.
- **Tests.** Synthetic fixtures only (the fixture paper v1–v3, plus a `prose` fixture) cover:
  - the splitting table: every kind, `\input` order, preamble skipped, `\iffalse`, `comment`;
  - `map` round trips: every display character maps into its block, display → source → display is the identity on one-to-one entries, and atomic entries widen;
  - citations and references from a synthetic `.aux`: natbib author-year, numeric, cleveref;
  - macros with arguments, and the expansion limits;
  - the hash: re-wrapping, comments and moves keep it; a word edit and a replaced figure change it;
  - diff marks: an insert, a delete inside a block, a deleted block, a refined replace, a move;
  - `read` and round-1 `finish`, and an older ledger without `read`;
  - a prose comment's exact `start`/`end` and its rects on the same PDF words;
  - `changes[].read` and `to_check`;
  - state timing.

  On all six rounds of both: no exception; `tex` chips under 2% of display characters; and for every paragraph, the letters of its `text` outside math match the PDF words inside its `box` for at least 80% of them, with the paragraphs below that listed.

Engine, prose view and reading, 2026-10-16 (where the amendment above was silent, or the implementation differs).
- **`map`**: an atomic entry whose display and source happen to have the same length carries a fifth element, `[d, n, s, m, 1]`, so it is not read as one to one.
- **`thebibliography`** (in a `.tex` file, as in the `.bbl`) gives the References heading and one `bibitem` block per `\bibitem`; no kind holds a whole list.
- **`deleted` blocks** also have `was` (the old block's kind), and `before` (the old crop) when that kind is an image kind.
- **Boxes.** `anchor.line_boxes` gives a line the line box (hbox) holding each of its records; an hbox holding no records (a picture) counts for its own line. An hbox's own line is not used otherwise: a review ruler boxes every text line again at shipout, with the line TeX is reading then. A block's box is the run of its boxes down a page (gaps of at most `prose.CLUSTER`, 24 pt) holding most of its lines, with the runs on that page in another column; TeX gives the running head and foot of a page shipped out the line it is reading. A paragraph across a page break has its box on one page. A run-in `\paragraph` heading takes the next line's boxes, and the title `\maketitle`'s line and the next.
- **`comment`/`reanchor` with `prose`**: an `end` past the block's text runs on into the next blocks of the same file, one character between blocks. Further refusals: "prose is [block, start, end].", and "A box goes on a figure, table, formula or listing." for `kind: "box"` on a text block. `deleted` and `missing` blocks, and `start > end`, give "Nothing to comment on there."
- **State**: `reading` is a top-level key (null without a current prose.json); every `round` has `prose`. Changes keep analysis.json's `hashes`.
- **Ledger**: `new` writes `"read": {}`. `finish` stores an older ledger's read map before it closes any round (otherwise closing round N would count its unread paragraphs read). `read` of a block without a hash changes nothing.
- **Ingest**: a failure while building prose.json is reported as progress ("The prose view could not be prepared: …", traceback on stderr) and does not fail the ingest; `prose` tries again.
- **Text**: `\url` and `\href` read their URL as written (a `%` in it is no comment), and bibitem ranges are trimmed on the text as written. Inside a paragraph a `%` still ends the line for the engine, as for texdiff. Commands with `@` (internals) and `\penalty`, `\vskip`, `\kern`, … with their numbers and dimensions print nothing.
- **Older folders**: a closed round's hashes come from its prose.json, else from its source once per process (about 0.3 s a round). The first `prose` call on a folder from before this builds the missing rounds in turn (2.2 s for three rounds of a 25-page paper, once).
- Paper Pal's source was not at hand: the block rules and command table were written from the description above; the module header credits Paper Pal's design.

Prose integration, 2026-10-16 (`PARSER` 2).
- **`\subref`** prints its `sub@` label as the `.aux` has it (`Figure 6(a)` from `Figure~\ref{…}(\subref{…})`), not wrapped in parentheses again.
- **Moves**: a move whose range holds every letter of a block (all but its `\textbf{` lead-in or final period, as in two swapped run-in paragraphs) marks that block `moved`, not `ins` on it and `del` of its words at its old place.
- **Deletions inside a figure, table, formula or listing** (a deleted table row: the hunk is a point in the new block) touch that block: it is in the change's `blocks` and gets `before`.
- **A removed `\input`** (a delete or replace whose old text holds `\input{X}`, X no longer read): X's old blocks are `deleted` blocks at the deletion point, and the change's `blocks`.
- **Crops** of `figure` and `table` blocks leave out the lines of their `\caption` (the caption follows as text), unless nothing else has a box. A float without `*` on a two-column page is cut to the column most of its boxes start in, and does not continue in the other column; boxes synctex gives an included picture at its natural size (larger than its scaled box on the same line, same left and bottom) are left out.
- **Listings** (`verbatim`, `lstlisting`, `minted`) have `role: "listing"`; the app shows their text in monospace, not their crop (the crop is still in `clip`, for boxes and `before`).

Prose fix round, 2026-10-16 (the phase-1 UX and engine reviews; `PARSER` 3).
- **Formula boxes**: a `math` block's `box` and `clip` come from its own boxes. Boxes that another block's lines also hold (synctex gives the lead-in paragraph's lines, broken when the display starts, the display's first line too) and boxes outside the page's `text_x` (a ruler's numbers) are left out. Of the rest, those on the first page after the lead-in's last line are kept (a page shipped out while the display is read gives that page's boxes to the display's lines).
- **Splitting**: a line holding only `\label`, `\vspace`, `\hspace`, `\noindent`, `\indent`, `\hfill`, `\smallskip`, `\medskip`, `\bigskip`, `\index`, `\nocite`, `\nobreak`, `\allowbreak`, `\linebreak` or `\newline` (with its arguments) inside a paragraph continues the paragraph, as in TeX. An `\end{X}` on a text line that closes an environment or list opened before that line ends the paragraph there and closes X.
- **Hash**: the tokens of those commands (all but `\linebreak` and `\newline`), with their `[…]` and `{…}`, are left out, so moving `\noindent` or a `\label` onto or off a text line keeps the hash. The hashes of blocks that hold them change once with `PARSER` 3. Read maps stored before this show those blocks unread once.
- **`%` in listings** (texdiff, so hashes, hunks and anchoring too): the bodies of `verbatim`, `Verbatim`, `lstlisting` and `minted` (with or without `*`) and `\verb` arguments are not blanked as comments. Line breaks inside them are still ignored.
- **Split and joined paragraphs**: a move whose text and old place are separated in the new file by white space and comments only (a paragraph break put in or taken out) marks nothing; its change touches the blocks it overlaps and the blocks at the break. A move's deleted words are not placed in a block that the same move marks `moved`. A hunk that touches no block otherwise (a paragraph break, or a hidden command between blocks) touches the blocks overlapping or touching its new range (none in the preamble). An added `\input{X}` touches X's new blocks, as a removed one does.
- **`since`** (every block): the round its text is from. It is n for a block that shows this round's edits, else the `since` of the previous round's block with the same hash, else n.
- **Read rule**: a block is *fresh* when it has `ins`, `del`, `added` or `before`, or is `deleted`. A fresh block of round n is read only when `read[hash] >= n` (marked read in its round or later); any other block is read when its hash is in the map. So a new heading worded like one read before, or a paragraph back to an older text, stays unread until it is read.
  - **`read`** now writes `read[hash] = n`, the latest round it was marked read in (it kept the first).
  - **Who follows the rule**: `reading.read`, `changes[].read`, `summary.to_check` and final.pdf's "✓ read".
  - **analysis.json**: each change gains `fresh`, one bool per entry of `hashes`.
- **Comments made in prose** (`comment`/`reanchor` with `prose`): each block's part of the selection is placed on its own, and the parts on the first part's page are joined.
  - **A `math` block's part** is its `box`.
  - **A text block's part**: the block's display letters (outside formulas and chips) are aligned with the PDF words in its lines' synctex boxes, and the rects cover the words from the first matched one to the last. So the words that `\citet`, `\Cref` or a macro prints are inside the rects.
  - **Fallback**: when fewer than half the part's letters match, or the part holds a footnote, `anchor.to_pdf` of its source range, as before.

Reading in the PDF view, 2026-10-16 (the advisor reads only the PDF; the prose view is removed and reading confirmation moves into the PDF view, `mac/UX.md` §18; `PARSER` 4). Where it conflicts with the amendments "Prose view and reading", "Engine, prose view and reading", "Prose integration" and "Prose fix round", this wins. prose.json stays the engine's cache of a round's blocks, hashes and edits; read state and the ledger's `read` map are unchanged.
- **Removed** (the prose display only):
  - prose.json's `macros` (KaTeX's), and `prose.macros`.
  - Comments made in prose: `comment` and `reanchor` no longer take `prose: [block, start, end]` (`prose.locate` and its helpers are gone; CLI `--prose` is gone, and `comment`/`reanchor` need `--page` and `--rects`). Anchors stored earlier with `"via": "prose"` are ordinary anchors and stay.
  - In state: `round.prose` (the app no longer reads prose.json) and `comments[].prose` (replaced, below).
- **Kept, though only the prose view showed them**: a block's display `text`, `marks`, `map`, `ins`, `del`, `added`, `moved`, `before` and `clip`. They are how the engine finds which blocks this round edited (`fresh`, hence the read rule and the bars) and which block a comment is in; taking them out is not a simple removal.
- **`parts`** (every block of prose.json; `[]` for the title and for blocks without synctex boxes): where the block is read in the PDF, `[[page, [x0, y0, x1, y1]], …]` in reading order, one rect per column it is set in.
  - **Text blocks** (paragraphs, items, reference entries, headings): the synctex boxes of the block's lines (`anchor.line_boxes`), without the boxes of running heads and feet and without boxes taller than `prose.TALL` (40 pt: in two columns, TeX's output routine gives the line it reads at a shipout the boxes of the columns). They are grouped by page and column (`build.columns`; a box starting right of the gap less 3 pt is in the right column) and cut into runs down the column (gaps of at most `CLUSTER`, 24 pt). The parts are the run holding most of the block's lines (then most boxes), and of the runs in the column before and after it in reading order (the page's other column, or the page before or after) the one that continues it: its lines start no later (end no earlier) than the main run's; the same page first, then the most lines, then nearest the break. So a paragraph across a column or page break has a part in each, and a footnote or a stray box elsewhere has none.
  - **Running heads and feet** (`prose._running`): a box at most a line high (`LINE`, 14 pt), in the top or bottom eighth of its page, at least `HEAD_GAP` (12 pt) from every other line box of the page, and at the same rounded place on another page. synctex gives them the line TeX is reading when it ships a page out, which can be a line of any block.
  - **Headings** use only boxes no other block's lines hold (a paragraph ended by the heading's `\par` gives the heading's line its last lines).
  - **Formulas, floats, listings and pictures**: `[box]`.
- **Readable blocks** (`prose.readable`): a block with a hash, of kind `paragraph`, `item`, `bibitem`, `math`, `figure`, `table`, `code` or `picture`, and with parts. Headings, the title, `deleted` and `missing` blocks are not: the advisor reads in the PDF, where a deleted paragraph is not, and headings and the title and author block are not paragraphs to read (the advisor's decision, BACKLOG D4); the abstract is.
  - **Counting**: `reading.count`, `reading.unread` and `reading.read` are over readable blocks only.
  - **Changes**: `change_hashes` (from prose.json, and analysis.json's `hashes` and `fresh`, written by `build`) list readable blocks only. A change is read (`changes[].read`, `summary.to_check`, final.pdf's "✓ read") when it is not a move, touches at least one readable block, and each readable block it touches is read; a change that touches none (a heading, a deleted paragraph) needs V. analysis.json files written before keep heading hashes until the round's prose.json is built again (the papers list may count such a change as unread until then).
  - **`read`** (RPC) is unchanged: it adds the hashes of the blocks given (a non-readable block's hash changes no count). Finishing round 1 still adds every round-1 hash.
- **State `reading`** (null until the round's prose.json is current; the app then calls `prose`):
  ```json
  {"read": [3, 4], "count": 183, "unread": 9,
   "paragraphs": [{"block": 3, "section": 2, "parts": [[0, [108.0, 486.99, 504.0, 594.53]]]}, …],
   "headings": [{"block": 2, "level": 1, "title": "1 Introduction", "parts": [[0, [108.0, 460.56, 504.0, 468.72]]]}, …]}
  ```
  - **`paragraphs`**: the readable blocks in reading order; `section` is the block index of the innermost section or subsection heading above it, −1 for the title and abstract.
  - **`headings`**: the headings of level 1 and 2 (sections and subsections, starred ones and References included), `title` their number and text as printed (`3.2 Method`); References has `parts: []` (no source line of its own).
  - **`comments[].paragraph`**: the index of the block this round's anchor is in (the first, when it spans several), or null (an orphan, or an anchor in no block).
- **RPC and CLI**: `prose` (paper, round?) still builds a round's prose.json when it is missing or stale; its progress reads "Finding the paragraphs…" (ingest's too; a failure there is reported as "The paragraphs for reading could not be found: …"). CLI help texts say paragraphs, not prose view.
- **Moved review folders**: synctex keeps a `.bbl`'s absolute path, so `anchor.line_boxes` and `anchor._inputs` take a `.bbl` of that name beside the build PDF as the one TeX read; reference entries then have boxes (and parts) after the review folder was moved or copied.
- **Budgets**: unchanged (building one round: about 1 s on a 25-page paper; `state` stays under 0.15 s on the fixture paper with the larger `reading`).
- **Tests**: `tests/test_reading.py` (running heads and feet and parts on synthetic synctex boxes; a typeset two-column paper with a running head whose paragraphs cross column and page breaks: every paragraph's words, and no other's, are in its parts, and no part covers the head or the page number; readable kinds and the reading data in state; changes read over readable blocks only; a comment's paragraph; reference entries of a moved folder). `tests/test_prose.py` drops the KaTeX macros and the comments made in prose, and counts readable blocks.

App, reading in the PDF view, 2026-10-16 (`mac/UX.md` §18).
- **Removed**: the [PDF | Prose] control and P, the `WKWebView` prose pane and its page (`Sources/PaperCollab/Prose/` then, no package resources any more), `tools/vendor_katex.sh`, `--debug-prose-perf`, and the debug items `mode`, `prose-select`, `prose-click`, `prose-dump` and `shot-prose`.
- **Bars** beside the paragraphs not read (round 2 on), N, C and Shift-C in the PDF view with a toast for every press, the Reading menu (keys named in the titles, not key equivalents), Mark Paragraph Read / Unread in the PDF's menu, and the outline from `reading` alone.
- **Id labels** placed between the text and a margin ruler keep 7 pt from the text (was 4), 2 pt clear of a bar.

Reading fixes, 2026-10-16 (the verification of reading in the PDF view; `PARSER` 5). Where it conflicts with "Reading in the PDF view", this wins.
- **Items' and reference entries' parts**: boxes that an earlier block's lines also hold are left out (unless nothing is left). TeX ends the item before, or the paragraph leading into the list, while it reads this one's `\item` line, so synctex gives that line the earlier block's last lines; before this, an item's bar began beside the item above it.
- **Text blocks' parts** leave out boxes outside the page's `text_x` (`prose._in_text`, as formula boxes already did): ACL's review ruler numbers the lines of the item before at the next `\item` line.
- **`reading.paragraphs` order**: as printed, by their first part: page, then on a two-column page a part across both columns in the top half, the left column, the right column, one across both in the bottom half (the comment order, `state._printed`); then top, left, block. A float set on a later page, or below paragraphs that follow it in the source, is listed where it is printed. `section` and the outline's ranges still use block indices.
- **`comments[].paragraph`**: the first block of the anchor's file whose source range the anchor overlaps (a point: that holds it), not `deleted` or `missing` (`prose.block_of`). A float's block holds its whole environment, so a comment on a table cell or on text in a figure is in that float (it was null: a float's display text is its caption only). `prose.spans` is gone.
- **App**: N goes through `reading.paragraphs` in their order (from the first paragraph in view in that order; from a heading, the paragraph after it in the source). On a one-column page a part starting right of the middle of `text_x` (a float wrapped on the right) has its bar in the right margin. In an earlier or closed round N's message leaves out the C hint ("10 changed paragraphs · Round 2 is read only").
- **Tests**: `tests/test_reading.py` adds items and the ruler on synthetic boxes, a typeset list after a lead-in paragraph with line numbers (each item's parts hold its words and no other block's, and no number), the printed order with a float set at the foot of the page, and a comment inside a float. `test_prose.py` checks the paragraphs as a set.

Rounds close read, and bar fixes, 2026-10-16 (the advisor's decision BACKLOG D5; `mac/UX.md` §18; `PARSER` 6). Where it conflicts with the amendments "Prose view and reading", "Engine, prose view and reading", "Prose fix round", "Reading in the PDF view" and "Reading fixes", this wins.
- **Closing any round marks it read** (was: round 1 only). `finish` of round n, after final.pdf and email.txt are written (they list only the changes read in round n), adds every block of round n that has a hash, deleted blocks aside, to `read` when it is not there (`prose.close_read`), with the value n − 1, and 1 for round 1 (`prose.close_value`). So in round n + 1 every paragraph the student left alone is read (a block that is not fresh needs only its hash in the map), and the bars are exactly the paragraphs changed since round n, plus any the advisor marks unread. A paragraph edited in round n and not confirmed there keeps its bar when round n is viewed later (a fresh block of round n needs `read[hash] >= n`), and its change stays unread in that round's state: closing does not rewrite what the closed round shows. Without a current prose.json the round's blocks are built first ("Finding the paragraphs…"); a round without a build PDF marks nothing.
- **`read_through`** (ledger top level, new): the latest round whose closing marked its paragraphs read. `finish` sets it to n. A ledger without it counts as 1 (round 1 always marked them).
- **Ledgers closed before this rule.** When the latest round n is open and round n − 1 is closed with `read_through` < n − 1, `prose.read_map` (used by state, the papers list's `summary`, final.pdf and `finish`) adds round n − 1's hashes that are not in the map with the value n − 2 (`close_value(n − 1)`), as if round n − 1 had been closed under this rule. State writes nothing. The first write (`read`, `finish`: `prose.materialize`) stores them and sets `read_through` to the latest closed round, so un-reading such a paragraph sticks. Closed rounds keep what they showed: in round n − 1's view a block edited there needs a value ≥ n − 1, and a block it did not edit was read already, unless it was left unread in round n − 2 and the student left it alone (only in a ledger with two or more rounds closed before this rule): such a block now shows read in round n − 1's view.
- **`state.summary`** reads the map through `read_map` when the ledger has `read` (it read `read` as stored), so the papers list counts like the Changes tab. With the derived hashes it reads round n − 1's prose.json once per engine process.
- **Last lines of one word** (`prose._tails`). A text block's last line that holds a single word has no glue or kern of the block's own source lines, only the `\parfillskip` of the `\par` that a later line ends the block with (the blank line after it, `\end{itemize}`, the next `\item`), so synctex gives its box to that line: the block's parts (and bar) ended a line short, and the next item's began a line early. For paragraphs, items and reference entries, a box of the lines after the block's last line, up to and including the first line a block holds, is the block's when: no block up to this one holds it; it is at most a line high (`LINE`) and in the text (`_in_text`); it lies directly below one of the block's own boxes (its top at most `TAIL_GAP`, 6 pt, below that box's bottom, overlapping it across); and the letters of the PDF words inside it end the block's display text (`comments._fold`, so a hyphenated word's tail matches). Such a box is added to the block's parts and left out of every other block's (the next item, a heading).
- **Read state**: hashes are unchanged, so read state is.
- **App**: `Reading.bar(part, textX:, gap:, beside:)` takes the parts of the page's other paragraphs, read or not (so a bar never moves when another paragraph is read). A part is beside a wider one when they are level by more than 2 pt, overlap across more than half of the part, and the part is at most 90% as wide. On a one-column page a part beside a wider one that ends at the text's right edge (within 3 pt) and starts more than 3 pt inside it has the right margin, like one starting right of the text's middle (a wide right-wrapped float). A part whose bar falls on the same side as that of a wider part beside it gets its bar 4 pt further out (2 pt between the two bars): a float wrapped on the left, or two overlapping parts (a figure beside an oversized table box; before, their bars joined into one). Finish round says "… not marked read (the bars in the PDF). Finishing marks every paragraph read: the next round's bars show only what changes after this round."
- **Tests**: `tests/test_prose.py` `test_closing_a_round_marks_every_paragraph_read` (values, `read_through`, round 2 viewed after closing keeps its bars and unread change, round 3's bars are its own edits only, un-reading sticks) and `test_ledger_closed_before_rounds_marked_read` (an older ledger: round 3 without round 2's leftovers, state writes nothing, round 2's view unchanged, `summaries` agrees, the first write stores the hashes and an un-read sticks); `test_since` now expects round 2's unread edit read in round 3. `tests/test_reading.py` `test_last_lines_of_one_word` (Times, as ICLR: paragraphs and items whose last line holds only a year; each year is in its own block's parts and no other's). App `ReadingTests.testBarsBesideAWrappedFloat`.

Engine accuracy fixes, 2026-10-16 (BACKLOG O3–O10, O23, O24; no shape changes for the app; `PARSER` stays 6). Where it conflicts with §5 or earlier amendments, this wins.
- **Wrapped source lines** (`anchor._place`, O3, O8): for a range of text (not a point), words are also taken from the boxes of the source lines just before and after it (`NEIGHBOURS` = 1, line-high boxes only, from the one-pass `line_boxes`). synctex gives a PDF line to the source lines whose spaces it holds, so the first half of a hyphenated first word and a last line of one word (a paragraph's or a reference entry's) were left out of carried rects; only letters aligned with the range count, as before.
- **Paragraph breaks** (`texdiff.break_only`, O4): a hunk whose sides hold only white space and comments (a paragraph split or joined) changes no sentence: it makes no comment `changed` and is attached only to comments whose range it lies inside (like a move). `api._reattach` does not attach it by line either.
- **Files the build no longer reads** (O5): `TreeDiff.old_read`/`new_read` are the files under src each build read (the Input lines of its `.synctex.gz`, `texdiff.read_files`; null without one) and `TreeDiff.unread` the new tree's files its build did not read. A carried comment in a file the student took out of the paper (the old tree's `\input` of it, or of the file that read it, up to 5 levels, deleted, commented out or changed, and the file deleted or no longer read) is looked for (quote search) in read files only; if not found it is `gone`, confidence 1.0, at the place where that `\input` was, and the hunks holding the `\input`'s tokens are attached. A file not read while its `\input` is still in place (`\includeonly`, a build that stopped early) is not taken out. A gone point on a line that prints nothing before it, right before an `\input`, is drawn at the start of the file that `\input` reads (as for changes). `_search`, `_fuzzy` and `_by_label` skip unread files. Also fixed: a deleted file's hunk crashed `_inserted_before`.
- **Renamed files** (O6): rename candidates are old files the new tree lacks or no longer reads, and new files the old tree lacked or did not read. Besides sharing `RENAMED` (0.8) of both files' tokens, a pair qualifies when some file's `\input`/`\include`/`\subfile` of the old file was replaced by one of the new file and the new file holds `RENAMED_SITE` (0.5) of the old file's tokens. A file left behind unread is not compared with itself. prose's added/removed `\input` rules skip renamed files.
- **Words drawn twice in a picture** (O7): `to_pdf(…, hint, before)` with `before` = (src, pdf, anchor) of the last round: when the hint is looked for, the occurrence with the same rank (reading order) among those inside the range's synctex boxes is taken (`_rank`), else the nearest as before.
- **Reference labels** (O8): in a `.bbl`, a hunk inside a `\bibitem[…]` label is not listed (public false) when a hunk in the same entry's text makes the same edit (a year): one change, drawn on the year in the list.
- **Sentence ends** (O9): source (`texdiff.sentence`): no end after an abbreviation (`texdiff.ABBREVIATIONS`, now also `Sect.` and month abbreviations) or an initial; a full stop may be followed by closing brackets or quotes, and ends a sentence before `\footnote`; `\item` ends the sentence before it and starts one. PDF (`cards`): the same abbreviations; a footnote mark glued to a full stop ("cost.1") or set as its own raised word ends the sentence after the mark; a line starting with `1.`/`1)` followed by more words starts a list item like a bullet (a paragraph of its own, taken whole on cards) and is no sentence end.
- **Carried click notes** (O10): a note whose latest placement by the advisor (creation or re-anchoring) was a point stays a point beside its line (`anchor._beside`): at the same distance from the nearest of the line's rects in that round (`items[].click` = (src, pdf, anchor) of that round, from `api._carry`), so its card's Now block marks no words. `items[].point` now comes from that latest placement (was: the creation anchor).
- **Restored text** (O23): blocks of a file that an added `\input` reads and the previous round did not read are `added` (fresh), so a section brought back reads as new even when its hashes were read before.
- **Finish without paragraphs** (O24): when `finish` cannot find the round's paragraphs, `close_read` marks nothing and sets `read_through` to min(read_through, n − 1), so the next round counts round n's paragraphs read through `read_map` once they are found; `read_map` goes on without them (logging to stderr) if they still cannot be found.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_backlog_accuracy.py`, one or more per item on synthetic papers; each fails on the code before these fixes and when its own fix is reverted (the `\includeonly` case guards O5 against a false gone).

Readable changes and the summary's page size, 2026-10-16 (BACKLOG O14–O16, O18: the summary and the Changes tab showed LaTeX for formatting-only and `\input` edits, named no table, two-column summaries printed at about 60%, and leaders ran along margin line numbers). Where it conflicts with the amendments "Changes tab", "Final PDF in the margin style" and "Fix round M2b", this wins. Reviewed and accepted 2026-10-17.
- **Change keys** (analysis.json and state `changes[]`, from `cards.describe` at ingest, after `change_details`): `float`, the table or figure the change is in as its caption labels it in round n's PDF ("Table 2", "Figure 4"), or null; found through the `\input` lines that read the change's file (5 levels), and by the caption's first letters among that page's captions of its kind (`cards._float_name`). `about`, null or `{"kind", "text"}`: `"file"` for a hunk that holds only parts of `\input`/`\include` commands (and white space) on both sides, with what happened to the files they read ("Section file sections/07_conclusion.tex renamed to sections/08_conclusion.tex", "… replaced by …", "Figure 'drop_two_panel' removed", "Table 'results' added", "… moved"; a file is a Section file when it has a heading, else Figure or Table by its first float, else File); `"formatting"` for any other hunk (not a move or a replaced image) whose sides are the same once `cards.STYLE` commands, `\label`, spacing and braces are left out, or whose two contexts print the same sentence(s): "Formatting only: no printed text changed in §4.1" (the heading's printed number, else `§ <title>`; `in Table 2` inside a float).
- **Contexts of `\input` lines.** When the file an `\input` removed, added or moved holds a figure or table, that side of the change's `context` is an image of it (caption, pictures, tabulars, and the drawing beside the caption; `cards._float_context`) in the round that printed it: a removed figure has a `before` crop and no `now`.
- **Older analysis.json** without `float`/`about`: `state` (and `render.final_pdf`, through `state._change_details`) works them out once per process from the diff made again, keeping the stored contexts but for the figure or table of an `\input` line.
- **final.pdf.** Summary pages have the size of the build PDF's first page (Letter, A4), not the widened paper pages, so they print at 100%; the key's change part goes on its own line when it does not fit. A change's line (`render._change_text`: the summary's "Your changes" rows and the margin's `your change · …`) is its `about` text, else its words after `<float>: `. A change with `about` puts no struck words under a comment's margin item.
- **Leaders** (`render._leader`): past the words in the paper's margin on its side (a review ruler), a leader runs level through a gap between them (`render._gap`: the middle of a gap under 6 pt, else 3 pt from both; never a gap under 2 pt when there is another), leaving the chip's side at that height when it is level with the chip, else sloping to it before the numbers; then to its item. `render.final_pdf`'s placed items also give `leader` (its points) and `chip` (its rect), for tests.
- **App** (`mac/UX.md` §16): a row's location is `<float> · p.8` when the change is in a table or figure; its line, and a line under the expanded card's header, is `about`'s text (formatting in secondary); with `about`, no SOURCE block and no "Deleted in Rn" block.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_readable_changes.py` (synthetic: a renamed section file, a commented-out figure `\input`, `\noindent` before a run-in heading, cells of two tables; the summary and margin lines; an older analysis.json; leaders beside a two-sided ruler; `_gap`), `test_final_pdf.py` (summary sizes, `_change_text`), app `ChangeTests.testChangesTheEngineSaysInWords`.

Engine speed, 2026-10-16 (BACKLOG O12; no shape changes for the app; `PARSER` stays 6). Reviewed and accepted 2026-10-17.
- **`synctex view` on part of the file** (`anchor._view_target`, used by `_line_boxes`): synctex parses the whole `.synctex.gz` for each query (about 20 ms on a 25–60 page paper). A query for an input now reads a part of it in a private temporary folder (`tempfile.mkdtemp`, removed when the engine exits; at most `PARTS` = 16 parts, least recently used dropped, written again if the system clears one): the sheets (pages) whose records name an input of the same file name, every sheet that draws a form (`f` records), and every line outside the sheets, with the `Input:` lines of the sheets left out. synctex answers from the records of the input asked for, all in those sheets, so the boxes and their order are the same. The whole file is used when a part would leave nothing out (the main file names every page), no input has that name, or a part cannot be written. synctex is started by its full path with `close_fds=False`, so Python uses posix_spawn (2 ms less a call than a fork of the engine).
- **`rN/contexts.json`** (new cache, §2): `{"schema": 1, "contexts": {digest: Context | null}}`, the card contexts `state` works out for round-N records from before contexts were stored (M1 folders), keyed by the SHA-256 of the comment's JSON (`sort_keys`). `state` reads it before working a context out and writes it (temporary file, `os.replace`) after; entries whose comment changed since (an edit, a new round's record) are dropped then. The ledger is not written.
- **analysis.json of older rounds**: the change details `state._change_details` works out for an analysis.json from before they were stored are written into it, under the ledger's lock when no other process holds it (else left for a later state), and only when the file still holds the changes state read (so a concurrent `prose` or `reanchor` write is never lost). A later engine process reads them.
- **Measured** on two papers and a synthetic 64-page thesis (9 `\input` chapters, 96 comments, 82 changes): `synctex view` 20–23 ms → 3–4 ms a line; identical boxes in the same order for every line of every input of 11 PDFs (34,131 queries: both papers' three rounds, the thesis' two, and the ACM `sigconf,review` sample, the ICML 2026 and NeurIPS 2026 example papers with their sections in `\input` files). Re-ingest with compiling: ICLR round 2 12.6 → 10.6 s, round 3 13.0 → 11.2 s; ACL round 2 14.0 → 12.4 s, round 3 13.0 → 12.6 s (synctex 7.7 → 1.2 s over the four); thesis round 2 12.1 → 7.0–7.3 s (synctex 6.1 → 1.0 s). ledger.json, analysis.json and prose.json identical to before. `state` of copies without stored contexts or change details: 4.5 s (ICLR) and 3.6–3.7 s (ACL) on every launch before; now 3.4 and 3.5 s on the first, 0.01 s after, with the same state.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_synctex_parts.py`: every line of a synthetic paper (a file read twice, one opened mid-paragraph, a form, footnote, display math, list, table) gives the whole file's boxes from parts, and a cleared part is written again; an M1-style record's context is worked out once across engine processes and again after an edit, without writing the ledger; an older analysis.json gets its details stored, not while another process holds the lock nor when the file changed.

Template robustness, 2026-10-16 (BACKLOG O11: three-round reviews of synthetic papers in acmart `sigconf,review` and `acmsmall`, ACL review, ICLR, ICML 2026 submission and accepted, NeurIPS 2026 submission and final; no shape changes for the app; `PARSER` 6 → 7, so every prose.json is rebuilt once on first open, with the same hashes). Reviewed and accepted 2026-10-17.
- **Comments on `\maketitle` material** (`comments.locate`): acmart sets the teaser, the author block and the keywords at `\maketitle`, so synctex names that line, whose window holds no text; the fuzzy search then failed with an internal error (an empty search window: `_find` now skips it). When no match near synctex's lines agrees with the PDF words around it, an exact match that is the only place in all `.tex` files with the selection's letters (or with them without what `\cite`/`\ref` print), at least `MIN_UNIQUE` = 6 letters, is taken (`selection`, 1.0): authors, affiliations and keywords are printed in an order and under headings of their own. A name that occurs twice in the source (an author who is also the corresponding author) stays a `line` guess.
- **Captions are sentences of their own** (`texdiff._bounds`): `\caption{` (and `\subcaption`) starts a sentence and its closing brace ends one, so a comment on a caption is not `changed` by a replaced picture (same file name) or a new float width; a box on the float still is.
- **Paragraphs' places** (`prose._places`, parts): when TeX ships a page out while it reads a line, synctex gives that line the boxes the output routine sets: the page's head and foot (ICLR's foot is 15.5 pt high, more than `LINE`), the last footnote lines, a float's cells, a proof's end mark, lineno's numbers and acmart's whole title block. They gave stray bars (below a footnote, beside a table) and, in acmart's review mode, a part as high as the first page across both columns. Now a paragraph drops boxes also held by an earlier block's lines (other than a heading's: a run-in heading keeps the paragraph's first line), as items and reference entries already did; and every text block keeps only the boxes that print its text (`prose._own_text`: `OWN_SHARE` = 0.6 of the letters and digits of the words in a box lie in pieces of `OWN_GRAM` = 6 of the block's text, or the box ends with the block's first `OWN_LETTERS` = 12 letters or starts with its last ones); a box with fewer than `OWN_LETTERS` is kept when it is the line just above or below such a box (within `LINE`) or level with one inside `text_x` (an item's label), and when no box prints the block's text all are kept. Of several runs, the main one is one holding the block's own text rather than only its footnotes' (`OWN_BODY` = 0.3 outside `fn` marks), so a short paragraph with a long footnote is read where its text is.
- **Not read**: a paragraph or item whose text has no letter or digit (`prose.readable`; the `]` that closes ICML's `\twocolumn[…]` title block, which synctex placed on the author line).
- **Checked** on both private projects' copies (old against new engine): carried records, changes and every app comment's anchor identical; parts changed only where they were wrong (a table and a display formula of other blocks, a rotated table column on an appendix heading, the second line of run-in headings). On the eight templates: all 253 carried states as expected, every text comment anchored to exactly its selected words, every rect holding exactly its words, no ruler number in `text_x`, word boxes, rects, bars or leaders, bars equal to the app's (`Reading.bar`) and over no word.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_templates.py` (acmart teaser, affiliation and keywords; parts in an article with fancyhdr, footnotes and floats whose cells repeat the paragraphs' words; parts in acmart review mode; caption comments next to a replaced figure and a narrower wrapfigure; ICML's closing bracket). Each fails on the code before and when its own fix is reverted.

Review fix round, 2026-10-17 (the engine and visual reviews of the robustness workflow; no shape changes for the app; `PARSER` stays 7). Where it conflicts with the amendments of 2026-10-16, this wins. Reviewed and accepted 2026-10-17.
- **`\input` lines next to each other** (`texdiff._slide`): a pure insert or delete that covers whole source lines counts as much as one that starts at a word, and on a tie one that starts or ends a line wins, so a restored, added or dropped `\input` between two others is the whole line (was: "related} \input{sections/"). Everywhere a hunk is read as `\input` lines (`anchor._only_includes(diff, h, side)`, `_included`, prose's added and removed `\input` blocks, `cards`) the whole commands its range touches are taken (`texdiff.reads`, moved from `cards._reads`).
- **`\input` names** (`texdiff.INCLUDE`, `include_name`, `include_file`): `\input name` without braces is read too, and `./` and `a/../` are resolved (also in prose's file names); `anchor` uses the same pattern. An `\input` still in place that TeX now skips (`\iffalse … \fi`, a `comment` environment: `texdiff.HIDDEN`) takes its file out of the paper like a commented-out one; its gone point is after the skipped part.
- **Gone points** (`anchor.carry`): a point that falls inside an `\input` command (two edited `\input` lines in one hunk) moves before or after the command; with no `\input` after it, a gone point right after one is drawn just after the last printed word of the file that one reads (`_end_of`, the last section dropped, or one before `\appendix`). `to_pdf` of a point that matches no word is a `POINT_WIDTH` rect at its first line box, never the line boxes (a page number). A carried range made only of `\input` commands is drawn at the start of the file the first reads.
- **Dropped files** (O5): their comments are looked for by exact quote only (no fuzzy match into another section) before they are gone. Hunks inside a file the new build does not read are not listed (public false): they print nothing.
- **Restored text** (`anchor._restored`): a gone comment whose point a hunk touches (or an insert ends just before) is `moved`, method `exact`, 0.9, with that hunk attached, when its last quote (`items[].quoted`, the latest record with a non-empty quote, from `api._carry`) is found verbatim in the hunk's new text, in a file read by an `\input` the hunk adds or one TeX skipped before (`texdiff.hidden_reads`), or in a file those read (5 levels). It is drawn as that record's round printed it (`items[].quoted_at`: its source and PDF; O7's rank among equal words). The blocks of such files are `added` (O23).
- **Renames** (O6): `RENAMED_SITE` (0.5) is the share of the old file's runs of `SHINGLE` = 4 tokens found in the new file (was: single tokens, which two unrelated sections of one paper share half of). `cards` names each renamed pair of a hunk that holds several `\input` lines ("… renamed to …; … removed").
- **Sentence ends** (O9, `texdiff.abbreviation(word, following)`): `ABBREVIATIONS` never end a sentence; `AMBIGUOUS` words (`al. ms. no. nos. sec. secs. app.`) written in lower case end one before a capital letter or a command other than a citation, reference or footnote (capitalised, `Sec.`, `App.`, `No.`, they do not); an initial (`A.`, `U.S.`) ends one before a word of `STARTERS` (The, We, It, …) or such a command, so "Appendix A. The" ends and "J. Smith" does not. `cards._ends` passes the next word.
- **Neighbour lines** (O3, `anchor._place`): only neighbour boxes level with or within half a line of the range's own boxes count, so a line that closes a footnote no longer brings the footnote's words (a sentence after it was drawn on the footnote).
- **Carried click notes** (O10, `anchor._beside`): the line is found among the new rects by the letters of the old line (SequenceMatcher blocks of 3 or more), else by rank; a click in the page's margin or between the columns (`_in_margin`, from `comments.layout`) keeps its x when the new line is in the same column, any other click beside the line (after a short last line) keeps its distance from the line's end or start, and a point that would fall on a word is moved past the line's end; y keeps its offset from the line's middle, within the line.
- **Words a macro prints** (`cards._macro_words`, from `describe`): a replace that is neither `\input` nor formatting, whose context sides are text and differ but one marks no words of its own (`\answerTODO` → `\answerYes` print "[TODO]" → "[Yes]"), marks on that side the word-diff run nearest its `cut` (and has no `cut`). The summary, margin and Changes tab then say those words.
- **final.pdf** (`render`): a chip that would cover a margin ruler's number (1 pt to spare) goes past the ruler (3 pt beyond its outer edge) and its leader crosses no number; leaders pass between line numbers by their ink (`_ink`: a digits-only word's box less `DIGIT_INK` = 0.18 of its height above and below), so numbers set 2 pt apart (ICML, ACL) leave a gap and no leader goes round the whole ruler.
- **App**: id labels per `mac/UX.md` §5 (`Geometry.labelPlace`); the Changes tab word-diffs two text sides that mark no words when the change has no `about`, keeping only the runs at both cuts.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_review_fixes.py` (15, synthetic; each fails on the code before this round): restored, added and dropped `\input` lines between others; a drop merged with a renamed `\input`; a file read by a restored section; gone points after the last section and before `\appendix`; a point that prints nothing; a section replaced at its `\input`; `./`, brace-less, `\iffalse` and comment-environment drops and restores; no fuzzy match out of a dropped file and no change listed inside it; sentence ends; a re-wrapped click note; leaders and chips beside a dense ruler close to the text; `_gap` over digit ink; checklist macros; a sentence after a footnote. App: `LabelPlaceTests` (3), `ChangeTests.testWordsAMacroPrintsAreShownThoughNoSideMarksThem`.

Student-facing words, 2026-10-17 (UI v2: `design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md` §5 and decision 8; no shape changes for the app; the ledger's verdict values stay `verified`, `reopened`, `wontfix`). Where it conflicts with §9 or the amendments "Final PDF in the margin style" and later, this wins. It applies from the next `finish`: an email.txt and final.pdf written before are not rewritten, and `state.round.email` is email.txt as written.
- **Words** (`render.LABELS`): `reopened` reads "follow-up", `verified` "resolved", `wontfix` "no change needed"; "new" and "still open" stay. An earlier round's note reads `Round K (follow-up): <note>`.
- **email.txt**: the groups are `Follow-ups (k)`, `Still open (k)` and `New comments (k)`; then `Closed: R1-01, R1-03 (resolved); R1-05 (no change needed)`, and one line per closing note, `  R1-05 (no change needed): <note>`.
- **final.pdf, the margin**: an open comment's box is headed `↺ R1-05 · follow-up`, `○ R1-08 · still open` or `+ R2-01 · new`; a closed comment is one line, `✓ R1-03 · resolved` or `– R1-06 · no change needed` (a separator dot in both), with its verdict note below in the same grey.
- **final.pdf, the summary and outline**: Earlier comments are grouped `Follow-ups`, `Still open`, `No change needed`, `Resolved` (the resolved chips without a note, then "Closed. No action needed."); a group label wider than its column (`No change needed (k)`) takes two lines. The key, in the same order and only for the statuses of the round, is a sign and a word each, open ones black and closed ones grey: `↺ follow-up`, `○ still open`, `+ new comment`, `– no change needed`, `✓ resolved` (no swatch of the margin box: its colour no longer tells the statuses apart). Outline items read `R1-05 · follow-up · p.2`, `R1-03 · resolved · p.1`.
- **Colours** (`render.STATUS`: ID chips, margin boxes, headings and leaders, summary labels and chips, the key): an open comment (follow-up, still open, new) is black, `#1F1F1F` on white, told apart by its sign; a closed one (resolved, no change needed) is grey like the student's changes: mark `#A0A0A0`, fill `#F2F2F2`, text `#5C5C5C`. The blue, amber, red and green status colours of final.pdf go. The marks keep each comment's own colour (closed ones faded, as before); "✓ read" beside a change is grey, and the chips of the comments that ask about it black.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_rounds_core.py` `test_a_round_finished_now_uses_the_students_words` (synthetic: the words in email.txt, the margin headings, the key and summary order, the two-line `No change needed` label clear of its chip, and the outline; open chips and boxes black on white, closed ones grey; an earlier round's email.txt and final.pdf left as written); the word assertions of the existing tests follow.

Thread cards, 2026-10-17 (UI v2: `design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md` §2 and §5 step 2; the ledger is unchanged). Where it conflicts with §8 or the amendment "M2a engine" (`history[]`), this wins.
- **`history[].changes`**: each earlier round's entry names the changes attached to the comment in that round, as `rK/analysis.json` has them (`attached_to`, which a re-anchor in that round updated), in that file's order; `[]` in round 1 and for a round without analysis.json. Read only, like `comments[].changes` for the shown round. A comment carried by an unchanged anchor whose picture file was replaced (the change's `figure`) has `anchor_state` `unchanged` with that change attached, so the app knows in which round a figure changed.
- **App** (`mac/UX.md` §3): a round is a section of the card's thread when its `anchor_state` is `changed`, when it has attached changes, when it becomes `gone` or `orphan`, or when it follows a `gone` round (`moved`: restored) or an `orphan` one; otherwise it is a tag on the section above ("unchanged in R2"). The app no longer compares the printed text to decide this. The shown round's attached changes are underlined in the card's text by their own words (`changes[].context.now.sel`).
- **App colours** (`mac/UX.md` §2, replacing §10's status rows for the app): To check amber `#B45309` (Dark Mode `#F59E0B`) and Not found red `#DC2626` (`#F98B8B`), on tinted chips; every decided status (follow-up, resolved, kept as is, new) neutral grey `#6B7280` (`#A8ADB7`), told apart by its sign. A comment needs attention as before, except that a note placed by a click (`kind` note, not `gone`) is not low confidence, new or carried.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_cards.py` `test_two_rounds` (round 3: Table 1's box has `[[], ["c03"]]`, the re-anchored R1-04 `[[], []]`).

Email preview, 2026-10-17 (UI v2: `design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md` §3 and §5 step 4, the Finish sheet). Where it conflicts with §7 ("every result contains state") or the amendment "Earlier rounds and next steps" (`summaries` the one method without a state), this wins.
- **`email_preview`** (new RPC; paper) → `{"round": n, "text": "…"}`: the email.txt that `finish` would write for the open round now, `render.email_text(ledger, n)`, which depends on the ledger only. Read only: it writes nothing (no ledger, email.txt or final.pdf), takes no lock, and has no `state`, like `summaries`. Without an open round with a PDF it is refused as other open-round methods are ("There is no open round. Ingest the student's source first."). Requests run in order, so a preview sent after a `verdict` shows that answer. CLI `email-preview P`.
- **App** (`mac/UX.md` §17): Finish Round… is one sheet: the batch (follow-ups, new comments, still to check, closed), each unsent draft with Send as follow-up and Discard, what else is left (unrequested edits not checked, changed paragraphs not read), and this preview, asked again after every reply while the sheet shows; Finish and copy email then sends `finish` and copies `state.round.email`. The confirmation alert and the email sheet after it are gone.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_rounds_core.py` `test_email_preview_is_the_email_finish_writes` (rounds 1 and 2: the preview equals the email.txt that `finish` then writes, follows a follow-up and a resolve with their notes, leaves ledger.json and the folder's files as they were, has no state, and is refused once the round is closed).

UI v2 integration, 2026-10-17 (`design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md` steps 1–4 built, integrated and checked on whole rounds; no shape changes beyond the amendments "Student-facing words", "Thread cards" and "Email preview"). It gathers what UI v2 changed in this contract; where §4, §7, §9, §10 or an earlier amendment conflicts, this wins.
- **Ledger**: unchanged (schema 1; `verdict` null, `verified`, `reopened` or `wontfix`; carrying as §4). The app's words for them (`mac/UX.md` §2, §8): To check (null; Not found when the anchor is `orphan`), Resolve / Resolved (`verified`), Follow up / Follow-up (`reopened`), Keep as is / Kept as is (`wontfix`). The student reads resolved, follow-up and no change needed (amendment "Student-facing words"). Verify, Reopen, Won't fix and Withdraw are no longer shown; "Reopen" is kept for a later feature, reopening a comment closed in an earlier round, which needs the engine's carry (BACKLOG).
- **`verdict_note`** may go with any verdict. For `reopened` it is the follow-up the email carries ("Round N: …"); for `verified` and `wontfix` it is a closing remark, printed after the email's `Closed:` line (`  R1-05 (resolved): …`) and under the comment's closed heading in final.pdf; it is not a to-do. `verdict` with the current verdict and a new note updates the note (`mac/UX.md` §15 item 6); `verdict` null erases the note (`comments.set_verdict`), so the app itself keeps the note when it clears a verdict (it goes back to the card's field) and for undo.
- **Keys** (replacing §10's "V/R/W send verified/reopened/wontfix"): V sends `verified` and W `wontfix`; R puts the cursor in the card's follow-up field, and ⌘↩ there sends `reopened` with the field's text as the note (none when empty). V or W on a card whose field holds unsent text, or that was followed up this round, first asks whether the text becomes the closing remark. The same key again sends null; R on a Resolved or Kept-as-is card sends null too. ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z send the previous verdict and note again. An answer given from the keyboard then selects the next comment to check.
- **Attention** (replacing §10's "A comment needs attention when…"): the app no longer has that flag. Carried comments without a verdict are To check (Not found included) and are counted on the Comments tab and in `summary.to_judge`; a confidence below 0.75 shows as "position uncertain" on the card (a note placed by a click is exempt, amendment "Thread cards").
- **Unsent drafts** are the app's only, never the engine's: the text left in a card's field is kept in `~/Library/Application Support/Paperole/drafts/<first 8 bytes of the SHA-256 of the paper path, hex>.json` (`{"paper", "rounds": {"3": {"R1-03": "…"}}}`), written on every change, deleted when sent or discarded, when the round is finished and once a later round is open. Nothing of it reaches the ledger, the email or the student's folder until it is sent as a `verdict`. Test runs use `--drafts <dir>` or a temporary folder (`mac/README.md`).
- **Finish**: unchanged in the engine. The app's Finish sheet shows `email_preview` and then sends `finish` (amendment "Email preview").
- **Older review folders**: a ledger and outputs written before UI v2 open and read as before. email.txt and final.pdf already written keep their words (`state.round.email` is email.txt as written, so an earlier round's email still says "Reopened"); the next `finish` writes the new words.

UI v2 review fixes, 2026-10-17 (after the interaction and visual reviews; no shape changes; the ledger is unchanged). Where it conflicts with the amendments "Student-facing words" or "UI v2 integration", this wins.
- **final.pdf, the follow-up sign**: the margin box heading, the To-do chips and the key draw a U-turn arrow, as the app's follow-up sign (SF Symbol `arrow.uturn.backward`), where they drew an open circular arrow (↺); `render._glyph("reopened")`. Like the words, it applies from the next `finish`: a final.pdf written before keeps its sign.
- **App**: the shown round's attached changes of kind `move`, or whose `old_text` and `new_text` read the same, no longer mark the card's text (`mac/UX.md` §3). The verdicts the app sends are unchanged, but it sends none for a comment of a round that is not open (a choice row left open while an earlier round was shown sent the open round's `verdict`).
- **Unsent drafts**: a `--fixture` run (and so `swift test`) keeps them in a temporary folder too, never in Application Support (amendment "UI v2 integration").

Reading marks optional, 2026-10-18 (the advisor's decision: the margin bars overlap the Changes tab, which lists every edit to check; `mac/UX.md` §18 "Optional"). App only: no shape changes, the engine and the ledger are unchanged. Where the amendments "Reading in the PDF view" and "Rounds close read, and bar fixes" describe what the app shows, this wins.
- **App**: reading marks (the bars beside changed paragraphs not read yet, the status line's count with Next and Mark Read, the Contents' dots, N / C / Shift-C, the Reading menu and the PDF's Mark Paragraph Read / Unread, the Finish sheet's "Changed paragraphs not read" and its sentence about the bars, "changed paragraphs to read" in the window's subtitle and the papers list's "Paragraphs to read") show only while Settings › "Mark changed paragraphs to read" is on. It is off by default, kept in the app's defaults as `readingMarks` (bool; absent is off). A test run never writes it: `--background`, a `--fixture` run (`swift test`) and `--reading on|off`, which sets it for the run.
- **Engine, unchanged**: `state.reading` (paragraphs, headings, `read`) is still sent and still gives the Contents its sections; the app sends `read` only while the marks are on; `finish` still marks every paragraph of the round read, so turning the marks on later shows only what changed since the last round closed, plus any paragraph marked unread.
- **Changes tab, unchanged**: an unrequested change whose paragraphs are all read still counts as handled (`changes[].read`, `summary.to_check`), whatever the setting, so the app's counts agree with `summaries`.

Student notes, 2026-10-18 (the advisor's decision: a student explains a change, or why something was left as it is, with a comment line in the .tex, `% R1-03 …`; DESIGN.md §5 `attribute`; the app: `mac/UX.md`). Ledger schema stays 1: a ledger or analysis.json written before has no notes and reads as without any. Where §1 ("not expected to reply"), §4, §8, §9 (REPLY_RULE) or the amendment "Student-facing words" conflicts, this wins. Students still need not write anything.
- **Syntax** (`notes.comments_in`, `notes.parse`). A note is a LaTeX comment, an unescaped `%` to the end of its line, whose text (after more `%` and spaces) starts with a comment ID `R<round>-<number>`: any case, a number without its leading zero (`R1-3`), in brackets or parentheses (`[R1-03]`, `(R1-03)`), in full-width forms (`Ｒ１－０３`), with any hyphen or dash between round and number. More IDs may follow, after spaces or `,` `，` `、` `;` `；` `/` `&` `+` `and` `和` `与` `及`; then an optional separator (`:` `：` `-` `–` `—` `·` `.` `,` `，` `。`) and the note's text in any language, white space collapsed. The text may be empty: a bare `% R1-03` points at the change next to it. IDs are kept as the ledger writes them (`R1-03`), each once per line. A `%` that TeX prints is no comment: escaped (`\%`), in a verbatim, Verbatim, BVerbatim, LVerbatim, lstlisting or minted body, in `\verb` or `\lstinline`, or in the argument of `\url`, `\nolinkurl`, `\path` or `\href`'s address; after `\\` it is one. A comment that starts like an ID but is none (`R1.03`, `R1_03`, `R1 - 03`, `R-03`, `R1-03a`, `R103`) is malformed; `% R1: …`, `% R2-D2` and `% TODO R1-03` are no notes. A comment inside a block TeX skips (`\iffalse … \fi`, the `comment` environment) is still the student's comment: a note there counts (a student who hides a paragraph that way can say why next to it).
- **Which notes count** (`notes.find`, at the ingest of round n ≥ 2, after `anchor.carry`; round 1 has none). Only `.tex` files are read. A note (an ID and its text) that round n−1's tree held in a file its build read (every file when the files read are not known), at the same place, was left as it was: it is not round n's, and its problems are not listed again. The same place: the token just before the comment, or the one just after it, is unchanged (or moved with it) and is the one beside it now (`TreeDiff.same_place`), so a note stays left as it was when the student edits the words on one side of it. A note the old tree held elsewhere (moved, or written again at a new place, in any file) counts only when it names a carried comment and has a change next to it (below); without one it is not round n's, and on a closed or unknown comment it is no warning either. A note that was only in files the old build did not read never counted, so in a file the build reads it is new (a warning about it, `unread`, is not repeated while it stays in such a file). A note added or edited in round n counts when its ID is a comment carried into round n and its file is one the build reads. The lines of one ID are joined in source order (files by path, then lines): lines next to each other by a space, others by a line break, empty texts and texts already said (a note copied to several places) left out.
- **The changes next to a note** are attached to its comment (`notes._Source.nearest`, new side, same file). A note after text on its line takes the changes in the sentences on that line (`texdiff.sentence`) and no other, so a "kept as is" note at the end of a line never takes an edit of the next sentence. A note on a line of its own takes the nearest change within 3 lines (`notes.NEAR`; by the change's lines, white space aside) in its paragraph (between blank lines; a comment line is no blank line, so a note on the line above a paragraph is in it); when its paragraph holds only comments (a note between paragraphs) the nearest within 3 lines below it, never one above. It does not look past a sectioning command (`\part` … `\subparagraph`) or a float's `\end{…}` (figure, table, wrapfigure, wraptable, sidewaysfigure, sidewaystable, algorithm), nor up past a float's `\begin{…}` (a note just above a float reaches into it; one inside does not reach out). A note where a change took out a paragraph or more (its old text holds a blank line), between that change's point and the next token, takes it (a paragraph commented out with a note beside it). At the same distance a change on or after the note's line goes before one above it, and changes still level are all taken. A note with no change next to it attaches none. A note never moves a comment's anchor, and its own line is no change (texdiff blanks comments).
