Review fixes, 2026-10-18: a note took the nearest change anywhere in its paragraph, however far (a "kept as is" note took an unrelated edit 6 lines below, or 58 lines below past a `\section` in a source without blank lines, and hid it from `to_check`), and one between paragraphs took an edit in the paragraph above.
- **Ledger**. The carried comment's round-n record gets `"student_note": {"text": "kept as is: …", "file": "sections/intro.tex", "line": 42, "changes": ["c03", "c05"], "elsewhere": ["c05"], "lines": [{"file": "sections/intro.tex", "line": 42, "changes": ["c03"]}, {"file": "sections/intro.tex", "line": 60, "changes": ["c05"]}]}`: `file` relative to src, `line` (1-based) the first counted line's, `changes` the attached changes' ids in order, `elsewhere` those of them that carry had not attached to the comment (outside its sentence: attached by the note alone; the app does not read them as revisions of the commented text), `lines` each counted line with the changes next to it (the Changes tab names the line next to an edit). It is absent when the comment has no note that round; a note stored before the review fixes has no `elsewhere` or `lines` (read as []). The round gets `"note_warnings": [Warning]`. In analysis.json an attached change gets the comment's id in `attached_to` (so `unrequested` is false and it no longer counts in `summary.to_check`) and in `student_notes` (absent when no note names it). `reanchor`'s re-attachment (amendment "M2a engine") keeps a comment in `attached_to` while the change's `student_notes` names it, and sets its note's `elsewhere` to the note's changes outside the new sentence. Only the changes in its new sentence make it `changed`, as before.
- **Warning** `{"kind", "ids", "file", "line", "text", "message"}`, in order of file and line; `text` is the comment line as written, from its `%`:
  - `unknown`: no comment has the ID. "There is no comment R1-09, so this note is ignored." The ledger keeps what was read; when a comment with the ID exists by the time the state is built (the advisor made R2-01 later in the round), the state says "There was no comment R2-01 yet when this source was read, so this note is ignored." (`state.note_warnings`).
  - `closed`: the comment was closed in an earlier round. "R1-04 was closed in Round 1 (resolved), so this note is ignored."; "(kept as is)" for `wontfix`. A line naming an open and a closed or unknown comment counts for the open one and is a warning for the other.
  - `malformed`: `ids` is []. "This note's ID is not in the form R1-03, so it is ignored."
  - `unread`: a note in a `.tex` file the build does not read (texdiff's `unread`), with all its IDs. "The paper does not use sections/old.tex (the build does not read it), so this note is ignored." A malformed comment in such a file is not listed.
  - `not_tex`: a note (with a well-formed ID) added or edited in a `.bib`, `.sty` or `.cls` file under src (`notes.OTHER`), compared with the same file of round n−1. "Notes are read only in .tex files, so this note in refs.bib is ignored."
  - `error`: reading the notes failed (`file` and `line` null). The round then has no notes, and the ingest goes on. "The student's notes could not be read: …"
- **State**. `comments[].student_note` is the shown round's note, or null; `comments[].note` stays the advisor's text. `history[].student_note` is each earlier round's note, or null. Both are `{"text", "file", "line", "changes", "elsewhere", "lines"}` as in the ledger, with that round's change ids. `history[].figures` lists the changes of `history[].changes` that replaced a picture file (a change with `figure` in that round's analysis.json), so the app's thread says "new image file" for that round without guessing from its anchor. `history[].change_pages` (added 2026-10-19 for the card's turns, design/ui-v2.2 CARD.md §3.4; read only) maps each change of `history[].changes` to the page (0-based) it is on in that round's PDF, leaving out a change on no page, so an earlier round's turn of an edit a note linked elsewhere reads "· 1 edit on p.6"; `{}` when none (`tests/test_acl_layouts.py`). `comments[].changes` and `history[].changes` include the changes a note attached. `changes[].student_notes` lists the comments whose note of this round attached the change ([] when none). Top-level `note_warnings` holds the shown round's warnings: [] before the first ingest, in round 1, and for a round ingested before this amendment. `summary` and `summaries` are unchanged.
- **Email and final.pdf**. REPLY_RULE (§9) is now `No need to list what you fixed. To explain a change, or why you left something as it is, add a comment line next to it in the .tex: % R1-03 your note.` (`render.NOTE_EXAMPLE` is the example; final.pdf's "How to reply" keeps it on one line). It applies from the next `finish` and in `email_preview`; email.txt and final.pdf written before keep their rule. final.pdf does not show the notes.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_notes.py` (40, synthetic): the syntax variants (case, `R1-3`, brackets, separators with the full-width colon, full-width IDs, several IDs, Chinese text); malformed IDs and comments that are no notes; `\%`, verbatim, lstlisting, Verbatim*, `\verb`, `\lstinline`, `\url` and `\href` not read, `\\%` read; a note above an inserted paragraph, alone between paragraphs, at the end of an edited line, and in a paragraph without edits (none attached, not the paragraph before); IDs and lines joined in source order across files; unchanged notes not repeated, edited ones and added lines counted; unknown, closed (resolved, kept as is) and malformed IDs as warnings with file and line; notes in a file the build does not read; three rounds through ingest (notes in the ledger and state, warnings, `to_check`, `history[].student_note`, `state --round 2`, a re-anchor keeping the attachment, the email's rule, and a review folder from before notes). The key sets of `test_cli_round1` and `test_rpc` gain `student_note` and `note_warnings`; `test_rounds_core`'s stand-in diff stubs `notes.find`. After the review fixes (2026-10-18), 48: a note never taking a far change (7 lines down, after text on an unchanged line, past `\section` without a blank line, a note between paragraphs and the one above), a float's walls, a note where a paragraph was taken out (and not after a sentence taken out above), a note written again or moved to another file beside a change counting, one left in place beside a new edit or moved without one not counting (nor warning on a closed comment), a note moved out of an unread file counting, `not_tex`, a copied note said once with `elsewhere` and `lines`, `state.note_warnings`; and in `test_acl_layouts`, `history[].figures` through three rounds.
- **App** (mac/UX.md §2, §3, §16; `StudentNote`, `NoteWarning` in Models.swift, all optional so older states decode): each round's note shows in its thread section after the round's text, "R3 · student's note" with `file:line`, read only in every round; the edits it alone linked outside the commented text (`elsewhere`) make no section, label or underline there and are a link on the shown round's note ("· 1 edit"); an earlier round is "new image file" by `history[].figures`; a row has a sign for the shown round's note; a change whose `student_notes` names a comment reads "Answers R1-03" with that comment's note; `note_warnings` are one line at the top of the Comments tab with a Details… popover (file:line, the line as written, `message`). The app writes nothing for notes.
- **Review fixes** (2026-10-18, after an adversarial review): a note takes only a change close to it (above, "The changes next to a note"); a note written again at a new place (a bare `% R1-03` answering a follow-up beside a new edit), or moved to another file beside an edit, is no longer ignored as "left as it was", while one left in place beside a new edit is not repeated (so it does not take that edit); a note moved out of a file the old build did not read counts; notes in `.bib`, `.sty` and `.cls` files are warnings (`not_tex`); a copied note says its words once; `student_note.elsewhere` and `.lines`, `history[].figures` and the state's re-reading of `unknown` warnings; `notes.find` works out each file's lines, changes and walls once (a 275k-character file with 267 changes and 100 note lines took 3.4 s, now 0.02 s). Left as they were: a comment line glued to the text before it (`explain.% R1-03 kept`) makes TeX join the next line's first word to it, which texdiff does not see (it blanks comments to spaces; changing that would touch every `%` at a line end); a comment line without an ID under a note is not part of it (one line per note, as the advisor set).

Pictures as drawn, and page furniture in contexts, 2026-10-18 (BACKLOG O32's engine items, found in a student-notes demo; no shape changes for the app; `PARSER` stays 7). Where it conflicts with §5 or earlier amendments, this wins. Rects and contexts already stored (analysis.json, the ledger's records) are not rewritten; a round ingested from now on gets them.
- **Pictures** (`anchor.to_pdf`, `_pictures`, `_drawn`): when no word of a range matches and the range holds `\includegraphics`, its synctex boxes taller than a line are no longer drawn as they are. synctex gives a scaled picture its natural size, up and right from its reference point (bottom left), and also the line that holds it and, for some pictures, their printed size; so a picture made to fit one column of a two-column page was a change across the other column (demo round 4, c01: [70.87, 242.59, 527.32, 451.39] for a picture at x 70.9–289.1, its card's text the other column's sentences), and one in a `figure*` on a page of floats was cut to its caption's words. Each such box is scaled about its bottom-left corner, never beyond its size: to end at the gap between the columns when it crosses it and is in a float of one column (`figure`, `table`, `wrapfigure`, `wraptable`, `algorithm` without a star, in its file or around the `\input` that reads the file, up to 5 levels: `_wide`; the gap is the page's, `comments.columns`, or on a page of floats the PDF's middle one when at least half of its pages have two columns: `_gap`), else at the text's right edge on the PDF's pages (`text_x`), and inside the page; then down to just take in the drawings inside it (`_graphics`: a plot's background, a raster image, axes). Of the boxes so scaled, those that hold the most of the page's drawings go first and the smallest first among them; one per `\includegraphics` in the range, not overlapping, is taken. These rects stay inside the page and are not cut to the page's words; the line-high boxes are as before. A change's `rects`, its context sides and the contexts of comments anchored to a picture follow (on an axis label of Figure 7(b) the crop is Figure 7(b), no longer 7(a) and 7(b)).
- **Page furniture** (`cards.page_context`, `_furniture`): the sentences of a text context no longer run into the page number (a number alone on the page's lowest or highest line, `comments.without_page_number`) or a running head or foot (`_edge_row`: the page's highest or lowest row of text besides the page number, in the top or bottom eighth of the page, at least `HEAD_GAP` = 12 pt from its other lines, whose letters, digits aside, a page up to `NEAR_PAGES` = 2 before or after has in the same row within 2 pt of the same height). Words the anchor's rects hold are kept. Demo round 4, c05: the point of an insert after a column's last sentence showed "… across all tested retrieval depths. 7"; a line at the foot of one page only (a footnote) is text, as before.
- **Tests**: `test_acl_layouts.test_a_replaced_picture_is_drawn_where_it_is_printed` (a two-column paper whose plots, twice a column wide, are replaced in a `figure` with and without a background, in a `figure*` on a page of floats, and a small plot scaled up), `test_cards.test_a_point_after_the_last_sentence_of_a_page_leaves_out_its_furniture` (page number, running foot, running head; a foot line on one page is text). Both fail on the code before; `test_a_figure_replaced_under_the_same_name` now gets the picture, not the centred line that holds it.

Overview, 2026-10-19 (`design/overview/v2/OVERVIEW-v2.md` §5 and its §8 decisions, "as recommended"; `design/overview/FEATURES.md` §5.1: E1 `overview`, E1b the second engine process, E2 `edit_paper`, E3 `round.received`; `PARSER` 7 → 8, so every prose.json is found again once, with the same blocks and hashes). Ledger schema stays 1. Where §2, §4, §7 ("every result contains state") or the amendments "Prose view and reading" and "Earlier rounds and next steps" conflict, this wins.
- **`overview`** (new RPC; `papers`: [path], `marks?`: bool, default false, `buckets?`: {path: {section key: bucket id or null}}) → `{"papers": [{"path", "overview": PaperOverview} | {"path", "error"}], "students": [Student]}`, the papers in the order given; a folder that cannot be read gets its error. Read only, like `summaries`: no state, no lock, no file written, no prose.json built (`overview.py`). CLI `overview P [P…] [--marks]`. Measured on copies of both private projects and the demo (10 rounds): 0.07 s for the three in a new engine process, 0.01 s again (0.003 s for one paper); 28–32 KB a paper.
  ```
  PaperOverview = {"paper": {"title", "student", "created"},
    "summary": {"round", "closed", "status", "to_judge", "to_check", "answered", "handled"},  // as summaries
    "sections": [{"key", "title", "name", "suggested_bucket", "first", "last", "gone", "comments", "written"}],
    "rounds": [Round], "comments": [Comment],
    "threads": {"comments", "resolved", "kept", "open", "followed_up", "untouched_after_follow_up",
                "closed_after": {"1", "2", "3+"}},
    "pending": [n]}
  Round = {"n", "created", "received", "closed", "closed_at", "build": {"status", "pages"}, "pdf", "final_pdf", "email",
    "prose": "ok" | "pending" | "unavailable",
    "comments": {"new", "carried", "edited", "note_only", "untouched", "moved_only", "waiting", "not_found",
                 "resolved", "follow_up", "kept", "left_open", "to_check", "open_after"},
    "changes": {"total", "requested", "unrequested", "to_check"},
    "words": {"body", "added", "deleted"} | null, "unplaced": n,
    "spans": [[section, title, start, end]] | null,
    "match": [[from, to, kind, kept, of]] | null,
    "heat": [[section, new, open_before, to_check, changes, added, deleted]] | null,
    "edits": [[change, page, pos, section, attached_to, unrequested, to_check, move, added_words, deleted_words]],
    "excerpts": {change: "first words"}, "pages": [{"page", "comments", "changes"}],
    "marks": [[page, "comment" | "change", x0, y0, x1, y1, id]]}                    // only with marks: true; id since "App, paper map"
  Comment = {"id", "created_round", "kind", "color", "note", "path": [[round, state, page, pos, section, response, answer]]}
  Student = {"student", "papers": [path], "rounds", "comments",
    "land": {"columns": [{"bucket", "title"}], "papers": [{"path", "counts": [n]}], "total": [n]},
    "time": [{"path", "round", "next", "open", "you", "student", "received", "last"?}],
    "closed": {"papers": [{"path", "1", "2", "3+", "open", "followed_up", "untouched_after_follow_up"}],
               "total": {"1", "2", "3+", "open", "followed_up", "untouched_after_follow_up"}}}
  ```
  - **`prose`**: `ok` when the round's prose.json is current (`PARSER`); `pending` when it is missing or stale and the round has a build PDF; `unavailable` when the build failed or finding the paragraphs failed with this `PARSER` (`rN/prose.failed`, below). `pending` lists the pending rounds. Without `ok` a round has `spans`, `match`, `heat` and `words` null and its edits' and comments' `section` and words null; its pages, places and every count are given (from the ledger and analysis.json).
  - **Places** (`pos`, `start`, `end`): page units, `pos = page index + fraction`. The fraction runs from the page's text top to its text bottom: the highest and lowest paragraph `parts` on the page, widened to the round's usual text area (the median of those over its pages; a page without parts has the usual one), so a short last page ends part way. On a page with `build.columns` the left column is the first half of the page and the right column the second, `(column + fraction) / 2`; a rect across both columns counts as the left column when its top is in the top half of the page and as the right one in the bottom half (state's printed order; OVERVIEW-v2 §5.4 says the left column only), and a block's end stays in the column of its top. A comment's or change's place is its first rect's middle; a block's start is its first part's top. A round without paragraphs measures its pages by the nearest round that has them, else by the PDF's page height.
  - **Sections and `spans`**: the front matter (`front`, "Title and abstract") and each level-1 heading (References included: its heading block, its entries), in source order. A section starts where its heading is printed; without a place for it (References), where its first text block is (not a float), else where the text before it ends; starts never go back. It ends where the next one starts; the last one at the end of the last printed part, or of the last comment or edit counted in it, or at the end of the round's last page when it holds no paragraph (a reference list biblatex prints) or whole pages follow its last part (a listing that runs on: its part is its first page's box; on the ICLR copy the last prompt's second page). So the spans tile the row and floats count where they are printed: a comment or edit is in the section whose span holds its place (the last one past the end). `title` is the heading as printed ("3 Materials and methods").
  - **Section keys** stay the same across rounds while a section continues: a section takes the key of the section of the round before that it continues (`match`, below), else a new one from its own title (lower case words joined by `-`, at most 40 characters, `-2`, `-3` … within a round); `front` is the front matter. A renamed section keeps its key ("methods" titled "Materials and methods"); one merged into another ends. `sections` lists every key once (in the order first seen) with its latest `title` and `name` (without the number), the rounds `first` and `last` seen, `gone` (not in the latest round with paragraphs), `comments` (comments counted in it in any round, each once) and `written` (comments created in it, where they were written), and `suggested_bucket`.
  - **`match`** (round n ≥ 2 when rounds n−1 and n are `ok`, else null): prose.json's `previous` (below) puts each block of round n−1 in a block of round n. Each section A of round n−1 goes to the section B of round n holding most of its paragraphs (blocks of kinds `paragraph`, `item`, `bibitem`, `math`, `figure`, `table`, `code`, `picture`); on a tie, to the one with its title, then to the one with more of its comments (OVERVIEW-v2 §5.3 has the comments only; a split half that keeps the title continues the section), then the first. A section without paragraphs (or all of whose paragraphs were rewritten) follows its heading block, else a section of the same title. The front matter goes to the front matter. B continues the A that holds most of its paragraphs among those sent to it (title, comments, order break ties). Kinds: `split` (A's paragraphs in two sections, each more than a third of them: two rows), `merged` (A went to a B that continues another section), `moved` (a continuation outside the heaviest order-keeping run, weighted by paragraphs), `renamed` (title changed, numbers and case aside), `same`, `removed` (`to` null, nothing of A left), `added` (`from` null, no section's paragraphs went there, `of` its own count). `kept` and `of` are the evidence: "9 of its 10 paragraphs now sit in Methods". Keys in `from` are round n−1's.
  - **`edits`**, one per analysis.json change in its order: `page` and `pos` from its rects (null without); `section`; `attached_to`; `unrequested` (= no comment attached); `to_check` (the open round only: unrequested, not checked, not asked about by a comment of the round, not read, as `summary.to_check`); `move` (a move hunk, or an `\input` line `about` calls moved: it prints no words, 0 and 0); the words it put in and took out as printed, from prose.json: its `ins` spans and blocks `added` by it, its `del` entries and the `deleted` blocks it touches (kinds paragraph, item, heading, title, bibitem and the captions of figures and tables; inline math and chips are no words; a CJK character is a word). `words.added`/`deleted` are the round's sums (edits without a change id included), `words.body` the words of every text block but the reference entries. `excerpts`: a change's `about` text, else its context's changed words (Now, else Before), else its source text without LaTeX's comments, `\begin`/`\end`, commands and braces (a command's braced argument kept; as written when no word is left; since "Overview integration"), at most 60 characters.
  - **`comments[].path`**, one step per round the comment is in: `state` `placed`; `gone` (anchor_state gone: at its deletion point, else at the page of the change attached to it, else at the end of the nearest section still there at or before its own section of the round before, with `page` null); `unplaced` (orphan, or no page: `page`, `pos`, `section` null, counted in the round's `unplaced`, in no cell). `response`: `new` in its creation round; else `edited` (anchor_state changed or gone, or a change attached), `note_only` (a student note), `untouched` (written in the round before, or followed up there) or `waiting` (left open in the round before). `answer`: null in its creation round; else `resolved`, `kept`, `follow_up`, `left_open` (no verdict, round closed) or `to_check` (no verdict, round open).
  - **Round `comments`** count the comments in the round by those steps (`moved_only`: anchor_state moved among untouched and waiting; `not_found`: orphans; `open_after` = new + carried − resolved − kept). `heat` gives per section of the round the comments written in it (`new`), carried into it (`open_before`), those to check, and its edits and their words. Checked on all rounds of the copies and in tests: `carried` = the round before's `open_after`; edited + note_only + untouched + waiting = carried; the cells' new + open_before plus `unplaced` = new + carried; the cells' to_check = `comments.to_check`; the edits' added words = `words.added`. A round whose build failed has no comments (§6).
  - **`threads`**: per comment, its last verdict decides (`resolved`, `kept`, else `open`); `closed_after` counts only the rounds with a verdict between its creation and its close ("rounds you answered in"); `followed_up`: ever followed up; `untouched_after_follow_up`: untouched in the round after a follow-up.
  - **`students`**: the papers grouped by student, by the name folded (NFKC, case and all white space ignored; `edit_paper` puts one student's papers written differently together); `student` is the first paper's spelling. `land` (Student page, "Where the comments land", each comment once, where it was written): one column per standard section that any section of the student's papers falls in, in the order abstract, introduction, method, results, discussion, conclusion (titles Abstract, Introduction, Methods, Results, Discussion, Conclusion), then one per other section with comments, by its name; a section falls in `buckets[path][key]` when the app gives it (null: its own name), else its `suggested_bucket`. `suggested_bucket` is a suggestion from an English and Chinese word list (`overview.BUCKETS`: "Related work", "Background", "Preliminaries" → introduction; "Study area", "Evaluation settings" → method; "Experiments", "Analysis" → results; "Limitations" → discussion; …; the front matter → abstract), null for an appendix (a lettered number) or no match. `time` ("Where the time goes", calendar days, each date local), rounds whose build failed left out: a row per round trip, a closed round and the next round, `you` = closed_at − created, `student` = the next round's created − closed_at, `received` = the next round's received − closed_at (null without it; it can be negative); and a row for the open round, `open` true, `you` = today − created, `student` and `received` null. A closed last round has a `last` row (amendment "UI v4 data"; it had none before). `closed` ("How comments closed"): each paper's `threads` and the sums.
- **prose.json `previous`** (round n ≥ 2 with round n−1's paragraphs): a list as long as round n−1's `blocks`, each the index of the block of round n that continues it, or null (`prose._previous`): a hash only one block has on each side (also a paragraph too short for texdiff to see it move); else texdiff's mapping of its first token (else its last) into a block of the same file, unless the diff deleted the block wholly; else a block with its hash not taken yet. `deleted` and `missing` blocks are null. On the copies 199–227 blocks a round keep their hash, 4–12 are found through the diff, 0–2 are gone; the paragraphs moved across sections (one from Experiments into the Conclusion's file, one from Results into an appendix) are found by their hash.
- **`rN/prose.failed`** (new, §2): `{"parser", "error"}`, written when finding round n's paragraphs fails (ingest, `finish`, `prose`), removed by the next build that succeeds. The overview reads a failure with the current `PARSER` as `unavailable`, so the app does not keep asking.
- **`prose`** takes `state?` (default true); with false the result is `{"path"}` only (CLI `prose P --round N --no-state`). A round without a build PDF is refused before anything is built ("Round 2 has no build PDF."). The app's second engine process (FEATURES E1b) fills in `pending` closed rounds with it, one at a time, and asks `overview` again; it builds under the ledger's lock, so it never writes a file the main process is writing. The open round's paragraphs are still found at ingest (a failure does not fail the ingest) and by `prose` in the main process. Measured: 0.74–1.00 s a round on the copies.
- **`round.received`** (ledger, rounds ingested from now on; `project.received`): when the student last changed the source, the latest modification time of the files the snapshot copies (a .zip: its members' dates, read as local time), ISO 8601 local with offset; null when there are none. Rounds ingested before have none; the overview gives null and the app shows `created`.
- **`edit_paper`** (new RPC; paper, `title?`, `student?`) → {state}: sets the paper's title and/or student, white space collapsed, in one `ledger.edit`. Refused without either ("Give the paper's title or the student's name.") or with an empty one ("The student's name cannot be empty.", "A paper's title cannot be empty."). CLI `edit-paper P [--title T] [--student S]`. final.pdf and email.txt already written keep the old words.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_overview.py` (synthetic): four rounds with Study area merged into Methods (4 of 4), Methods renamed and Results split (3 and 3 of 6), Related work moved, the appendix removed and a Conclusion added; spans against the headings in the PDF; comments following a merge, a rename and a split, gone with the appendix, unplaced, and gone without a place; edits and their words, moves without words, `to_check` against `summary`; a `figure*` written in one section and printed in the next, and a heading and a comment in the right column; stale, missing and failed paragraphs and `prose` with `state: false`; `round.received` from a folder and a .zip; `edit_paper` and the students' numbers (two spellings of one name, `buckets`, `time`, `closed`), CLI; RPC without state and the time (a four-round paper in well under a second in a new process); `prose._previous` without TeX (a short moved paragraph by its hash, twin paragraphs by the diff, a deleted one nowhere); a listing that runs on to the last page. The split's title tie-break, the front matter's and moved sections' rules and the listing's were checked to fail when taken out.

App, overview navigation, 2026-10-19 (`mac/UX.md` §21; OVERVIEW-v2 §5.1, FEATURES OV-6: a paper's overview on a click, Review on a double click or at a position, Back / Forward, All Rounds). No shape changes: how the app reads the amendment "Overview".
- **Asking**: `overview` with `papers: [path]` for the selected paper while its overview is shown, through the engine process the review uses, written after that paper's `state` request (one engine, in order). It is asked again only after a request that may change a paper: every method but `state`, `prose`, `summaries`, `email_preview` and `overview` (and, since "Overview integration", after a `prose` that filled in a round it had pending). The second, read-only process (FEATURES E1b) was not started yet at this step (it came with "App, hierarchy").
- **Reading** (`OverviewData.swift`): `papers[]` only, the entry with the path asked for (else the first); every field optional; `spans`, `match`, `heat`, `edits` and `path` rows read by position, a missing or null item as nil, booleans also as numbers; section keys, change ids and `attached_to` as strings whether sent as strings or numbers. Keys it does not read (`students` until "App, hierarchy"; until "App, paper map" also `excerpts`, `unplaced`, `marks`, `sections[].name`, `first`, `last`, `written`, a comment's `kind`, `color`, `note`) are ignored. Pages are 0-based and places `page index + fraction`, as above; the app shows page numbers from 1.
- **Fallback**: an engine without `overview` replies "Unknown method 'overview'." (rpc.py); the app then shows the overview from the paper's state (title, latest round, the rounds' rails) with one line saying the map needs a newer engine, and raises no alert. An entry's `error`, or any other error, shows in that line instead.
- **Review at a section**: section keys are not paragraph blocks, so the app finds the section's heading in that round's `state.reading.headings` by the title `spans` gives (white space and case aside, level 1 first) and shows it as a Contents click does; without such a heading, the span's first page.
- **Tests** (`mac/Tests/PaperoleTests/NavigationTests.swift`): a synthetic reply in the amendment's shape (`mac/Fixtures/overview-m2a.json`, the `state-m2a.json` paper's), one written by the engine for a synthetic two-round paper with its paths replaced (`mac/Fixtures/overview-engine-sample.json`), and replies with fewer fields, numbers for ids and booleans, nulls and an `error` entry.

App, paper map, 2026-10-19 (`mac/UX.md` §21 "The paper map"; OVERVIEW-v2 §3.3, §4, §5.4–5.5; FEATURES OV-2, OV-3). One engine change; otherwise how the app reads the amendment "Overview".
- **`marks` name their comment or change**: each row is `[page, "comment" | "change", x0, y0, x1, y1, id]`, the id appended (`overview._marks`), so the thumbnails colour a comment's mark by its `color` and `kind`, outline the comment followed, and draw one bar or ring per change. A gone comment's anchor at its deletion point gives marks too. An engine before this sends six items: the app reads the id as nil. Test: `tests/test_overview.py::test_edits_and_words` (the comments of round 4's marks are those placed there, and at most the gone ones besides; the changes' ids are edits).
- **Asking**: `overview` with `papers: [path]` and `marks: true` (28–32 KB a paper without marks; the marks add one row per rect).
- **Reading** (`OverviewData.swift`), besides the amendment "App, overview navigation": `rounds[].unplaced` (the row's "+n unplaced" cap; without it, the comments whose step that round is `unplaced`), `excerpts` (an edit tick's help tag), `marks`, `sections[].name`, `first`, `last`, `written`, a comment's `kind`, `color` and `note`. A cell's count is its `heat` row's `new + open_before`; a chip's its `to_check`; a tick per `edits` row with a `pos`; bands from `match` only between consecutive rounds that both have `prose: ok`; a failed round's waiting comments are the `open_after` of the last round before it that built.
- **Fixtures**: `mac/Fixtures/overview-m2a.json` (hand-written) now uses the engine's words (`answer` resolved / kept / follow_up / left_open / to_check, `response` new in the creation round), `match: null` in Round 1 and `unplaced`; `mac/Fixtures/overview-kelp.json` is the engine's reply with marks for a synthetic four-round paper it built (the module fixture of `tests/test_overview.py` with more comments and edits), its paths replaced by `/tmp/synthetic/kelp`.

App, hierarchy, 2026-10-19 (`mac/UX.md` §22; OVERVIEW-v2 §3.1–3.2, §8 decisions 8 and 10; FEATURES OV-7–OV-9, E1b, E2). One engine change; otherwise how the app uses `overview`, `edit_paper` and `prose` for All Students, Your Move, the Student page and the sidebar's students. Where it conflicts with the amendment "Earlier rounds and next steps" (`summaries`' entries), this wins.
- **`summaries` name the paper**: each entry is `{"path", "summary", "paper": {"title", "student"}}` (`api.summaries`, from the ledger it already reads), so the app groups every listed paper by its ledger's student at launch and on activation without asking for each one's state. An error entry has no `paper`; an engine before this sends none, and the app keeps the names in its list. Tests: `tests/test_cli_round1.py`, `tests/test_rounds_core.py` (the entries' shape), `tests/test_overview.py::test_edit_paper_and_students` (a student changed with `edit_paper` is in `summaries` at once).
- **Asking for the hierarchy**: `overview` with every listed paper, `marks` false (true since amendment "UI v4 data"), and `buckets` from the advisor's Map Headings answers (kept app side per paper in papers.json; the app's "its own column" sent as null), through the review's engine process, when a page of the hierarchy is shown and the list, the answers, a paper or the app's activation changed since. The app reads `students[]` (matched to a student's page by its papers), each paper's `summary` (the sidebar's badges and Your Move), `paper.title` / `student`, `rounds[]` (dates, comments, spans, heat, match, pages for the key page), `threads` and `sections[].suggested_bucket`, `name`, `gone`. "Waits on you" is the app's: a paper whose latest round is open, a failed build included (decision 10).
- **`edit_paper`**: Edit Paper… sends the title and/or the student that changed (never an empty student, which the engine refuses); Rename Student… sends the new name for each of the student's papers, one request each. The reply's `state` is applied only for the paper shown.
- **The second engine process** (FEATURES E1b): a second `rpc` process the app starts with the first round to fill and ends with the app; `prose` with `paper`, `round` and `state: false` for each closed round an overview reports in `pending` that has a build PDF, one at a time, each paper's oldest first, then `overview` again (the paper's after each round, the hierarchy's after each paper). Each round is asked once per run; a failure is the engine's `rN/prose.failed` (`unavailable`). The open round is left to ingest and Review's own `prose`.
- **App side, not the engine's**: Finished (`papers.json` `finished`, a date), Map Headings' answers (`buckets`), the folded students (the app's defaults). A test run (`--no-list`, a fixture, `--background`) saves none of them.

Overview integration, 2026-10-19 (OVERVIEW-v2 v1a and v1b together; `mac/UX.md` §21, §22 "Integration"). One engine change; otherwise how the app asks.
- **`excerpts` from source text** (`overview._unlatex`): when a change has no `about` and no context words, its excerpt is its source text without comments, `\begin{…}`/`\end{…}` (and their optional argument), commands (and their optional argument) and braces, a command's braced argument kept ("\noindent \textbf{Warm-up Strategy.} We adopt" → "Warm-up Strategy. We adopt"); as written when no word is left. On the copies 39 of 200 excerpts began with LaTeX before, 1 after (an old round's stored context). Test: `tests/test_overview.py::test_an_excerpt_from_source_text_has_no_latex`.
- **Asking again after `prose`**: `prose` is read only for the app, so a round it fills in changed nothing the app counted, and an overview asked before it kept the round `pending`; after the PARSER 7 → 8 change every open round's row said "Finding sections…" until the next answer, though Review's own `prose` had found its paragraphs. Now, when the main process's `prose` reply comes for a round the overview had pending, the overview is asked again (or when it is next shown); a round filled in by the second process while Review is shown does the same; and every round filled in, by either process, is part of the hierarchy's request key, so its next request is a new one. Tests: `NavigationModelTests.testARoundFilledInWhileInReviewIsAskedForAgain`, `HierarchyModelTests.testARoundFilledInMakesTheNextRequestNew`.
- **Times** (RPC, same copies): `overview` of one paper 106–182 ms as the first request of a new process, 3–6 ms after (with marks 3–4 ms; 30–35 KB, 39–48 KB with marks); the seven papers 229 ms first, 30 ms after; `prose` 0.9–1.3 s a round from the command line (a new process each).
- **Known**: a round built before its change ids could be matched again by the lazy diff (amendment "Prose view and reading", **Change ids**) has `words` but no word on any edit, since its added and deleted blocks name no change.

Overview review fixes, 2026-10-19 (after the integration's data and interaction review; `mac/UX.md` §21, §22 "Review fixes"). Additions only; where the amendment "Overview" says otherwise, this wins.
- **`sections[].written_as`**: `[{"name", "number", "written"}]`, the comments created in the section by the heading it had in the round each was written (its `name` without the number and its `number`, as prose.json had them then); the counts add up to `written`. Test: `test_comment_paths_and_counts` (R2-01 written under "3 Methods", renamed "Materials and methods" in Round 3).
- **`students[].land`**: a section the app gives a bucket for (`buckets[path][key]`, the advisor's Map Headings) puts all of its comments there, as before; otherwise each comment lands in the column of the heading it was written under (`written_as`: its `suggested_bucket`, else a column of that name), not in the column of the section's latest name, so a later rename or a section continued under another heading does not move it. A paper without rounds has no row in `land.papers` nor in `closed.papers`. Test: `test_students_land_where_written_and_a_failed_last_round` (no TeX).
- **`students[].time`**: a closed round followed by a round whose build failed has its row (`next` the failed round, `student` to its import); the failed round, open, has the open row with `failed: true` (it waits on the advisor, decision 10). Rows otherwise as before.
- **`match`**: a section of round n−1 that had paragraphs, none of them left, whose heading went into a section of round n that continues another section, is `removed` (`to` null, `kept` 0, `of` its count), not `merged` with 0 kept (OVERVIEW-v2 §5.3). A section without paragraphs still follows its heading, and one that follows its heading alone into its own continuation stays `same` or `renamed` with `kept` 0 (the app's band tag says "Followed by its heading"). Test: `test_a_section_whose_paragraphs_are_all_gone_is_removed_not_merged` (no TeX).
- **Digests kept per file**: each round's prose.json digest is kept per file for the process (`overview._DIGESTS`, replaced when the file's time, size or columns change) instead of an LRU of 256, which a hierarchy request over more rounds than that missed every time (0.6 s a request for 29 papers of 261 rounds). Test: `test_digests_are_kept_per_file`.

Tags, key page and Reopen, 2026-10-20 (the advisor's requests of 2026-10-20: `design/tags/TAGS.md` §3, §6, §7, §9, §10 with the design critique's fixes of the same day; `design/overview/v3/VISUAL.md` §5.1 `part`). Ledger schema stays 1: readers ignore unknown keys, so a ledger written before has no tags and nothing reopened. Where §4 ("verdicts only in the open round"), §6 (open-round methods), §9, the amendments "Student-facing words", "UI v2 integration" ("Reopen" kept for a later feature) or "Overview" conflict, this wins.
- **Names.** The advisor's Reopen (a comment closed in an earlier round brought back into the open round) is `revive` in the engine: RPC `revive`, record key `revived`, state `revivable`, overview `revived`, render status `revived`. It is **not** the verdict `reopened`, which stays Follow up everywhere (ledger, CLI `verdict … reopened`, `overview` `follow_up`, `render.LABELS["reopened"]` "follow-up"). The student reads "reopened" for a revived comment (`render.LABELS["revived"]`).
- **Ledger.** A comment may hold `tags`: `[{"id", "by": "advisor" | "suggestion", "at"}]` in the order put on, on the comment itself (not on a round); no key, or [], is not tagged. Ids match `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}$` (built-in ids are words, `grammar` … `praise`; the advisor's own are `u-` and 8 hex digits, the app's), at most 12 a comment. The top level may hold `tag_names`: {id: name}, the names the app sent for the advisor's own tags (a backup for a folder read on another Mac; the app's `tags.json` is the catalogue: names, groups, order, hidden). A round record may hold `revived`: `{"from": k, "at"}`, the round the comment was closed in and when it was reopened.
- **`tag`** (new RPC; paper, id, `tags`: [id], `accepted?`: [id], `names?`: {id: name}, `state?`: bool, default true, `round?`) → `{"id", "tags": [id], "state"}` (`state` of `round`, default the latest; without it when `state` is false: the app's picker, one request a click). Sets the comment's **whole** list (`tags.set_tags`, idempotent): an id it held keeps its `by` and `at`, a new one gets now and `suggestion` when in `accepted`, else `advisor`; duplicates are dropped; [] (or null) takes the key away; `names` go into `tag_names`, white space collapsed (1–64 characters). On a comment of **any** round, a closed round and a finished paper included, without an open round: tags change no round's record, email.txt or final.pdf (§4's rule that only the open round changes does not apply to them). Refused, changing nothing: a bad id ("'Logic' is not a tag id: …"), more than 12 ("A comment holds at most 12 tags."), not a list, an empty name, an unknown comment. CLI `tag P R1-05 logic wording [--accepted logic] [--name u-3f9c1a2b=Hedging] [--no-state]`, `tag P R1-05 --clear`.
- **`comment`** takes `tags?`, `accepted?`, `names?` as `tag` does, saved with the comment in one write (a bad id refuses the comment). CLI `comment … --tags logic,wording [--accepted logic] [--name ID=NAME]`. Tags appear in no email, final.pdf, summary page or outline.
- **`revive`** (new RPC; paper, id, `undo?`: bool) → {state}. CLI `revive P R1-05 [--undo]`. For a comment whose last record (round k) is `verified` or `wontfix`, with the latest round n open and built (n > k):
  - **Placement**: `texdiff.diff_trees(rk/src, rn/src)` and `anchor.carry` from its round-k anchor (the item `_carry` builds, `api._carry_item`; `old_pdf` round k's), so `anchor_state` (changed, moved, unchanged, gone, orphan), method and confidence say what changed **since it was closed**. The record: that, verdict null, note "", `revived: {"from": k, "at"}`. Round n's changes (analysis.json) that start in its new sentence are attached to it (`_reattach`, as for `reanchor`), so the edit that broke it is no longer unrequested; with such an edit (not a move) a record not gone or orphan is `changed`. Its `context` is round n's with `changed` and `against` round k's text (`cards.reference_round`: the closing round in the round revived; later, a revived round counts as a follow-up's does). It keeps its id; rounds k+1 … n−1 have no record.
  - **Then** it is a carried comment of round n: To check (`summary.to_judge` counts it), any verdict and note, and §4's carrying (closed by `verified`/`wontfix`, carried into n+1 otherwise, as an ordinary record there, its `revived` round in `history`). An open round holding a revived record is not replaced by `ingest` ("Round N already has comments or verdicts…"), as with a verdict.
  - **Undo** (`undo: true`, while round n is open): the round-n record goes (its verdict and note too), and the comment comes off every change of round n it was attached to (`unrequested` again). Refused: "R1-05 was not reopened in Round 4."
  - **Refused**, changing nothing: "There is no round yet. …", "Round 4 is closed: import the next draft first.", "Round 4 has no PDF (the build failed): there is nothing to place R1-05 on.", "R1-05 was written in Round 4.", "R1-05 is already in Round 4." (revived), "R1-05 is still open; it is in Round 4 already.", "R1-05 is not closed.", "There is no comment R1-05."
- **Edits near closed comments** (the Changes tab's hint, so the advisor finds what the student broke; TAGS.md §9.7, made part of Reopen by the critique). At ingest of round n (`api._shadows`), the comments closed before round n are carried into round n's source without the PDF (`anchor.shadows`: the diff rules of `_by_diff`, else the quote found verbatim): those closed in round n−1 from their round-(n−1) anchors, earlier ones from round n−1's `shadows`. rN/analysis.json gains `shadows`: {id: {"anchor": {file, start, end, lines, quote, prefix, suffix}, "closed": k, "verdict"}} (one whose words were all deleted, or found nowhere, is not followed further), and a change that edits one's sentence (not a move nor only a paragraph break; an insert at the sentence's start counts, as for carry) gains `near_closed`: [ids]. When round n−1 was ingested before this (no `shadows` key), the comments closed before it are first carried into its source from the rounds they were closed in, one diff a closing round (`api._older_shadows`), so an older folder gets the hint at its next ingest. A failure is a progress line ("Edits near closed comments could not be found: …", traceback on stderr) and leaves the round without them.
- **State.** `comments[]` gain `tags` ([id]), `env` (the creation anchor's `env`, for the app's suggestions by place; null before anchors had it), `revived` (the shown round's record's, or null) and `revivable` (bool: `comments.revivable`, about the paper's open round now, so an earlier round's state has today's value, like `rounds` and `summary`); `history[]` gains `revived`. `changes[]` gain `near_closed`: `[{"id", "round": the round it was closed in, "verdict", "revivable"}]`, the comments of analysis.json's `near_closed` that have no record in the shown round. `note_warnings`: a `closed` warning about a comment revived in the round since says "R1-05 was closed when this source was read, so this note was not attached to it; you reopened it later."
- **email.txt** (and `email_preview`): a first group `Reopened (k)`, before `Follow-ups`, of the comments revived in the round and not closed again (a follow-up written for one keeps it there; resolved or kept again in the round, it is in the `Closed:` line as any). Each entry: the id, page and quote, the original note, `  Closed in Round 2 (resolved), reopened in Round 4.`, and `  Round 4: <note>` when a follow-up was written. A comment carried after its revived round is ordinary again (`Round 4 (follow-up): <note>` when followed up there); an earlier round's closing remark (`Round 2 (resolved): …`) is never repeated under an open comment, only an earlier follow-up's note is (`render._verdict_note`; before Reopen no open comment had one).
- **final.pdf**: a revived comment's margin box is an open one (black), headed `R1-05 · reopened` **with no sign** (no circular arrow: ↺ meant follow-up in final.pdfs before 2026-10-17, and the U-turn is the follow-up's), its follow-up first, then grey `Closed in Round 2 (resolved) · Round 1: <note>`, or without a follow-up its note and `Closed in Round 2 (resolved), reopened in Round 4.`; the summary's Earlier comments open with `Reopened (k)`, its To-do chip has no sign, the outline reads `R1-05 · reopened · p.8`; the key lists no sign for it (the word is on the box and the group).
- **`overview`** takes `key_pages?`: {path: {"round", "page"}} (the advisor's Set as Key Page, kept app side in papers.json like `buckets`; refused unless ints: "key_pages maps a paper folder to {round, page}.") and `tag_order?`: [id] (the advisor's visible tags in order; the writing profile's picks consider only these, in this order; without it the built-in order, then the other ids). CLI `overview P… [--key-page P ROUND:PAGE]… [--tag-order logic,grammar]`.
  - **`spans`** rows gain a fifth item, `part`: `front`, `body`, `references` (a section holding reference entries, or an unnumbered References / Bibliography / 参考文献) or `appendix` (a section numbered by a letter, or an unnumbered Appendix / Appendices / Supplementary material, and every later section but a reference list); `sections[]` gain `part` (the latest round's). The app folds the appendix by it (VISUAL.md §5.1; the fold decided once per paper is the app's).
  - **`comments[]`** gain `tags`, `revived` (`[{"round", "from"}]`, [] when never) and `revivable`. A revived step's `response` is `edited` (changed or gone since it was closed, or an edit attached), `note_only` or `untouched`; its `answer` as any carried one's. **Round `comments`** gain `revived` (revived in the round); so `carried` = the round before's `open_after` + `revived` (the identity of "Overview" holds with it). **`threads`** and `students[].closed` (rows and total) gain `revived` (comments ever reopened); `closed_after` counts every answered round, the closing one before the revive included; a revived comment is open until closed again.
  - **`key_page`**: `{"round", "page", "chosen", "followed"}` in the latest built round, or null without one. Without a choice the busiest page (most comments and edits, the first of equals: `MapRules.keyPage`), `chosen` false, `followed` null. Chosen in the latest round: that page (within the PDF), `followed` null. Chosen in an earlier round: the block that stands for the page there (the largest figure or table on it, else the first paragraph or item that starts on it, else the first block read in the PDF that starts on it) followed through each later round's prose.json `previous` to the latest round, `followed` "block" and the page its first part is on; when the page has no block, a round on the way has no current prose.json, or the block is gone, the same page number (within the PDF), `followed` "page".
  - **`tags`** (each paper; `tags.paper_tags`): `{"comments", "tagged", "open", "rounds": [{"n", "new", "untagged", "by_tag": {id: k}}], "by_tag": {id: Row}, "untagged": Row}`, Row = `{"comments", "open", "closed_after": {"1", "2", "3+"}, "followed_up", "revived", "written_in": [[section key, k]]}` (`written_in`: where the comments were written, the most first, then the sections' order; a comment written in no section is in none). A comment counts in the round it was written (`created_round`, a tag put on later too) and in each of its tags' rows; `comments`, `tagged`, `open` and a round's `new` and `untagged` count it once. Counts only: no shares, no ranking, rows in no particular order (the app shows its own order).
  - **`students[].tags`** (`tags.student_tags`, from every paper of the student): `{"comments", "tagged", "rounds": [{"path", "n", "date", "pages", "first", "closed", "added", "new", "untagged", "by_tag"}]` (each built round of each paper, in time order; `added`: the words the student put in, the round's `words.added`), `"by_tag": {id: Row + "papers": {path: k}}`, `"untagged"`, `"first_drafts": {"points": [path], "skipped": [{"path", "why": "small" | "untagged"}], "trend": {id: {"word", "why", "counts"}}}`, `"facts"`, `"focus"`, `"praise"}`.
  - **Trend words** compare first drafts only (each paper's Round 1, in time order; TAGS.md T6 with the critique's fix). A first draft is a point with at least 5 new comments, at least 4 in 5 of them tagged (else `skipped`, `small` or `untagged`). `word` is null with `why` `two_points` (fewer than three first drafts), `small` / `untagged` (three or more, fewer than three points: the first skipped one's reason) or `lengths_differ` (the longest point has more than 1.5 times the pages of the shortest). Else over the points' counts (`counts`): `same` when they all lie within one of each other; `fewer` when every step goes down, the first is at least 4 and the last at most half of it; `more` when every step goes up, the last at least 4 and at least double the first; else `mixed` (no clear trend: 3 → 2 → 1 is noise). No trend words between rounds of one paper.
  - **`facts`**: at most two, one a tag, in `tag_order`, never Well done (`praise`): for each tag the first that holds of `{"tag", "kind": "none_since", "path", "round"}` (it has 2 comments or more, and the student's rounds since the last one with it, at least two of them closed, have none: "none since Reef's Round 2"), `{"tag", "kind": "slow", "n", "of"}` (n of its `of` comments closed after two answered rounds or more, n ≥ 3 and at least a third), `{"tag", "kind": "new_words", "path", "round", "n", "words"}` (the latest closed round from Round 3 of a paper in which the tag had 3 comments or more before: its n new comments with the tag against the `words` the student put in, never as a rate; this is what a student with one paper gets). A fact never sets two papers' counts side by side.
  - **`focus`** (For the next draft): at most two tags of `tag_order`, never `praise`, with 3 comments or more and a score (`open` + its new comments in each paper's latest round + `followed_up`) of 2 or more, the highest first, ties in `tag_order`: `{"tag", "comments", "open", "followed_up", "score", "example": {"path", "id", "round", "page", "text", "note"}}`, the example its latest open comment (else its latest), `text` the words it is printed beside in its latest round that prints them (240 characters at most), `note` the advisor's. **`praise`**: `{"comments", "latest": {"path", "id", "round", "page", "note"} | null}`.
- **Not in the engine**: tag suggestions are the app's (TAGS.md §5: as the advisor types; this Mac only; the engine's one request at a time would hold the review up), so there is no `suggest_tags`; nor are Merge / Separate (`tag_alias`), deferred with the critique.
- **Tests** (synthetic): `tests/test_tags.py` (set, keep `by` and `at`, clear, names, refusals leaving the ledger as it was, a closed round with final.pdf and email.txt unchanged, contexts keyed without tags, CLI and RPC; the overview's counts on a built paper; without TeX: a paper's rows (two tags, once in totals, written in, how they closed, revived), the trend words and each reason for none, facts in the advisor's order and never Well done, a one-paper student's `new_words`, focus and its example, Well done, `_students`), `tests/test_revive.py` (a three-round paper: edits near the two closed comments named and their shadows, `revivable` in an earlier round's state; an older folder's Round 3 without shadows, Round 4 naming a comment closed in Round 2 and one closed in Round 3; revive with refusals, `changed` since the closing round, the edit attached, context against Round 2, a gone one, undo, the note warning, the email's Reopened group before and after a follow-up and resolved again, the overview's path, counts and threads, final.pdf's group, box and outline, and Round 4 carrying it on; the open round, closed round and failed build refusals; a closing remark not repeated under an open comment, without TeX), `tests/test_overview.py` (`test_key_page_and_parts`: busiest, chosen, followed through the rounds, an unknown round, parts, RPC and CLI; `test_the_key_block_is_followed_through_the_rounds`, `test_parts_of_a_paper` without TeX).

App, folding parts, 2026-10-20 (`mac/UX.md` §23; `design/overview/v3/VISUAL.md` §5 with the design critique's fixes). No engine change; how the app reads `spans`' `part` (amendment "Tags, key page and Reopen").
- **Reading** (`OverviewData.swift`, `OverviewFold.swift`): a `spans` row's fifth item as `part` when it is a string (an engine before sends four: nil). A round's parts are the engine's when every row has one, else worked out by the app as before the field: References by its heading (References, Bibliography, Literature cited, Works cited, 参考文献), the appendix as the lettered first-level sections (or one called Appendix…) after it, or, in a paper without References, a run of them at the end that starts with A. The app folds the appendix's first run of rows and the References row.
- **What folds is the app's**: decided once per paper (`FoldPlan`), at the map's own width, from the spans' extents; a block's count is the sum of its sections' `heat` (`new + open_before`), its chip the sum of their `to_check`; a band joins two rounds' blocks when both have the part. Nothing the engine counts changes; the table view and the help tags still read each section.
- **Fixture**: `mac/Fixtures/overview-sonar.json`, a fictional four-round paper (made by a script from the design's example, with `part` in every row: front, body, references, appendix), its path `/synthetic/sonar`.

App, tags, key page, Reopen and the last place, 2026-10-20 (`mac/UX.md` §24; `design/tags/TAGS.md` with the design critique's fixes). No engine change; how the app uses amendment "Tags, key page and Reopen".
- **Catalogue**: the app's own (`Tags.swift`), Application Support/Paperole/tags.json `{"schema": 1, "tags": [{"id", "name", "group", "hint", "focus", "sign", "hidden", "created"?}]}`, written at the first change; a test run (`--background`, a fixture, a unit test) keeps one of its own (`--tags <file>`, else a temporary folder). Built-in ids `grammar` … `praise`; the advisor's own `u-` and 8 hex digits. Merge (Settings › Tags › Merge Into…) is the app's: each listed comment holding the merged id gets the list with it replaced (`tag`, `state: false` for another paper), then the id leaves the catalogue (a built-in one is hidden); no `merged` map and no `tag_alias` (deferred), so it cannot be undone and papers not listed keep the id.
- **Writing tags**: `comment` with `tags`, `accepted` (the ids taken from a suggestion) and `names` (the advisor's own tags' names) for a new comment; `tag` with the comment's whole list when a card's picker closes (once, not a request a click), with `round` while an earlier round is shown, so the state that comes back is that round's; from Tag Comments… of a student's papers and from a merge, `tag` with `state: false`. The app shows a card's tags before the reply.
- **Reading**: `state` `comments[].tags`, `env` (suggestions by place), `revived` (the row's word "reopened", the conversation's "you reopened it", Undo Reopen), `revivable` (Reopen in Round N on a closed card of an earlier round), `history[].revived`, `changes[].near_closed` (the Changes tab's "near R1-07" and Reopen R1-07…); `overview` paper `tags` (Topics), `comments[].tags`/`revived`/`revivable` (following a tag, the panels), `key_page` (the strip's bookmark, the hierarchy's key pages), `students[].tags` (the writing profile). Every one decodes as optional, so an engine before them reads as no tags, nothing reopened, the busiest page; a dictionary keyed by path (`by_tag[…].papers`) is not read (the decoder's snake-case conversion would change paths with underscores).
- **Requests**: `overview` of the paper shown and of the hierarchy send `tag_order` (the catalogue's visible tags in order) and `key_pages` (papers.json's `key_page`: `{path: {round, page}}`, page 0-based); a change of either asks again. `revive` and `revive` with `undo: true` for Reopen and Undo Reopen; the state that comes back is the latest round's, which Review then shows with the comment selected.
- **Key page** in papers.json: `"key_page": {"round": 4, "page": 5}` beside `buckets`; never written by a `--background` run (the app's list is written by no test run any more).

Audit fixes, 2026-10-20 (the code audit of the day's tags and Reopen; `mac/UX.md` §25). Additive: an app or engine before them keeps working.
- **`retag`** (new RPC; paper, `from`: id, `into`: id or null, `names?`: {id: name}, `state?`: bool, default true, `round?`) → `{"changed": [id], "state"}` (without `state` when it is false). Merge Into… for one paper (`tags.replace`): every comment holding `from` holds `into` in its place, keeping `from`'s `by` and `at`; a comment holding both keeps its own `into` once; `into` null takes `from` off (an empty list takes the key away). Each comment's list is read from the ledger as it is now, never one the app sent: whole lists sent through `tag` from overviews the app held could be stale and overwrote tags put on since. Any round, closed or not, as `tag`; email.txt and final.pdf never change. `changed`: the ids changed, in the ledger's order ([] when none held `from`). Refused, changing nothing: a bad id ("'Math' is not a tag id: …"), a missing `from` or `into` ("Missing parameter: into"), a bad name. The app sends it to every listed paper and lets the tag leave its catalogue only when every one took it. CLI `retag P u-1a2b3c4d clarity [--name u-1a2b3c4d=Hedging] [--no-state]`, `retag P grammar` (taken off).
- **`tag_names`** in **`state`** (top level, {id: name}, {} when none) and in each paper of **`overview`** (beside `key_page`): the ledger's names of the advisor's own tags, so the app names a tag its catalogue lacks (deleted or merged while the paper was out of the list, a folder from another Mac) instead of showing its id.
- **Shadows that failed** (amendment "Tags, key page and Reopen", edits near closed comments): when carrying the closed comments fails at ingest, rN/analysis.json is written **without** `shadows` (it was `"shadows": {}`), so the next ingest derives them from the rounds they were closed in (`_older_shadows`) rather than taking an empty set as all there is; a present `{}` still means that no closed comment was followed.
- **Tests** (synthetic): `tests/test_tags.py::test_merge_reads_each_list_from_the_ledger` (a tag put on after the app read the paper kept, `by` and `at` kept, both held, taken off, refusals, `tag_names` in state and overview, CLI), `tests/test_revive.py::test_shadows_that_failed_are_derived_at_the_next_round`; the state's key set in `test_rpc.py` and `test_cli_round1.py`.

App, audit fixes, 2026-10-20 (`mac/UX.md` §25). The app sends `retag` for Merge Into… (no longer `tag` per comment), learns `tag_names` from every `state` and `overview` reply before showing it, never writes over a tags.json it cannot read (moved aside as tags.json.bad), and caps a tag's name at 32 characters and 64 code points, the engine's `names` limit.

Export, 2026-10-21 (`mac/UX.md` §28, Settings › Data & Storage: the advisor's review of 2026-10-20, "数据的导入导出"). Additive and read only: an app or engine before it keeps working (an app without the method never asks; an engine without it replies "Unknown method 'export_comments'.", which the app words as an engine to update).
- **`export_comments`** (new RPC; `papers`: [path], refused unless a list of strings: "papers is a list of paper folders.") → `{"papers": [{"path", "paper": {"title", "student"}, "tag_names", "comments"} | {"path", "error"}]}`, in the order given; a folder that cannot be read gets its error (an EngineError's text, or a file error's), as in `summaries`. No `state`, no lock, nothing written (`export.py`, from the ledger and `overview.paper_overview`). CLI `export P [P…]`.
  - **`comments`**: every comment of the ledger, in its order: `id`, `created_round`, `created`, `kind`, `color`, `note`, `tags` ([id]), `rounds`: one per round it has a record in, oldest first: `round`; `section`, that round's own title of the section it is printed in (`spans`' title; null when not placed or the round's paragraphs are not known yet); `page` (0-based, null when not placed), `place` (`placed`, `gone`, `unplaced`), `response` and `answer` as the overview's `path` has them (`answer` null in the round it was written); `state`, `verdict` and `verdict_note` as the ledger's record has them; `student_note`, the text of the student's note in that round (`student_note.text`, null without one); `text`, the PDF words it marks there.
- **The app** writes the files (`CommentExport`): CSV, one row a comment and round, in the app's words, pages from 1, tags by name (the catalogue's, else `tag_names`, else the id), UTF-8 with a byte-order mark and CR LF; or JSON `{"schema": 1, "exported", "of", "papers": [{"title", "student", "folder", "comments"}], "errors"}`. Everything else of Data & Storage (zips, backup, restore, the tags' file) is the app's own, with `ditto`; the engine stays the only writer of ledgers and PDFs, and no review folder is written into: they are zipped, copied or moved whole.
- **Tests** (synthetic): `tests/test_export.py` (the kelp paper of `test_overview`: each comment once, its rounds, places, responses, answers, verdicts and notes, sections as each round prints them, the appendix's comment gone; tags, `tag_names` and a student's note on a copy; a missing folder; refusals; CLI; nothing written).

Data foundations, 2026-10-22 (`design/data/DATA.md` §2.2–§2.4 and §6.3, with the advisor's decisions and cuts of its §0: no Repair Mine, nothing per student). Ledger schema stays 1 and every new field is additive: a ledger, analysis.json or backup written before keeps working, and an engine before this reads the new fields as unknown keys. Where §2 (analysis.json a cache; a crashed round's folder deleted), §4, §5 (`ledger.edit`, `render.final_pdf`), §6, §7, the amendments "Overview" and "Tags, key page and Reopen" (`buckets`, `key_pages` app side) or "App, hierarchy" ("App side, not the engine's") conflict, this wins.
- **`paper.id`**: `p-` and 16 hex digits, random at `new` (`ledger.new_paper_id`). A ledger written before has none: it is derived (`ledger.derived_id`, the SHA-256 of `paper.created`, round 1's `created` and the names and sizes of r1/src's files, so byte copies of a folder share it and papers made in the same second from other sources don't), stored by the first write that changes the ledger, and given meanwhile without writing. In `state.paper`, `summaries[].paper` and `overview`'s `paper` (below).
- **Versions**: each write is a new version. The ledger gains `rev` (+1 a write), `saved` (when, ISO 8601 local) and `trail`: `[rev, digest]` of the last 300 versions and of every Finish (`[rev, digest, "finish"]`, kept for good), the current one last; the digest is the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the ledger's JSON without `trail`, keys sorted, compact (`ledger.digest`, `ledger.version`; rev 0 for a ledger written before). The version read is put on the trail first when it is not there (written by an older engine, which leaves `rev` alone, or before versions). `ledger.compare(a, b)` (pure): `same` (equal versions), `older` (a's version on b's trail), `newer`, else `diverged` (both changed since they parted, or no telling: the safe side). `ledger.edit` writes nothing when the block changed nothing (a derived id alone is no change), so a call that changes nothing makes no version.
- **`fork`** (new RPC; paper) → `{"id", "forked_from", "state"}`: a new random id, the old one in `paper.forked_from` (Keep Both, or a copy listed beside the folder it was copied from). CLI `fork P`.
- **The three facts in the ledger**: `paper.finished` (the day the advisor marked the paper finished, `YYYY-MM-DD`, or null), `paper.buckets` (Map Headings: {section key: standard section, or null for a column of its own name}, or null) and `paper.key_page` (Set as Key Page: `{"round", "page"}`, page 0-based, or null). A key absent means the ledger lacks the fact; present, even null, it is the ledger's.
  - **`edit_paper`** takes `finished?`, `buckets?` (`""` read as null) and `key_page?` besides `title?` and `student?`: each when the key is given, null clearing it. Refused, changing nothing: "finished is a day (YYYY-MM-DD) or null.", "buckets maps a section key to a standard section or null.", "key_page is {round, page} or null." (round ≥ 1, page ≥ 0, ints). CLI `edit-paper P [--finished DAY|none] [--buckets JSON|none] [--key-page ROUND:PAGE|none]`.
  - **`adopt_facts`** (new RPC; paper, `finished?`, `buckets?`, `key_page?`, checked as `edit_paper`'s) → `{"adopted": [names], "paper": {"id", "finished", "buckets", "key_page"}}`, no state: the app's papers.json values, sent once per paper. A fact the ledger lacks and the app gives (not null, not {}) is written; one the ledger has, even as null, is never overwritten; nothing to adopt writes nothing, so asking again changes nothing.
  - **`overview`**: each paper's `paper` gains `id`, `finished`, `buckets` and `key_page`, the ledger's, and while the ledger lacks one the app's (`buckets`, `key_pages` as sent; `finished` null). The ledger's `buckets` and `key_page` win over the app's for `students[].land` and the computed `key_page`. **`summaries`**: `paper` gains `id` and `finished`.
- **Rounds** gain `source_name` (the source's file or folder name at ingest: "easy-before-hard-r2.zip"), `build.tex` (the TeX that built it, the log's first line without "This is " and the run's date: "pdfTeX, Version 3.141592653-2.6-1.40.26 (TeX Live 2024) (preloaded format=pdflatex 2024.3.19)"; null without a log; `project.tex_version`) and, from round 2, `analysis_sha256` (below). Rounds ingested before have none.
- **Safe writes** (`disk.py`): `atomic_write(path, data)` writes a temporary file beside the file (`.<name>.<random>.tmp`), flushes it with `F_FULLFSYNC` (`fcntl`; macOS's `fsync` leaves data in the drive's cache; `fsync` where the file system has no `F_FULLFSYNC`), renames it over the file and syncs the folder; a failure (a full disk) leaves the file as it was and no temporary file; a file keeps its permissions. `disk.replace(tmp, path)` does the same for a file already written (final.pdf, rendered to `final.pdf.tmp`). Every engine write of a primary file uses one: the ledger, analysis.json, final.pdf, email.txt, the round checksums and the copies in .history. Caches (prose.json, contexts.json, analysis.cache.json, prose.failed) are renamed into place without the flush.
- **analysis.json is primary, in step with the ledger** (`analysis.py`). Ingest writes it (`analysis.write`) and the round records its SHA-256 in `analysis_sha256`. A later write under the lock (Reopen and its Undo, re-placing a comment: `_reattach`, `_unattach`) is staged as `rN/analysis.json.new` (`analysis.stage`, the round recording the new checksum), the ledger is saved, then it is swapped in (`analysis.settle`); a call that fails removes what it staged. After a crash, the next locked call (and `state`, which takes the lock only then, as for `rN.old`) swaps a staged file in when the ledger records its checksum and removes it otherwise. `analysis.read(paper, n, staged)` reads it as written.
  - **Caches move out**: what `state` works out for the changes (`section`, `context`, `float`, `about`) and what `prose` finds (`hashes`, `fresh`) go to `rN/analysis.cache.json`, `{"schema": 1, "analysis": SHA-256 of analysis.json, "changes": {change id: {key: value}}}`, used only while analysis.json has that checksum (`analysis.store_cache`); `analysis.changes(paper, n)` (state, overview, render, summaries) merges it in. So viewing never rewrites a primary file, and a finished round's checksums hold. An analysis.json that holds such keys from before keeps them; the cache's win.
- **Ledger history** (`history.py`): `<paper>/.history/<UTC yyyymmdd-hhmmss>-<rev>-<kind>.json`, a copy of the ledger, beside it `<same>.r<N>-analysis.json`, the open round's analysis.json then. Kinds: `ingest`, `finish`, `reopen` (the version after, `ledger.edit`'s `keep`), `edit` (the version before any other write, when the newest copy is over 10 minutes old or there is none), `replaced` (the version `put_back_ledger` replaced, as its bytes were, even unreadable). Kept: the 30 newest of the last day, the newest of each local day for 30 days, every `finish` and `replaced` copy for good (`history.prune`, after each copy).
  - **`history`** (new RPC; paper) → `{"versions": [{"name", "time", "rev", "kind", "comments", "rounds"}]}`, newest first, no state; `time` is when the version was saved (else copied), `comments`/`rounds` counts, null for a copy that cannot be read. Works when the ledger cannot be read. CLI `history P`.
  - **`put_back_ledger`** (new RPC; paper, `version`: a name from `history`) → {state}: the version as a new version, `rev` past the replaced one's and every kept copy's, the replaced one on its trail and copied as `replaced`, so an older export never looks newer; the paper keeps its id; the version's analysis.json of its open round comes back with it (staged and swapped like Reopen's). Works when the ledger cannot be read. Refused, changing nothing: "This version does not have Round 3, which is on disk: putting it back would lose it." (an `rN` folder the version does not name), "In this version Round 2 is still open, but it has been finished since: putting it back would undo that." (closed in the ledger, or with a round checksum file on disk), "This version is from before Round 2 was started again with a new source: putting it back would not match that round's files." (a round the ledger has with another `created`: re-ingested since), "There is no saved version …" (a name not of `history`'s form, so no path), "This saved version cannot be read, so it cannot be put back.". CLI `put-back P VERSION`.
- **No round folder is deleted because the ledger does not name it**: at ingest an `rN` the ledger does not name, an `rN.old` of a round it does not have, and the half-built `rN` of a killed re-ingest (whose `rN.old` comes back) move to `<paper>/.orphans/rN-<local yyyymmdd-hhmmss>` (`-2`… when taken). Re-ingesting an open round without comments still replaces it, and a failed ingest still removes the round it was building. The round it replaces waits in `rN.old-<12 hex digits of its ledger record's SHA-256>` until the ledger names the new one, and is deleted after that save (under the lock); after a kill, the next locked call (or `state`) puts it back while the ledger still has that record (the half-built round to `.orphans`), and deletes it once the ledger has another, so a round half deleted never comes back as the one the ledger describes. A plain `rN.old` (an engine before this) comes back as before.
- **Round checksums**: Finish writes `rN/manifest-sha256.txt` before closing the round, in shasum's format (`<SHA-256>  <path>`, paths relative to the round's folder, sorted; a name with a backslash or a line break escaped as shasum does; read back with only `\n` ending a line, so a name may hold `\r`, a form feed or U+2028), over the round's primary files: every file of `src`, `build/<stem>.pdf`, `.synctex.gz`, `.log`, `.bbl`, analysis.json, final.pdf and email.txt, those that exist (`disk.round_files`); `shasum -a 256 -c manifest-sha256.txt` in the round's folder checks it without the app. Rounds finished before have none.
- **`check`** (new RPC; `papers`: [path]) → `{"papers": [{"path", "problems": [Problem]} | {"path", "error"}]}`, in the order given, no state: read only, no lock, not even `.lock` created, nothing written, no file iCloud removed read (that would download it); a problem is reported, never repaired (`check.py`). Problem = `{"code", "message", "round": n | null, "files": [paths relative to the folder; ledger.json for a comment's]}`. Codes: `missing_folder`; `no_ledger`, `ledger_unreadable`, `ledger_too_new` (the ledger's own checks are then skipped); `source_missing` (rN/src missing or empty), `build_pdf_missing` (named, missing or empty), `build_pdf_damaged` (cannot be opened), `page_count` (not the ledger's `build.pages`), `final_pdf_missing` (a closed round's); `round_changed` (a finished round failing its checksums: files changed or missing, or extra in src); `analysis_out_of_step` (analysis.json is not the one `analysis_sha256` names, while nothing is staged); `duplicate_ids`, `ids_beyond_last_seq`; `path_outside_round` (a build path not under its `rN/`, or an anchor's file absolute or with ".."); `leftovers` (temporary files, `rN.old`, a staged analysis.json, over 10 minutes old, in the folder, its rounds or .history); `orphans` (.orphans not empty); `conflict_copies` (`ledger 2.json`, "ledger (conflicted copy).json"); `evicted` (files iCloud removed from this Mac: a `.name.icloud` placeholder or a dataless file; such a round's checksums are not read). Messages are the engine's plain words; the app shows its own on the paper's row. CLI `check-folder P [P…]` (`check` is the CLI's name for `check_change`).
- **Tests** (synthetic, temporary folders): `tests/test_data_safety.py`: ids (random, derived and not written by viewing, shared by a byte copy, not by another source made in the same second, stored at the first write, `fork`); versions (rev, trail, same/older/newer, diverged copies, an older engine's write diverged and then on the trail, rev 0, 300 and every Finish, no version for a call that changes nothing); the three facts (`adopt_facts` once and never over the ledger's, `edit_paper` set and clear, refusals for both leaving the ledger as it was, `summaries`, the overview's app fallback and the ledger winning for `land` and the key page); `atomic_write` with `F_FULLFSYNC`, permissions kept, a full disk leaving the file and no temporary file, a failed ledger write; analysis.json killed before and after the ledger's save, a failed call, the cache only for its analysis.json; history (the first `edit` copy, none within 10 minutes, one after, the limits with every kind, analysis copies pruned with theirs); `put_back_ledger` (in step with its analysis.json, older than the result, the replaced copy kept, each refusal, bad names, an unreadable ledger put right and kept as it was); `.orphans` from recovery and from ingest; a re-ingest killed before and after the ledger's save (never half a round back); Put Back refused after a round was started again; round checksums of any file name; `build.tex` and `source_name`; the round checksums (the files listed and not, `shasum -c`, state, prose and the next ingest leaving a finished round intact); `check` finding each code on copies of a finished paper, every folder's files and times the same before and after, and the RPC and CLI; the CLI's `edit-paper`, `history`, `put-back` and `fork`. Existing tests now expect `.history` in a folder listing, the half-built round and a stray `r2.old` in `.orphans` (`test_project.py`), the change details and block hashes in analysis.cache.json with analysis.json unchanged (`test_synctex_parts.py`, `test_prose.py`), and `id`/`finished` in `summaries` and `state.paper` (`test_cli_round1.py`, `test_rounds_core.py`, `test_overview.py`).

Export and import files, 2026-10-22 (`design/data/DATA.md` §3, §5.2 and §5.4, with the advisor's decisions and cuts of its §0: no "PDFs and comments, to send to someone" kind, no Repair Mine). Additive: an app before it never asks; an engine before it replies "Unknown method 'archive_write'." The ledger is unchanged. Where §7 ("requests run one at a time") or the amendment "Export" ("everything else of Data & Storage is the app's own, with `ditto`") conflicts, this wins: the engine writes and reads export files (`archive.py`, stdlib `zipfile`), still never writing into a review folder but by renaming it whole.
