- **The file** (backup.json schema 2): one plain .zip holding one folder named as the file (its name made safe), with `README.txt` (what it is, how to import it, and `shasum -a 256 -c manifest-sha256.txt` to check it without the app), `backup.json`, `manifest-sha256.txt` (every other file's SHA-256 in shasum's format, `disk.manifest_line`), `students/<student>/<folder>/…` (each review folder whole, `.history` and `.orphans` included, not `.lock` or links, nor a cache's temporary file renamed while it is written; empty folders kept; a file over 1 MB that would deflate more than 100× stored, not deflated, so the file never fails its own limits; permissions and times kept; student and folder names made safe as the app's `DataFiles.name` and "… 2" when two would be one on a Mac), and `app/tags.json`, `app/drafts/<id>.json` and `app/settings.json` (only with scope `everything`), each the JSON the app gave.
  - **backup.json**: `{"schema": 2, "app": "Paperole" (the format's name; made_by's `variant` is "Paperole" or "Paperole Dev"; README.txt shows the app's name. Until 2026-10-27 the app was PaperCollab and wrote "PaperCollab", "PaperCollab Dev": read forever, `archive.FORMATS`, `AppVariant.readsFormat`), "kind": "export"|"automatic", "scope": "everything"|"chosen", "created", "made_by": {"variant", "install", "app", "engine"}, "papers": [{"folder" (relative to the file's folder), "id", "title", "student", "rounds", "comments", "ledger": {"rev", "sha256" (of ledger.json's bytes)}, "bytes", "draft" (a path or null), "was_at" (the folder's path as listed)}], "missing": [{"path", "title", "student", "why"}], "tags" (how many, or null), "manifest": {"sha256" (of manifest-sha256.txt), "files"}}`. The ledger's title and student win over the app's; a paper whose ledger cannot be read is still exported (id, rounds, comments and rev null) and refused at import.
- **`archive_write`** (new RPC; `papers`: [{path, title?, student?}], `dest` (the .zip's path), `scope?` (`chosen`), `kind?` (`export`), `tags?`, `drafts?` ({paper path as given: its drafts file's JSON}), `settings?` (refused unless scope is `everything`), `made_by?` ({variant, install, app})) → `{"path", "bytes", "backup": backup.json}`, no state. Each paper's files are streamed in under its lock (`ledger.locked`: an edit meanwhile waits) and hashed as they go, into a hidden temporary file beside `dest`, which is read back against every checksum and only then renamed to `dest` (replacing a file there, as the save panel agreed) with `disk.replace`; kind `automatic` never replaces a file: "… 2.zip" instead (another Mac backing up into the folder meanwhile), the reply's `path` naming it; a failure, a cancel or a refusal leaves nothing. It starts only with free space of the folders' size × 1.2 + 5 GB (decimal) on the destination's disk and stops below 2 GB ("There is not enough free space on the disk of “…”: this needs about 5.3 GB free (the files and 5 GB to spare), and 3.1 GB is." / "Stopped: less than 2 GB is free on the disk of “…”. Nothing was written."). A listed folder that is not there, or holds no ledger, is left out and named in `missing`; the same folder twice is written once; a `dest` inside a review folder is refused. Progress: "Writing “<title>” (1 of 3)…" per paper, then "Checking…". CLI `archive-write DEST PAPER… [--scope everything|chosen] [--kind export|automatic]`.
- **`cancel`** (new; `params`: {"id": a request's id}) → `{"cancelling": true|false}` (whether that request was waiting or running). rpc.py now reads stdin on a thread of its own: `cancel` is answered at once, while another request runs; every other request still runs one at a time, in order. A request asked to stop sees it through `progress.cancelled()` (the CLI's never is); `archive_write`, `archive_check` and `archive_unpack` stop at the next block and reply with the error "Cancelled. Nothing was written."; other methods run to the end. A request cancelled while it waits stops as soon as it starts.
- **`archive_check`** (new RPC; `path`: a file or folder, `papers?`: the app's list [{path, id?}] (each folder's id is its ledger's while that reads, else the one given: a folder gone, or a ledger that cannot be read; places are compared with their links resolved, so "/tmp/…" is "/private/tmp/…")) → read only, nothing unpacked, no state: `{"kind": "export"|"automatic"|"backup"|"folders", "zip", "schema" (2, 1 or null), "created", "made_by", "scope", "intact", "checked", "problems": [{"file", "problem": "damaged"|"missing"|"extra"}] (at most 100, relative to the file's folder), "missing" (backup.json's), "tags", "settings" (the app's files' JSON, null when absent or not intact), "papers": [Paper]}`. `kind` `backup` is a schema-1 backup (the app's Back Up… before this); `folders` a .zip or folder without backup.json, whose review folders (a ledger.json at most three levels down, the shallowest, not in `__MACOSX` or "Replaced by Import") are read. `intact`: no file damaged, missing or extra and every paper importable and passing the check; `checked`: every file was checked against the file's checksums (schema 2) rather than only each entry's CRC.
  - **Paper** = `{"folder" (as backup.json names it, or the path inside the .zip, "" for a .zip that is the folder itself; for a folder on disk its absolute path), "in_place" (a folder on disk: listed where it is, never unpacked), "id", "title", "student", "was_at", "status", "reason", "listed" (the list's path it matched, or null), "found" (status `new` in a file only: `was_at` when the folder there is this very paper and the list does not name it, the list lost or the paper removed from it, else null; the app then leaves it unticked and adds it only as a copy), "this", "yours" (the facts the preview shows: `{"title", "student", "round", "open", "comments", "saved"}`; `yours` null unless matched with a folder that is there), "problems" (`check`'s problems of this copy: in a file, the ledger's own and each round's, from the entries; a PDF is not opened), "yours_problems" (`check` of your copy, when matched), "draft" (its drafts file's JSON, or null), "facts" (schema 1 only: finished, buckets and key_page as its papers.json entry had them, for `adopt_facts`)}`.
  - **status**, in this order: `cannot_import` with `reason` (a file of it damaged, missing or extra: "A file in it is damaged: r1/final.pdf."; the file's checksums damaged; no ledger.json; a ledger over 32 MB, unreadable or written by a newer Paperole; backup.json naming its folder badly); `different_paper` (a second paper with one id in one file: "Another paper in this file has the same id."; or the listed folder with its id is another paper: not made, or round 1 not received, at the same moment; a title or a name may have been corrected since); `new` (no listed paper has its id, nor, when the list gives no id, its `was_at`); `put_back` (listed, its folder not there; the app tells an unplugged drive from a missing folder); `yours_damaged` (your copy fails the check, this one does not); else `ledger.compare(this, yours)`: `same`, `older` (this copy), `newer`, `diverged` (also when yours cannot be read).
  - **Refused** (EngineError, for the whole file): not a .zip; a link; an absolute name, an empty, `.` or `..` part (also `..` between backslashes); NUL in a name (zipfile cuts the name there: `orig_filename` is read); two names equal after NFC and case folding, one name twice, a file and a folder of one name; a password-protected entry; more than 200,000 entries; an entry over 1 MB compressed more than 100×, or all entries together more than 100× the file; more than one backup.json; no review folder; backup.json not Paperole's (nor PaperCollab's), of a kind for reading ("… holds PDFs and comments for reading; it cannot be imported."), of a newer schema ("… was made by a newer Paperole. Update Paperole to import it."). An entry yielding more or fewer bytes than it declares, or failing its CRC, is damaged; a line of manifest-sha256.txt that cannot be read makes every paper `cannot_import`, as a changed checksums file does. `__MACOSX` is never read. Names not flagged UTF-8 are read as UTF-8 when they decode.
  - CLI `archive-check PATH [--listed PAPER…]`.
- **`archive_info`** (new RPC; `paths`: [file]) → `{"files": [{"path", "backup": backup.json as written, or null for a .zip without one} | {"path", "error"}]}`, in the order given, read only, no state: what each file says it holds, from its backup.json alone (each entry still checked at the boundary as `archive_check`'s; nothing else read or checked, so it is quick on a folder of backups). The app's automatic backups use it (`mac/UX.md` §28): which files of the backup folder are this app's own to rotate, and the newest one holding a paper. A file that is not there, not a .zip, unsafe, for reading or of a newer schema is an `error` of its own.
- **`archive_unpack`** (new RPC; `path`, `paper` (its `folder` from `archive_check`), `dest` (a folder), `fork?`, `copy?`) → `{"path", "id", "problems": check of the new folder}`, no state. Refused when `dest` is a review folder or inside one (a ledger.json Paperole reads in it or above it: never into a round's `src`). Only that paper, with the same refusals, after its files are checked as `archive_check` checks them (a paper `archive_check` calls `cannot_import` is refused with its reason); written into a hidden folder in `dest` (`.Paperole-import-…`; `.PaperCollab-import-…` before 2026-10-27, swept too), each file created anew (never through a link), synced, given its permissions (at most 0755) and time, then every file read back from the disk against its checksum, and renamed into a new folder named as its folder in the file (made safe; "… 2" when taken, the name claimed by making it). Free space as `archive_write`'s, for its size. `fork`: a new id (`forked_from` the file's; a second copy of a listed paper, or `different_paper`); `copy`: Keep Both, a new id and " (copy)" after the title, saved as one version of the new folder's ledger before it is named. A failure or cancel leaves nothing. CLI `archive-unpack PATH PAPER DEST [--fork] [--copy]`.
- **`swap_in`** (new RPC; `incoming`, `current`: two review folders, neither inside the other, `incoming` on `current`'s disk) → `{"path" (current's), "replaced"}`, no state: Use This Copy. Under the current paper's lock, `current` is renamed to `<its parent>/Replaced by Import/<its name> <YYYY-MM-DD>` ("… 2" when taken), then `incoming` to `current`; when the second rename fails the first is undone (and a "Replaced by Import" made for it removed), and the error is the rename's. The app closes the paper and saves any pending edit first; the app's automatic backup before it is the app's. CLI `swap-in INCOMING CURRENT`.
- **Keep Both** is `archive_unpack` with `copy`; Use This Copy is `archive_unpack` into the current folder's parent, then `swap_in`; Put back and Add are `archive_unpack` (into the old place's parent, or the chosen folder); a folder listed in place needs no unpacking (`fork` for a second copy).
- **The app** (`mac/UX.md` §28): asks `archive_check` with every listed paper and the id its list keeps; Export… and the automatic backups are `archive_write` (kind `export` or `automatic`, scope `everything` when every paper is in it), Cancel is `cancel`; Add and Put back are `archive_unpack` (into the chosen folder, or the old place's parent and then renamed into the old place when the file named its folder otherwise), Keep Both is `archive_unpack` with `copy` (a folder on disk: `fork`, then `edit_paper` of its title), Use This Copy is `archive_unpack` beside yours then `swap_in`, and a `found` paper's Keep Mine lists that folder where it is (nothing unpacked); the daily check is `check`; Restore from Last Backup… finds the newest of its own backups holding the paper with `archive_info`; of two listed folders with one id the later is given its own with `fork`.
- **Shared code**: `disk.manifest_line` and `disk.parse_manifest` (the round checksums use them too, unchanged), `check.read_ledger(raw)` (the check's reading of a ledger's bytes), `ledger.derived_id(paper, lg, files=None)` (the names and sizes of r1/src given rather than read, for a ledger inside a file).
- **Tests** (synthetic, temporary folders): `tests/test_archive.py`: round trips of everything (four papers of three students: the four-round kelp paper, a forked copy of it, the fixture paper with a comment, a hand-made one), one student and one paper, each folder removed and put back from the file: every file's bytes and permissions, each round's `state` and the `overview` the same, progress, backup.json, tags, settings and drafts; `shasum -a 256 -c` on the unzipped file; what `archive_write` leaves out and refuses; each status (new, same, older, newer, diverged, put back by id and by place, yours damaged, a different paper, one id twice in a file, an unreadable and a newer ledger); Keep Both, a fork and taken names; refusals (a link, a folder link, `..`, `..\`, `.` and empty parts, an absolute name, NUL, case and NFC twins, a file and a folder of one name, a zip bomb, all entries together a bomb, a password-protected entry, too many entries); a file that deflates too well stored, odd file names and a vanished temporary file reading back, `archive_unpack` refused inside a review folder; a damaged line of checksums; damage (a flipped byte, a size larger and smaller than the entry, a missing, an extra and a changed file, the checksums file changed, a ledger over the limit); files not imported (for reading, schema 3, not Paperole's, no review folder, not a .zip); a schema-1 backup (put back, its facts and draft); a folder zipped as Finder does (`ditto -c -k --sequesterRsrc --keepParent`, with `__MACOSX`), a review folder and a folder searched three levels down (hidden and Replaced by Import skipped, deeper not found); `swap_in` and its rollback; cancel and low free space leaving nothing (`write`, `unpack`); an automatic backup never written over a file; a listed paper whose ledger cannot be read matched by the app's id, a readable ledger's own id winning over the app's; a listed place spelt through a link; what files say they hold (`archive_info`: an automatic backup's backup.json, a folder's .zip without one, a link, a file for reading, a file not there or not a .zip); RPC with `cancel` of a waiting export; CLI. Also checked by hand on the three synthetic reviews of `samples/promo-demo/tools/build_reviews.py` through the CLI: written, found in place, lost, checked, put back: every file and permission and each `state` the same.

Code review fixes, 2026-10-24 (`design/review/2026-10-24/REVIEW.md`: the engine batches B1–B5 and B16, and the review of the whole diff; `mac/UX.md` §30 for the app). `PARSER` 8 → 9, so every round's prose.json is found again once. Ledger schema stays 1; the one new key, `build.rulers`, is additive: an app before it ignores it, and `state` works it out for rounds ingested before. Where §5, §7, §9 or an earlier amendment conflicts, this wins.
- **Snapshot** (`project.snapshot`):
  - Every copied folder and file name is in NFC, for a folder source and for a zip.
  - A zip member without the UTF-8 flag (0x800) is read as UTF-8 when its cp437 round trip decodes (Finder's Compress and Info-ZIP write such names), else kept as it is; `\` reads as `/`, so a member ending in `\` is a folder. `received()` uses the same members (`project._members`). The unsafe-path message names the recovered name.
  - A zip's single top-level folder is unwrapped when no .tex file is beside it; each other top-level file (a README, the PDF) is skipped with the warning "Skipped <name> beside the source folder". `received()` counts only the members the snapshot copies, and gives None for a zip with an unsafe path.
  - The snapshot's own warnings ("Skipped the symbolic link refs.bib") come first in `build.warnings`, before the log's, so the round keeps them (§4).
- **Detect**: a given main with a folder part is refused: "The main file must be at the top of the source, not in a folder: paper/main.tex." (latexmk runs in src; a ledger that already has one still builds its later rounds, since `detect` is not called then). fontspec, xeCJK, polyglossia and unicode-math are also looked for in the files main's preamble reads: the text before `\begin{document}`, comments removed, then the files it reads through `texdiff.INCLUDE` and `include_file`, two levels deep, files under src only. Local `.sty` files and `\RequirePackage` are not read.
- **Compile**: when the first latexmk call did not time out, left a PDF and a log, its log has an error, and it took at most 150 s (TIMEOUT/2), the same command runs once more with `-g` in the time left, with the progress line "Compiling again after LaTeX errors…" (with `-f`, latexmk stops after a LaTeX run with errors, before the runs that resolve `\cite` and `\ref`, so every citation printed [?]). The 300 s timeout covers both calls. An error's continuation lines (`(pkg) …` or indented, up to a blank line) are joined into its message, as a warning's are.
- **File names**: source files are keyed by their path relative to src as spelled on disk. A name as synctex records it (the `\input`'s spelling), an `\input` name and an older ledger's anchor `file` are matched to it in any case and Unicode form (NFC and case folding: `texdiff.name_key`, `spellings`, `find_file`), as macOS opens it: in `read_files` (`old_read`, `new_read`, `unread`), `include_file` (so reads, includes, dropped files, `project`'s preamble scan and cards resolve such an `\input`), `anchor._inputs` and `to_pdf`, `comments.locate` (new anchors store the on-disk spelling) and prose.json's block `file` (`_Reader.target`; `block_of` and its index match by the key, so an anchor in the older spelling still finds its paragraph). carry and shadows retry an anchor's file in the old tree by the key. Two files whose names differ only in case or Unicode form, one in each tree, are one renamed file (`renamed`), whatever their tokens, so a round snapshotted with Finder's NFD names before this compares with the next round's NFC names. Block hashes do not hold the file name, so read state is kept.
- **Source diff** (`texdiff`):
  - **Comments** are blanked by one scanner, shared with notes (`texdiff.COMMENT`; `notes.SCAN` is gone): a `%` is no comment inside verbatim, Verbatim, BVerbatim, LVerbatim, lstlisting and minted bodies, `\verb`, `\lstinline`, the braced argument of `\url`, `\nolinkurl` and `\path`, `\href`'s address, or as `\%`. So an edit after a URL that holds `%` is a change, and `% R1-03` inside a URL is no student's note. `blank_comments` is cached (16 texts).
  - A UTF-8 byte order mark (U+FEFF) is no token, so a BOM an editor adds is no change.
  - **Moves** are of printed text only: delete and replace pieces count only in files the old build read (or when that is not known, or for the build folder's `.bbl`), insert and replace pieces only in files the new build reads. A paragraph cut from the paper into a scrap file the build does not read is a delete, and one pasted from such a file an insert. The matcher is a seed-and-extend 8-gram index with `find_longest_match`'s tie-break: the same hunks, about 9 times faster on reordered sections (4.9 s to 0.5 s on 34k tokens).
  - Deleting a whole file that the old build did not read is no change.
  - **`\import{folder}{name}` and `\subimport{folder}{name}`** are includes (groups 3–4 of `INCLUDE`), the folder and name joined relative to src, as prose reads them. Taking such a line out makes its file's comments gone where it was, with the delete attached; the command's arguments print no letters for `to_pdf`'s matching. A nested `\subimport` is resolved from src, not from the importing file's folder.
- **Carry**: `anchor._search` compares the paragraph breaks inside a quote, so a quote across a blank line is found verbatim, also in the searches without fuzz (dropped files, restored comments, shadows).
- **Paragraphs** (prose.json, `PARSER` 9). This replaces the amendment "Prose view and reading"'s block description where they differ:
  - **Block fields**: `role` and `since` are gone, an `item` has no `depth` or `label`, and `number` is for headings only (null for math). Only `figure`, `table`, `math`, `code` and `picture` blocks have `box` and `clip`, with `parts` `[box]`; a text block's `box` is null (the example's paragraph reads `"box": null` and has no `role`), and the title has `parts` [] and `box` null. A float or formula that `\input`s files gains `"inputs": [file…]`, only when there are some.
  - **Headings**: `\part` and `\chapter` are headings. When a file read uses `\chapter`, `level` is 1 for a chapter, 2 section, 3 subsection, 4 subsubsection and 5 paragraph, numbered within the chapter (1, 1.1, 1.1.1; subsubsection unnumbered; A, A.1 after `\appendix`); otherwise as before. `\part` is level 1 with number null (a Roman numeral would read as an appendix letter) and changes no counter. So a thesis's overview sections are its parts and chapters. A run-in heading's extra line follows `\paragraph`, not level 4.
  - **`comments[].paragraph`** (`block_of`): when no block of the anchor's file overlaps the anchor, the first block that `\input`s that file (a float's tabular or TikZ file).
  - **Change ids**: in a lazy build whose diff does not match analysis.json, the ids are placeholders "?01", "?02", … (they were null) and `changes` is {}; wholly added blocks stay fresh and moved ones moved.
  - **A round n−1 that cannot be read**: `rN−1/prose.failed` is written and round n is built without diff marks (against null, previous null), as round 1 is, instead of failing on every call.
  - **Splitting**: `\\[2pt]` and `\verb|…|` no longer open a display in a text line (a `\[` there swallowed every paragraph up to the next `\]`). A line holding only a command not known to print nothing (an unknown package command such as `\gls{llm}`, `\SI{5}{ms}` or `\todo`; `\nolinkurl` and `\path`) continues an open paragraph; the known ones (`prose.SILENT`: hidden, spacing, dimension and definition commands, `\par`) still end it. ICML's `\icmltitle`, `\icmltitlerunning`, `\icmlsetsymbol`, `\icmlauthor`, `\icmlaffiliation`, `\icmlcorrespondingauthor`, `\icmlkeywords` and `\printAffiliationsAndNotice` are front matter that is not read (BACKLOG D4).
  - **Parts**: of the runs that continue a paragraph in the next column or page, the one holding the block's own text wins over one of only its footnotes, right after the same-page test.
  - **Read state**: blocks this splitting changes (a span after `\\[2pt]`, a paragraph joined across a `\gls` line, chapter titles, ICML front matter) get new hashes, so they show unread once; the others keep theirs. When an open round's source meets one of the new diff rules above, its re-made diff no longer matches analysis.json, so that round's prose has placeholder ids and no `changes`.
- **The analysis cache**: `analysis.settle` keeps the cache's entries for the new analysis.json when it swaps a staged one in (a re-stage within a round changes only `attached_to` and `unrequested`), so Reopen and its Undo, a re-anchor or Put Back no longer drop `hashes` and `fresh` until the next prose build.
- **Final PDF** (amendment "Final PDF in the margin style"):
  - **Placing** (until the amendment "Notes whole, the band under the paper"): after the smaller sizes and the cut to 3 lines, then 1, the items may stand closer together at the smallest size (3 pt instead of 6). When even that does not fit (about two dozen items on one side), the side's `your change` items are left out (the summary lists every change no comment asked for) and the sizes are tried again on the comments; when that fails too, each comment keeps only its heading line (`R1-01 · new`: no note, no deleted words), as close as it must. They overlap only beyond about 39 open comments on one side. No item is placed above y 44 or within 24 pt of the page's foot (they were placed above the page).
  - **The summary**: a row is at most a page high. A longer text (about 2,400 characters) is cut with …: the main text first, then at least two lines of the muted text, and a muted `(full text in the email)` when the main text was cut. email.txt keeps it whole.
  - A comment followed up in round N without a note shows the latest earlier follow-up's note, muted, in its margin box and summary row, as the email did.
  - **The outline**: the paper's own items without a page (a URI bookmark) are left out and their children move up; the first item kept is level 1 and each is at most one level below the one before, so the paper's items never nest under `Comments (k)` (such a bookmark with grandchildren made Finish fail with "Internal error: ValueError").
  - The margin's header is fitted once per PDF, and the layout is the one stored at ingest (`build.text_x`, `columns`, through `state._build_layout`).
- **email.txt** (§9): every non-blank line of an entry is indented by two spaces: the note, the follow-up line (`Round N: …`), an earlier follow-up (`Round K (follow-up): …`) and a revived comment's follow-up.
- **Tags**: `retag` onto a tag a comment already holds drops `from`'s entry, so the comment keeps its own `into` with its `by` and `at`. A tag id is matched whole: an id with a trailing line break is refused in `tag`, `retag`, `names`, `accepted` and `tag_order`, and ignored when read from a ledger (`tags.ids`).
- **Ledger, API and data** (amendments "Data foundations" and "Export and import files"):
  - **`paper.finished`**: an ingest that adds a round sets it to null when it held a day (a ledger without the key is left alone). summaries and the overview then report null, the app clears its copy, and `adopt_facts` never brings the old day back, since the ledger has the key.
  - **Hidden files**: a name with a path part that starts with `.` (Finder's `.DS_Store`; the snapshot never copies one) is left out of `disk.round_files` (src), of a finished round's `extra`, and of the entries an older manifest lists when they are compared; `archive._rounds` skips them the same way. So looking at a finished round in Finder no longer makes it `round_changed`. `ledger.derived_id` still counts them (BACKLOG).
  - **Recovery**: every locked call moves an `rN` the ledger does not name (an ingest stopped hard) to `.orphans/rN-<time>`, and `state` does too when it finds one, taking the lock then; ingest no longer does it itself. A ledger.json a sync service swapped for an older one therefore has its newer `rN` moved to `.orphans`, kept, never deleted.
  - **`put_back_ledger`**: the version is read as `check.read_ledger` reads a ledger (each round an object with an int `n`, else "This saved version cannot be read, so it cannot be put back."). Each round's `last_seq` is the larger of the version's and the current ledger's, so ids given out since are never given again. "This version does not have Round N, which is on disk" applies to the rounds the current ledger names; an `rN` it does not name went to `.orphans` first.
  - **Errors**: a ledger.json that is not valid JSON or UTF-8 replies "ledger.json cannot be read. Put back an earlier ledger." (it was "Internal error: JSONDecodeError …"). An editing call on a missing folder, or one without ledger.json, replies "<path> is not a Paperole paper folder (no ledger.json)." and makes no `.lock`; `put_back_ledger` still takes the lock there.
  - **`check`**: a comment whose `rounds` is not an object is `ledger_unreadable`: "The ledger cannot be read: the rounds of R2-01 are damaged." (files [ledger.json]). Any failure checking one folder gives that folder `{path, error}`; before, only OSError did.
  - **History**: "the newest of each local day for 30 days" now includes days under 24 h old, so a busy day of 30 or more copies no longer drops the day before's last one.
  - **Archives**: `archive_write` refuses a destination inside any review folder, chosen or not: "Save the file outside the review folders: “…” is in one." `archive_check` of a folder honours `cancel` between review folders, and checks a folder listed where it is once (its `yours_problems` are its `problems`). `archive_unpack` hashes only that paper's entries; the manifest and its pin are still checked. `swap_in` needs ledger.json only in `incoming`; `current` only has to be a folder ("There is no folder <path>."), so Use This Copy and Restore can repair a folder that lost its ledger.
  - `state.unchecked` is the one "edit to check" rule, used by `summary` and the overview.
- **`build.rulers`**, beside `text_x` and `columns`: written at ingest by `comments.layout`, which computes the three in one pass, and given in state's `round.build`. For each page of the build PDF, `[left, right]`: each side the bounds `[x0, y0, x1, y1]` (§3's coordinates, rounded to 0.01) of the page's margin line numbers on that side of `text_x` (`comments.margin_numbers`), or null when that side has none; null for a page whose `text_x` is null; `[]` for a failed build. Margin numbers lie wholly left or right of every other word, so a page's text extent is `text_x` joined with the rulers' x-extents. For a round ingested before, `state` works it out from the PDF once per engine process (about 5–6 ms a page), as for `text_x`. State's `round.build` keys: `status`, `pdf`, `pages`, `text_x`, `columns`, `rulers`, `warnings`, `errors`, `log_tail`. The app places id labels from them (`PDFController.labelBounds`), with PDFKit's `page.selection` only as the fallback for a page without an entry.
- **The app's side** (`mac/UX.md` §30): a Round 1 the engine refused is sent again from the "Start Round 1" sheet with `ingest`'s `main` (and no `engine` while Engine is Automatic, so detection still runs); Build Round N Again… is an `ingest` that replaces the open round under §6's rule; Export and Import send `archive_write`, `archive_check` and `archive_unpack` to an engine process of their own, ended after each job, while `fork`, `edit_paper`, `swap_in` and `export_comments` stay on the review's.
- **Code**: gone are `texdiff.include_arg`, anchor's copies of `CAPTION` and `_id_key`, `notes.SCAN`, `archive._now`, the guarded imports in api, `prose.item_label`, `_roman` and `BULLETS`. New: `texdiff.name_key`, `spellings` and `find_file`, `prose.SILENT` and `_newlines`, `disk.hidden`, `state.unchecked`, `comments._why_not_revivable` (Reopen's rule stated once, no change).
- **Tests** (synthetic, temporary folders): new or extended in test_project (real ditto and Info-ZIP zips, real latexmk builds with errors), test_texdiff (the new matcher against difflib's on random lists and on reordered papers), test_anchor (two-round ingests with a case mismatch, NFD, a scrap file, `\import`), test_notes, test_revive, test_prose, test_reading, test_final_pdf, test_tags, test_data_safety, test_archive, test_rpc, test_rounds_core and test_review_layouts. Full suite: 413 passed, 2 skipped (the private sample PDFs).

Resume a finished round, 2026-10-25 (the advisor's request of 2026-10-25: "after finishing I find more things to change"; `mac/UX.md` for the app). Additive: ledger schema stays 1, an app before it never asks, and an engine before it replies "Unknown method 'resume_round'." and reads the new round keys as unknown. Where §6 ("A closed round is frozen"), the amendments "Data foundations" (round checksums, history kinds, `put_back_ledger`) or "Export and import files" conflict, this wins. "Resume", not "Reopen": Reopen is a comment's (`revive`).
- **`resume_round`** (new RPC; paper) → {state}. CLI `resume-round P`. The latest round, finished, is open again as it was before its Finish, as far as the advisor can see: its comments, verdicts and their notes, Reopens, checked changes, tags and read state; then add, edit, answer, delete, Reopen, re-place and Finish as in any open round. It changes no source and builds nothing. Only the latest round, only while it is finished: once the next round is ingested, the round before it stays finished for good. Refused, changing nothing: "There is no round yet. Ingest the student's source first." and "Round 2 is open already: only a finished round can be resumed." (also when a later round exists, which is then the open latest one).
- **What Finish wrote is never deleted or overwritten** (the student may have it): `rN/final.pdf`, `rN/email.txt` and `rN/manifest-sha256.txt` move (one rename each, `disk.move`: never over a file, both folders synced) into **`rN/earlier/`**, named for that finish's local time from its `closed_at`: `final 2026-10-21 1530.pdf`, `email 2026-10-21 1530.txt`, `manifest-sha256 2026-10-21 1530.txt` (`… 1530 2.pdf` when a name is taken or named by an earlier finish, the same suffix for all three). The kept checksums still list the round's files as they were then (`final.pdf`, `email.txt`, src …), so they are a record, not a list to run `shasum -c` on in `earlier/`. Nothing is added to the email or final.pdf: the advisor writes their own words when sending. The next Finish writes `final.pdf`, `email.txt` and the checksums as usual, dated that day; `earlier/` is none of the round's files (`disk.round_files`, `verify_manifest`'s `extra` reads src only).
- **Round keys** (new, both optional): **`finishes`**: `[{"at": that finish's closed_at, "resumed": when it was resumed, "final", "email", "manifest": the kept files' paths relative to the paper ("r2/earlier/final 2026-10-21 1530.pdf"), null for a file that was not there}]`, oldest first; the round's own `closed`/`closed_at` are its latest finish's (false/null while resumed). **`marked_read`**: the hashes Finish marked read (`prose.close_read` now returns those not in the map before, sorted). Resume takes them off the map while they still have Finish's value (`prose.open_read`) and sets `read_through` to n − 1 (1 for round 1), as before Finish; so a paragraph left unread, or marked unread, has its bar again. A round finished by an engine before this has no `marked_read`: its paragraphs stay read. Resume removes the key, the next Finish writes it again, and an ingest of round n + 1 drops it from round n.
- **History**: the version before Resume (the round finished) is copied into `.history` as kind **`resume`** (`<UTC time>-<rev>-resume.json`), with that round's analysis.json beside it as an open round's (a re-placed or reopened comment of the resumed round writes a new one), kept for good like `finish` and `replaced` (`history.FOR_GOOD`); `history` lists it. The app labels it ("before a round was resumed").
- **Ingest** refuses to replace a resumed round (it would delete `rN/earlier/`): "Round 2 was finished before and resumed. Finish it again before ingesting a new round." The next round after it is ingested as after any finish (carry, shadows, notes, `read_through`).
- **State** (additive): `round.resumable` (this is the latest round and it is finished: show Resume Round N; false for an earlier round viewed; like `rounds` and `summary`, the paper's now), `round.finishes` (`[{"at", "resumed", "final", "email"}]`, absolute paths, null when the file is not there; [] for a round never resumed; for the Finish sheet's "Finished before on Oct 21" and Show in Finder). `summary` (also in `summaries` and the overview) gains `closed_at` (the latest round's, null while open) and `resumable` (the latest round is finished), for the round menu, the paper's context menu and the overview's More menu. The overview's `rounds[]` gain `finishes`, as in state. A resumed round is open everywhere: `summary.closed` false with its `to_judge` and `to_check` (your move again), the overview's round open and with you, `final_pdf` and `email` null until it is finished again. The round trips count to its latest finish.
- **Recovery**: a move that fails puts back those made. Resume moves the files before the ledger is saved; after a kill between the two, the next locked call (and `state`, which takes the lock only then) puts them back while the ledger still has the round finished: a file of it missing and exactly one kept file of that kind in `earlier/` that none of its `finishes` names (`api._unresumed`).
- **`put_back_ledger`**: the version from just before a Resume (`resume`), put back while that round is still open again, gets its files back from `earlier/` (after the save; after a kill, by the next locked call as above), and its analysis.json as it was finished when the resumed round changed it (the one kept with the copy, staged and swapped in with the ledger). Refused, changing nothing: "This version is from before Round 2 was resumed: putting it back would not match that round's files." (a kept file in `rN/earlier/` that the version's round does not name, which it cannot get back: the round was finished again since, or resumed twice since; or the round's analysis.json changed since and the version has none kept: a `finish` copy).
- **`check`**: an open round needs no `final.pdf` or checksums (as before), and files in `rN/earlier/` are never `round_changed`. New code **`earlier_changed`**: "Files kept from an earlier finish of Round 2 are missing or changed.", files: each kept file a finish names that is not there, a kept final.pdf or email.txt whose SHA-256 is not the one its kept checksums list for `final.pdf` or `email.txt`, a kept checksums file with a line that cannot be read, and any name outside `rN/earlier/` (never read). A file iCloud removed is not read. `check.kept_changed` (shared).
- **Export and import**: a review folder goes into a file whole, so `rN/earlier/` is in it and under the file's checksums; `archive._rounds` runs `kept_changed` on a copy inside a file, so Import shows `earlier_changed` there too; `archive_unpack` brings the files back byte for byte. README.txt gains "rN/earlier/ holds what an earlier finish of round N gave, named for its time, when the round was resumed."
- **Tests** (synthetic, temporary folders): `tests/test_resume.py`: the round as before Finish (state equal but for `finishes`, ledger records, read map with a paragraph marked unread and an edited one left unread, kept files byte for byte, the `resume` copy), editing in it, Finish again (new files dated that day, the kept ones unchanged, checksums, check), names in the same minute; the next round (`marked_read` dropped, refusal, round 2's view, the overview); Reopen and re-placing in a resumed round; refusals (no round, open, a later round, ingest replacing); a killed Resume put back by `state` and a failed move undone; Put Back of the `resume` copy (its analysis.json too) and the refusals once finished again; history pruning; check's `earlier_changed` (changed, missing, a name outside), export, import and a changed file in an export; CLI and RPC. `summary`'s new keys in test_cli_round1, test_rounds_core, test_project and test_rpc. Full suite: 423 passed, 2 skipped (the private sample PDFs).

Reference previews, 2026-10-30 (`design/pdf-reader/PLAN.md` §4.5, P0.3's engine half). Additive and read only: ledger schema stays 1, an app before it never asks, and an engine before it replies "Unknown method 'links'.", which the app reads as no table (a click goes there as before, with Back). Where §2 (the round's files) or the amendment "Prose view and reading" (the prose pass) is silent, this adds to them.
- **`links.py`** (new). `build(paper, lg, n, data)` writes **`rN/links.json`** from round n's build PDF (the PDF the app shows for every round, open or closed, so pages are 0-based into it, §3) and its prose.json `data`: a temporary file (named with the process id: two engine processes may build it at once), then `os.replace`. A cache outside the round's checksums, as prose.json is (`disk.round_files` lists neither); cached per process by path and modification time. **`LINKS`** (an int, 1) and **`prose.PARSER`** are stored; a file with other values, or one that is not valid JSON or lacks `links` and `targets`, is stale and built again.
  - **When**: ingest builds it right after prose.json (`api._prose`; so does `finish` when it builds a missing prose.json), and the `links` RPC when it is missing or stale; the `prose` RPC and the overview's second process do not. `build_quietly` never raises: an error leaves no table (the traceback on stderr; that process does not try again until the round's build PDF or prose.json changes), and a link or page that fails is left out alone ("links: 2 links left out after errors" on stderr).
- **`links.json`**: `{"schema": 1, "version": LINKS, "parser": PARSER, "round": n, "hyperref": bool, "links": [Link…], "targets": [Target…]}`. `hyperref` is true when the PDF has a link other than a URL's.
  - **Link**: `{"page": 0, "rects": [[x0, y0, x1, y1], …], "text": "(Chen et al., 2021;", "kind": "cite", "targets": [0, 1], "via": "pdf" | "source"}`, sorted by page, then by the first rect's top and left. `rects` are §3's (top-left, one per run of the printed words); `text` is the PDF words whose centre lies in them ("" for a footnote mark set inside a word); `kind` is the first target's; `targets` index `targets`, the clicked one first: a hyperref link's own target, then for a citation of several keys (`\cite{a,b,c}`) the others in the source's order; a source link's are its keys in order.
  - **Target**: `{"kind", "label", "key", "page", "box", "parts", "block", "at", "url", "note", "cited", "count"}`. `kind` is `figure`, `table`, `algorithm`, `equation`, `section`, `theorem`, `appendix`, `cite`, `footnote`, `url`, `missing` or `other`. `label` is the preview's title: "Figure 3", "Table 1", "Algorithm 1", "Equation (3)", "Section 3.1", "Appendix A", "Theorem 2" (Lemma, Definition, … from the label's type), "Footnote 1", a citation's "Chen et al., 2021" (natbib's short authors and year; "[4]" without them; its key without either), a URL's address, "Undefined reference" or "Undefined citation", else the printed words. `key` is the `\label` name or citation key (null for a destination no label names, a URL). `page` and `box` (§3) are the first of `parts` (`[[page, rect]]`), all null/[] when the target has no place (a URL, a missing one, a label in no block). `block` indexes prose.json's `blocks` (null when none). `at` is a hyperref destination's point `[x, y]` (top-left), else null. `url` is a URL's address. `note` is set for `missing`: "No \label{fig:old} in this round's source." or "No \bibitem{Nobody2020} in this round's references.". `cited` is the sorted pages of the links that cite it (a hyperref link its own target, a source link each of its keys) and `count` their number ("Cited 3×: p.2 · p.5").
  - **The target's place**: a figure, table, algorithm, formula, listing or picture: its block's `box` (the whole float with its caption; hyperref's point lies above the caption). A section or appendix: the heading's first part widened down to `LINES` (6) more text lines of its column. A theorem: the block's first line and 6 more, within its first part. A citation: its `bibitem` block's first part. A footnote, and an entry without a bibitem block (biblatex): the text lines from the destination's point (words whose top is at most `RAISED`, 3 pt, above it: the point is raised above its first line, so not the previous note's last line) down to the next destination of that kind on the page (the document's named destinations `cite.*` and `Hfootnote.*`, so an entry no link reaches still ends the one above it, and the links'), at most 8, in the point's column. Otherwise the block's first part.
- **Source 1, hyperref's links** (PyMuPDF's `page.get_links()`). Consecutive rects to one destination on a page, on the same line at most 30 pt apart, or on the next line (at most `MERGE`, 20 pt, down) when the first ends its line and the second starts its own (no word of their column after or before them), are one link: natbib's "(Chen et al.," and "2021;"; two references to one target on consecutive lines stay two. A named destination's point is PDF space (y up) and is turned to §3's; a page link's (`LINK_GOTO`) is top-left already; a URL is its own target, only for `http:`, `https:` and `mailto:` (`SCHEMES`): a link or `\url` to any other (`javascript:`, `file:`, a relative one) is left out, and the app still checks the scheme before it opens one. The target is found, in order: `cite.<key>` (biblatex `cite.0@<key>`) gives the key; an anchor the `.aux` gives a label (`\newlabel`'s fourth group, `figure.1` → `fig:curves`) gives that label's block (of several labels on one anchor, subcaption's `figure.caption.2` for a figure and its subfigures, the one whose `.aux` number the link prints, "2b", else the first); otherwise the block on the destination's page whose box or part holds the point (by its height; x only between blocks level with it) or starts at most `BELOW` (12 pt) below it, one of the destination's kind first. The kind comes from the destination's prefix (`figure`, `table`, `algorithm`, `equation`, `AMS`, `section`…`subparagraph`, `chapter`, `appendix`, `theorem`/`lemma`/…, `Hfootnote`, `Item`, `lstlisting`), then cleveref's type, then the label's prefix (`fig:`, `tab:`, `eq:`, `sec:`, `app:`, `thm:`, `lem:`, …), then the block's kind (a theorem destination is named after its counter, `theorem.2` for `\newtheorem{lemma}[theorem]{Lemma}`: there cleveref's type and the label's prefix come first when they name a theorem's kind, so "Lemma 2"); the number from the label's `.aux` entry, else the heading's number or the caption's "Figure N:".
- **Source 2, the source** (without hyperref, and "??" and "[?]", which hyperref leaves unlinked): each `cite`, `ref` and `url` mark of prose.json's blocks (`\ref`, `\eqref`, `\cref`, `\autoref`, `\cite…`, `\url`, `\href`) is found among the PDF words of its block's box or parts: the block's display words and those PDF words (letters, digits and ? only; accents and case dropped) are aligned (difflib, no autojunk), and a mark is taken only when all its words align in a row next to an aligned word beside it; leading words printed otherwise ("fig. 1" for prose's "Figure 1") are taken when the PDF words before the aligned ones are unaligned and as many. A label is in the block whose range holds its `\label`, else the block just before it when only white space and labels lie between (a `\label` on the line after `\section`), else the next block of its file, and a label in a file a float `\input`s is that float's; a citation key's entry is the `bibitem` block whose `\bibitem` names it. A label missing from the `.aux` is `missing` ("??"), as is a key in neither the `.aux` nor the bibitems ("[?]"), and, from either source, a label whose `.aux` number is the caption package's `\caption@xref` (a `\label` before `\caption`, printed "??"): "\label{fig:early} comes before \caption, so it names no number.". A run on a hyperref link's words adds nothing but a citation's other keys (above); every other run is a link with `via: "source"`.
- **`links`** (new RPC; paper, round?) → `{"round": n, "path": "<abs>/rN/links.json" | null, "table": links.json's object | null}`, read only and **without `state`** (like `summaries`). It builds the round's prose.json first when missing or stale (under the ledger lock, as `prose` does; "Finding the paragraphs…"), and the table when missing or stale. `table` is null, with no error, when the paragraphs cannot be found (noted in `rN/prose.failed`, not tried again with this PARSER) or the table cannot be built. Refusals: "There is no round yet. Ingest the student's source first.", "There is no round 5.", "Round 2 has no build PDF.". CLI `links P [--round N]`.
- **Not yet**: a footnote or a `\footnote`'s mark without hyperref; a reference typed by a macro prose cannot expand, or printed otherwise than prose prints it (beyond leading words), without hyperref; `\cref{a,b}` with one label missing and hyperref (the missing one has no link); biblatex without hyperref (its .bbl has no `\bibitem`); a table across pages gives only its first part; subfigures give the whole figure without the subfigure's own box; theorems known only by an unprefixed label and no cleveref or hyperref are kind `other`; a `\label` before `\caption` without the caption package names the section (PLAN §4.6's "… so it names Section 3" is not given: the target is titled after the label's prefix); two references to one target, the first ending a line and the second starting the next, are one link. The app's half (§4.1–4.4) comes next.
- **Budgets**: the synthetic promo-demo (8 pages, 81 link rects): building one round's table 0.04 s after its prose pass in the same process (0.12 s on its own, most of it reading the pages' words); a `links` call with a current table about 1 ms. A synthetic 42-page paper with 3958 link rects: 0.42 s after a 0.64 s prose pass, most of it PyMuPDF's `get_links` (quadratic in a page's links); a synthetic 212-page thesis with 5991: 1.4 s after a 4.0 s prose pass, half of it reading again the words of the pages past `anchor._words`'s 64-page cache. Each page's words are indexed once by their centre's y (bisect), so a link costs about the same on any page.
- **Tests** (synthetic, compiled in the test, temporary folders): `tests/test_links.py`: with hyperref (figure, table, formula, section, theorem as whole blocks; `\citep{a,b}` with each link's other key; counts and pages; "??" and "(?)" from the source; a footnote; a URL); without hyperref (the same from the source, `\citep{a,b}` as one link of two keys, the link's rects on its words); cleveref and numeric natbib without hyperref ("fig. 1 and table 1", "[1, 2]"); a page link (LINK_GOTO); subcaption's figure and subfigure on one anchor, a lemma on the theorem counter, a `\label` before `\caption` (caption package); two footnotes on a page, also with the second's link unread; a reference broken across lines joined, three to one formula on consecutive lines kept apart; `javascript:` left out; a damaged, stale or incomplete links.json rebuilt; no .aux or .bbl; failures (the table, the paragraphs, one link) giving no table or one link fewer and never an error, a failed table not built again until prose.json changes; an ingest whose table fails; the RPC and its refusal; both rounds of a copy of samples/promo-demo (every named link in a block of its kind).

The same place across rounds, 2026-10-30 (`design/pdf-reader/PLAN.md` P1.1). Additive and read only: ledger schema stays 1, an app before it never asks, and an engine before it replies "Unknown method 'follow'.", which the app reads as nothing to follow (it keeps the page number).
- **`prose.follow(paper, frm, block, to)`**: round `frm`'s block in round `to` through each round's prose.json `previous` (§ amendment "Overview"): forward the block that continues it, backward the first block of the round before that it continues. None when a round on the way has no current prose.json, its `against` is not the round before or its `previous` does not have one entry per block of the round before, or the block is gone on the way (deleted, new in its round, out of range).
- **`follow`** (new RPC; paper, round, block, to) → `{"round": to, "block": k | null, "exact": bool, "parts": [[page, [x0, y0, x1, y1]], …]}`, read only and **without `state`**, and it never builds a prose.json. The block itself (`exact` true), else the nearest of the 50 blocks before it that has a place in round `to` (`exact` false: a paragraph new in its round, or one the diff could not follow, goes to the one before it). `parts` are block k's in round `to`'s prose.json (as `reading.paragraphs`). block null, parts `[]`: nothing to follow. `round` and `to` must be rounds of the paper, all three numbers ("round, block and to are numbers.").
- **Tests**: `tests/test_overview.py`: `prose.follow` without TeX (forward, backward over two rounds, a gone and a new block, a broken chain, a missing prose.json); `follow` on the four kelp rounds (a paragraph from Round 1 to Round 4 on another page and back, the Conclusion new in Round 4 to its neighbour with `exact` false, the same round, bad numbers).

Notes whole, the band under the paper, 2026-10-30 (`design/comments/PLAN.md` P0.1, the advisor: final.pdf is read on a screen; never smaller type, never a cut note). final.pdf only; the ledger, RPCs and the app are unchanged.
- **One size.** `render.SIZES` (9, 8, 8 and 7.5 pt, the later cut to 3 and 1 lines) becomes `render.SIZE` (head 8.5, note 9, small 8 pt): every margin item is whole on every page, a closed comment's verdict note too (it was at most 3 lines). The deleted words under a comment stay at two lines with "(+N more words deleted)", and a `your change` line at two.
- **A crowded side.** Items stand GAP (6 pt) apart, or closer down to 3 pt. A side that still cannot hold them leaves out its `your change` items first (the summary lists every change no comment asked for), as before; then its tallest comment goes to the band (the lowest of equal heights), one at a time, until the rest fit beside the paper. No item is placed above y 44 or within 24 pt of the paper's foot.
- **The band.** Under the paper's foot the page grows taller (its MediaBox reaches lower; the paper, its margins and every mark keep their coordinates; never wider, since a wider page is drawn smaller at fit width). The band is in the margin's colour across the page, a 0.6 pt edge along the paper's foot, a muted 7.5 pt "Continued from the margin" with a rule, then the comments in their margin boxes, whole, in the reading order of their anchors (the left column first on a two-column paper), across a grid of columns BOX_W wide from 12 pt in to the right margin's x: as many columns as the grid has, or as there are comments, fewer standing in the grid's middle; each comment in turn at the foot of the shortest column (the leftmost of equals), so the band reads across, row by row, and fills the page's width (the advisor, 2026-10-30: a band at the right with its left empty looked unbalanced). The grid's slots are BOX_W + GAP apart at least (as many as fit), so columns never overlap on a narrow page (504 pt). A comment in the band has no leader: a small pointer hangs from the foot of its chip (beside it, the next chip on the line covered it). A page that fits keeps its size.
- **The outline on a grown page.** `set_toc` writes a destination's top as the page's height less y, as if the MediaBox began at 0; `_outline` gives it y less the MediaBox's y0, for the comments and the paper's own items alike, so they land at their words on a page with a band (they landed the band's height too high).
- **`final_pdf`'s return value** (for tests): an item in the band has `"side": "B"` and `"leader": None`.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_final_pdf.py`: a crowded one-column page (a band, the page taller and as wide, every note whole and its id once in the margin or the band, a page that fits keeps its size); an overfull side (changes first, then the tallest comments, the lowest of equals; the margin's items GAP / 2 apart at least; the band on the grid); a note longer than a page (whole, in the band); the outline's comments on a grown page land at their words; a band item's chip has its pointer; the tallest comment goes first wherever it stands; a two-column page with both margins overfull (the band read across, row by row, the left column's comments first, every column of the 5-slot grid used); fewer band items than columns stand in the band's middle; a 504 pt page whose band columns do not overlap.

Checks, 2026-10-30 (`design/pdf-reader/PLAN.md` §6.2 P1.5's engine half; the advisor: one setting turns checks on or off, off by default, nothing computed while off). Additive and read only: ledger schema stays 1, an app before it never asks, and an engine before it replies "Unknown method 'checks'.", which the app reads as no checks. Where §2 (the round's files) or the amendment "Reference previews" (the link table) is silent, this adds to them.
- **`checks.py`** (new; `check.py` stays the folder check of §6.3). `build(paper, lg, n, data, table)` writes **`rN/checks.json`** from round n's build PDF, `.aux`, `.bbl`, `.log` and synctex, its `src` and the `.bib` files the `.aux` names (`\bibdata`) or, for biblatex (its `\abx@aux@` lines in the `.aux`), the main file's preamble's `\addbibresource` and `\bibliography` (read only inside `src`), its prose.json `data` and its links.json `table` (None when the table cannot be built: the checks that read it find nothing): a temporary file named with the process id, then `os.replace`. A cache outside the round's checksums, as links.json is (`disk.round_files` lists neither); cached per process by path, modification time and size. **`CHECKS`** (an int, 4: 2 the fixes of the review of 2026-10-30; 3 a `\todo` only when printed, 4 each check's key, 2026-10-31), **`prose.PARSER`** and **`links.LINKS`** are stored; a file with other values, or one that is not valid JSON or lacks `checks` or `table`, is stale and built again, and so is one built without the table (`"table": false`) once the round has a links.json. The `checks` RPC, given such a file, tries the table again (`links.build_quietly`: once a process when it fails) and builds the checks again when it succeeds.
  - **When**: only the `checks` RPC builds it, when missing or stale, after the round's prose.json and table (built first when missing, as the `links` RPC does); ingest, finish, `prose` and `links` never do, so nothing is computed while the app's Checks setting is off. `build_quietly` never raises: an error leaves no checks (the traceback on stderr; that process does not try again until the round's build PDF, prose.json or links.json changes), and a single check that raises is left out alone ("checks: spelling failed: ZeroDivisionError: division by zero" and its traceback on stderr).
- **`checks.json`**: `{"schema": 1, "version": CHECKS, "parser": PARSER, "links": LINKS, "table": true | false, "round": n, "checks": [Check…]}` (`table`: whether the link table was read).
  - **Check**: `{"code", "group", "text", "detail", "note", "places", "key"}`. **`key`** (`checks._keys`, review 2026-10-31) names the slip alone, for Mark as OK: `"<code>|<ident>"`, where the ident is what the slip is, the same while it is — a float's `\label` (`float_*`), the label (`label_twice`), the .bib key (`no_year`, `no_venue`; `lowered_caps` with the word; `same_work` the keys), the word (`spelling`), the family (`ref_style`) — else the check's `text`; with `"|r<n>"` when only its place knows it (`overfull`, `straight_quotes`, `doubled_word`, `draft_marker`, an empty `todo_note`, a float without a `\label`), so a mark on it holds for its round alone and never passes to another slip; and `"#2"`, `"#3"`, … for the second and later checks of one key in reading order. `group` is `references`, `bibliography`, `consistency`, `typesetting` or `submission`. `text` is the sidebar row, a terse fact without the page (the app adds it): "Figure 4 · not referenced", "“BERT” prints as “Bert” · [2]", "“data set” 1× · “dataset” 1×" (float_before "Figure 2 · before its first citation", float_far "Figure 3 · 3 pages after its first citation": the row shows the pages of their two places). `detail` is one or two sentences for the help tag, what was found and where it came from; `note` a sentence the advisor may send the student, prefilled in a comment; both are always set (the shape allows null). `places`: `[{"page": 0, "rects": [[x0, y0, x1, y1], …], "text": "??" | null}]`, §3's coordinates as links.json's, in reading order (the block of prose.json holding the place's first word, in prose.json's order, then that word's place in the block; a place in no block, such as a margin note or a page, after the last block that starts before it by page, column (build.columns: right of the gap), top and left), one per occurrence; `rects` may be `[]` (a page only) and `places` `[]` (the PDF's metadata); `places[0]` is where a click goes first; `text` is the printed words there ("??", "data set", "Bert"), a source place's "main.tex:42", or null.
  - **Order**: groups in the order above, then by the first place's page and top; checks without places last in their group.
  - English everywhere, curly quotes in `text`, `detail` and `note`; pages in words are 1-based ("p.2"), in places 0-based (PDF page indices, never the `.aux`'s printed page).
- **References**.
  - `undefined_ref` "Undefined reference fig:old": the table's `missing` targets titled "Undefined reference" (a label the `.aux` lacks, and the caption package's `\caption@xref`), each link to one a place (of a link of several keys, only the "??" words in it); then the `.log`'s "Reference `x' on page N undefined on input line M", whose file comes from the log's stack of open files (`(./sections/a.tex` … `)`; a box's printed contents after an `\hbox` message are skipped) and whose line's PDF lines (`anchor.line_boxes`) hold a "??" no place has: one place per warning. detail "The reference to fig:old on p.1 and p.3 prints “??”: there is no \label{fig:old} in this round's source." (or the caption's reason); note "This reference prints “??”: there is no \label{fig:old}.".
  - `undefined_cite` "Undefined citation Nobody2020": the same for citations ("[?]", "(?)", a "?" of "[1, 2, ?]"; the `.log`'s "Citation `x' … undefined", natbib's and biblatex's too; biblatex prints the key, in bold, so that word is the place). For both, a warning whose source line's PDF lines already hold a place of its key from the table (one not taken by another warning) takes no "??". Printed, when no place is found: "??"; for a citation natbib's "?" (the warning was natbib's), biblatex's key, else "[?]".
  - `label_twice` "Label sec:model defined twice" (or "3×"; the note "defined twice" or "defined 3 times"): the `.log`'s "Label `x' multiply defined", or two `\newlabel{x}` in one `.aux`; `@cref` and `sub@` labels skipped. Each `\label{x}` in the source is a place (synctex's lines of its source line, else its block's first part), its text "main.tex:33".
  - `float_unreferenced` "Figure 4 · not referenced": a figure, table or algorithm block with a caption number ("Figure 4: …"), before `\appendix`, none of whose labels (the `\label`s in its source range or the files it `\input`s) the body's source names in `\ref`, `\eqref`, `\autoref`, `\cref`, `\Cref`, `\vref`, `\nameref`, `\subref`, `\pageref`, `\labelcref`, `\cpageref`, `\crefrange` or `\hyperref[…]`, no link of the table reaches, and whose number the text never names by hand ("Figure 4", "Figs. 3 and 4", "Table 2", "Alg. 1", and the numbers between in "Figures 2–4" and "Figures 2 to 4", in paragraphs, items and headings) nor a range of references covers (`\crefrange{a}{c}`, `\Crefrange`, and `\ref{a}--\ref{c}`, `–`, `-` or `to` between two of `\ref`, `\cref`, `\Cref`, `\autoref`: the floats of the two ends' noun numbered between them); a float without a label counts unless named by hand. One per float, not per label.
  - `float_before` "Figure 2 · before its first citation": a float on an earlier page than the first link that reaches it (any of a link's targets; the first in reading order), not on p.1 (a teaser); `float_far` "Figure 3 · 3 pages after its first citation": `FAR` (2) or more pages after it. Places: the float's box, then that first link. Both skip floats after `\appendix` (placed far from the text that cites them by design; a deviation from the request, which named the appendix only for float_unreferenced).
- **Bibliography** (the cited entries only: the `.aux`'s `\citation` or biblatex's `\abx@aux@cite{refsection}{key}`, without `\nocite{*}`'s `*`; the citation as printed, the table's label "Wang et al., 2026" or "[4]"; places: the entry in the references list, the table's target box, else its bibitem block's part).
  - `same_work` "Same work twice · [4] · [5]" (or "Same work 3× · …"): two keys or more whose titles are the same, letters and digits only (at least 10), from the `.bib` (a crossref's fields fill in), else the bibitem's first `\newblock` (a `.bbl` or a thebibliography without a `.bib`).
  - `lowered_caps` "“BERT” prints as “Bert” · [2]": a `.bib` title word outside braces with a capital after its first letter, which the printed entry (the `.bbl`'s TeX, else the PDF's words of the entry) has only in another case; the place is the printed word when found. One per entry, every such word in its detail.
  - `no_year` "No year · [1]": no `year` (nor biblatex `date`). `no_venue` "No venue · [3]": by type, `article` journal|journaltitle, `inproceedings`/`conference`/`incollection` booktitle, `book` publisher, `phdthesis`/`mastersthesis` school, `techreport` institution, `misc` howpublished|url|eprint|note|publisher; other types are not checked. Both need a `.bib`.
- **Consistency** (the PDF words of the paragraphs, items and headings before the references, each in one block; tokens with `/`, `@` or a letter-dot-letter dropped; the two words a hyphen at a line's end breaks are dropped, since the PDF cannot tell "pre-" "training" from "pretrain-" "ing").
  - `spelling` "“data set” 1× · “dataset” 1×": one word in two styles, both in the paper: British and American (modelling, labelled, colour, behaviour and their forms), -ise and -ize, hyphenated and joined (parts of 2 and 3 letters at least), two words and joined (both of 3 letters at least, neither a stopword or a particle: "set up" and "setup" are both right; the joined form must be in the text); never a hyphen against a space; a plural's s folded; case aside. Most frequent first; every occurrence a place.
  - `ref_style` "“Figure 1” · “Fig. 1”", "“Eq. (1)” · “(1)”", "“Table 1” · “table 1”": one family (figures, tables, equations, sections, algorithms, appendices) named two ways in mid-sentence: a name word ("Fig.", "Figure", "figure", "Eqs.", "§"; plurals as their singular) before a number; a bare "(2)" only from the PDF words in a link to an `equation` target, not after a name and a list ("Eqs. (2) and (3)"), never raw text. A word that starts a sentence (after ".", "?" or "!" other than "e.g.", "et al." …, a block's first, or an item's first after its label: "•", "–", "1)", "(a)", "iv.") is left out, so IEEE's "Figure 3" there beside "Fig. 3" and cleveref's "fig." beside "Fig." are fine, and a family written one way throughout gives nothing.
- **Typesetting**.
  - `overfull` "Overfull line · 40.4 pt too wide": the `.log`'s "Overfull \hbox (Xpt too wide) in paragraph|in alignment|detected at line(s) a--b" over `OVERFULL` (1 pt); `\vbox` and Underfull are skipped. The lines a..b of the log's file give their PDF lines (synctex); when the file TeX closed last is `\input` (or `\include`d) by the log's file at a line c with a ≤ c < b, and the log's file's lines a..c hold a paragraph's end (a blank line, `\begin{document}`, a sectioning command, `\par`), the paragraph began in that closed file (its last paragraph, no blank line at its end): its lines a to its end and the log's file's lines c..b are read instead, and the detail says "(sec/one.tex line 1 to main.tex line 41)"; of those, the one whose words (to X + 20 pt past the box's right) hold most of the letters of the box's text (the log's next line, font names and `[]` dropped), then the one reaching furthest right. The place spans the line and its overflow.
  - `straight_quotes` "Straight quotes · main.tex:18": a `"` in the body's source (the main file after `\begin{document}`, the files its blocks come from), outside comments, verbatim-like environments (verbatim, Verbatim, lstlisting, minted, comment, alltt, filecontents), math (`$…$`, `$$…$$`, `\(…\)`, `\[…\]`, equation, align, … and tikzpicture), `\verb` and fancyvrb's `\Verb` (with its `[options]`), an accent (`\"o`), the parts TeX skips after the main file's preamble (`\iffalse … \fi`, unnested, and the comment environment: texdiff's HIDDEN, blanked as comments are, for every check reading the source) and the arguments of `\url`, `\href` (its first), `\includegraphics`, `\input`, `\include`, `\label`, the `\ref` family, `\cite…`, `\texttt`, `\lstinline`, `\todo`, `\usepackage`, and both of minted's `\mintinline` (braced or delimited code) and `\inputminted`; none when csquotes' `\MakeOuterQuote{"}` or babel's German (n)german, (n)austrian or (n)swissgerman options make `"` meaningful. One per source line; the place is the line's PDF words that start with ” (OT1 prints `"` as ”) or hold a `"` (T1), else its PDF lines.
  - `doubled_word` "“the the”": two consecutive PDF words of a paragraph or item, case aside, the first alphabetic of 2 letters at least and not "that" or "had".
- **Submission**.
  - `type3_font` "Type 3 font · DejaVuSans" and `unembedded_font` "Font not embedded · Helvetica": `page.get_fonts(full=True)` by xref, fonts of one name (its 6-letter subset prefix dropped) joined; Type 3, or `ext` "n/a" and not Type 3. Places page by page: the spans set in that font (by name; PyMuPDF names an unnamed Type 3 font "Unnamed-T3"), one rect a line, their text as the place's.
  - `draft_marker` "“TODO” left in": a PDF word `\W*(TODO|FIXME|XXX|TBD|TBA)\W*`, case as written, in a block that is not a reference entry; one per word.
  - `todo_note` "Todo note · “Check the numbers”" (or "Missing figure · …"; "Todo note · main.tex:7" for an empty note): a `\todo` or `\missingfigure` in the body's source, comments and the parts TeX skips blanked, **only when the PDF prints it** (the advisor, 2026-10-31: what the source holds but the PDF hides breaks no rule; only what the PDF shows, or its metadata leaks, counts). Not printed: todonotes with `disable`; a command defined to print nothing (`TODO_HIDDEN`: `\newcommand`, `\renewcommand`, `\providecommand`, `\DeclareRobustCommand` or `\def` with a mandatory argument and a body of `{}`, `\relax`, `\ignorespaces`, `\unskip`, `{{}}` or `\iffalse#1\fi`), in the preamble or in the body before the note; neither counts inside a conditional (`_unconditional`: `\if…` to its `\fi`, nested; a draft switch's other branch), and a command defined only there is not the paper's own. The words looked for (`_todo_words`) are the note's longest run of words without a command, accent, brace or math, its first 4 (`\ref` prints a number, `\"o` an ö), compared joined, so a word hyphenated across lines or a dash between still matches (`_find_words`). A todonotes margin note is looked for outside the text's columns (`text_columns`: the left and right edges most paragraph parts share; a part a margin note widened is outvoted) on the pages its line prints (on any page only when synctex has none for it and todonotes is loaded); an inline note (the `inline` option, todonotes' default by `\setuptodonotes{inline}` unless `noinline`, `\missingfigure`, or the paper's own command, `TODO_DEFINED`) on the PDF lines its line prints, never elsewhere, past as many runs of its words as the line's own text holds; the first word may carry a glued prefix ("[TODO:Check"); a run's words are used, so a later note of the same words is placed at its own. A note without words to look for (empty, or commands and accents only) under todonotes: `\missingfigure` at its "Missing figure", `\todo` at its line's PDF lines. Every other check of the submission group reads the PDF's words, links or metadata already; the source only tells which names to look for (`author_block`) and whether the paper is meant to be anonymous.
  - `coloured_text` "Red text · “This sentence is still a…”" (5 words): a run of spans of one colour, not black or grey (channels within 24 of 255 of each other), in a paragraph or item, outside a PDF link rect of 5 pt or more high (colorlinks: the links, citations and footnote marks); spans outside the median text area of the pages (`build.text_x`; a margin note) are skipped and do not end a run; a run that is a block's first and at most 6 words ending in "." or ":" (a run-in heading a style colours) is left out. The colour is named by the nearest of 15 ("Red", "Blue", "Dark green", …).
  - Only for a paper meant to be anonymous (page 1 prints "Anonymous" followed by "author(s)"/"authors", or `\documentclass` or a `\usepackage` takes anonymous, submission, blind or doubleblind (not `=false`), or review for the classes and packages where it means anonymous (`REVIEW_ANONYMOUS`: acl, acl_natbib, cvpr, iccv, eccv, wacv); acmart only by anonymous (its review numbers the lines), elsarticle (its review doubles the spacing), llncs and IEEEtran never by an option), else silently skipped: `pdf_author` "PDF author · Lin Zhou" (the info dictionary's or XMP's dc:creator, unless it says anonymous; places []); `author_block` "Author named · “Lin Zhou”" (the names, affiliations and addresses of `\author`, `\icmlauthor`, `\IEEEauthorblockN`, `\affiliation`, `\institute` and `\email` in any source file, split at `\\`, `\and`, commas and "and", `\thanks` dropped, of two words or with an @, found as a run of page 1's words; one check, each found a place); `acknowledgements` (a heading, or a paragraph starting, "Acknowledg(e)ment(s)"; its word the place); `personal_url` "Link may name an author · github.com/linzhou" (GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket or Hugging Face user paths, the owner after `datasets/`, `spaces/`, `models/` or `collections/` on Hugging Face when a repository follows it and is not one of its pages (`HF_PAGES`: `datasets/squad` and `datasets/squad/viewer`, canonical ones without an owner, name no one), the owner after `users/`, `groups/` or `organizations/`; `<user>.github.io`; `host/~user` ("www." left out, CMU's `/~./` read as `/~`, a name never ending in "."); names lower-cased where the host ignores case; in the table's URL targets and the PDF's words outside the references (a word under a URL link is the link's, judged by its whole URL; a name cut at a line's end, "nyu-", is none); the hosts' own subdomains (docs., api., help., discuss.) and lookalike hosts name no one, gist. does; organisations (`ORGS`: google, facebookresearch, openai, huggingface, …), reserved paths (`RESERVED`, the hosts' own pages) and placeholders (`PLACEHOLDER`: XXX, anonymous…, user, username, your-…, author, redacted, blind) are fine, and so is anonymous.4open.science. Nothing is guessed beyond that (the advisor, 2026-10-31: a list of datasets' homes errs, and a check is a reminder to mark OK): a dataset's home on a personal path is reported, as is a URL whose line breaks inside its account; a link's printed words are not compared with its target; one per account, each link or word a place).
- **`checks`** (new RPC; paper, round?) → `{"round": n, "path": "<abs>/rN/checks.json" | null, "checks": [Check…] | null}`, read only and **without `state`**. `checks` is null, with no error, when the paragraphs cannot be found (as `links`) or the checks cannot be built. Refusals as `links`: "There is no round yet. Ingest the student's source first.", "There is no round 5.", "Round 2 has no build PDF.". CLI `checks P [--round N]`. `api._built_round` and `api._prose_quietly` are shared with `links`, unchanged in behaviour. Each check of the reply also carries **`ok`** (whether the ledger's `checks_ok` holds its key; not stored in checks.json).
- **`check_ok`** (new RPC; paper, key, ok? default true; 2026-10-31: a check is a reminder the advisor may double-check and mark OK) → `{"checks_ok": [keys]}`, without `state`. Writes the ledger (under its lock): `checks_ok`, a new top-level map (additive, schema stays 1), key → the latest round's number when marked; `ok: false` removes the key (and writes nothing when there is none). Any round, open or closed: the advisor's own note, never the student's, kept for the rounds after while the check's words stay the same. Refusal: "key is a check's key, ok true or false.". CLI `check-ok P KEY [--clear]`.
- **Not built**: the later set of PLAN P1.5 (uncited entries, a venue written several ways, acronyms, heading case, "Figure" split from its number); `undefined_ref`'s place for a "??" the table lacks when the log's file is outside `src` or synctex has no box for its line (the check stays, with fewer places); the command a reference used (detail says "the reference to"); `spelling` and `ref_style` in captions and footnotes; `lowered_caps` misses a word the entry also prints unchanged elsewhere (a booktitle); `author_block` reads names from source commands only (a name typed in a `\begin{center}` title block is not found); two Type 3 fonts PyMuPDF cannot name on one page share their spans; with biblatex, the citation in a row is its key (the table's label), not the printed "Smith 2019"; a nested `\iffalse` ends at its first `\fi`; a bare `huggingface.co/<name>` (an old canonical model, or a user) still counts as an account, and so does a dataset's home under `host/~user` or `<user>.github.io` (marked OK by the advisor when it is one); a margin note TeX hyphenates is not found as words (its place is its line).
- **Budgets**: the synthetic promo-demo (8 pages): a round's checks 0.11–0.26 s in a fresh process after its prose.json and table (most of it PyMuPDF's words and spans of each page), a `checks` call with a current file under 1 ms; a synthetic 21-page paper 0.12 s.
- **Tests** (synthetic, compiled in the test, temporary folders): `tests/test_checks.py`: a five-page defect paper (hyperref with colorlinks, a `.bib` with plain style, anonymous by `\documentclass[anonymous]{article}`) with every code, each asserted on its text, detail and note, and places (page, the PDF words under the rects: each "??", the "(??)" in a table cell only the log knows, the "?" of a citation of three keys, the margin note of a `\todo`, the line past the margin, "Bert:", "”warm”"); the shape and the order; the same paper without hyperref and with a thebibliography (no lowered_caps, no_year, no_venue or pdf_author; titles from `\newblock`); its clean twin (anonymous, colorlinks, a footnote, an uncited entry of the same title) and the fixture paper v1 with no checks; a copy of samples/promo-demo (round 1 reports Figure 2's Type 3 DejaVuSans on p.6, round 2 does not; no spelling or ref_style check; Algorithm 1 two pages after its first reference in both, a true fact; within 2 s); the cache (outside the round's checksums, stale on CHECKS, PARSER or LINKS, damaged, rebuilt; a failing check left out alone); a failing build (no checks, not tried again until prose.json changes), paragraphs that cannot be found, no table (the checks that need none still run; built again with the table once it can be built); a two-column biblatex paper (biber: same_work of 3 keys "3×", no_year, no_venue, an undefined key printed in bold as the place; label_twice 3× and its note; spelling's places in reading order, the left column's lower one first; no ref_style for a list item's "Figure 1" after its bullet beside "Fig. 1"; no float_unreferenced for the middle of `\Crefrange` and `\ref--\ref`; no todo_note or straight_quotes from a comment environment, `\iffalse` or `\Verb`; an overfull paragraph that began in an `\input` file without a blank line at its end); a natbib paper without hyperref of 111 pages (two undefined keys on one line, one "?" each; 220 `\todo` notes that print nothing: none reported, and none either when the command is not seen to print nothing (each looked for on its own line only), todo_note within 0.5 s); a todonotes paper (a margin note starting with a `\ref`, an empty `\todo{}` at its line, `\missingfigure{}` at "Missing figure", an inline note with a dash); ingest writes no checks.json; the RPC, the CLI and their refusals (each check's ok; `check_ok` and `check-ok` marking and clearing in the ledger, read back as ok in the reply; checks.json without it); the keys (unique in the defect paper, by label, .bib key and word, per round for a place; `_keys` alone: the round and the repeats); the rules without TeX (spelling's hyphen against a space, a line's end, a stopword, a one-letter part, British, -ise, a plural;
