ref_style's IEEE and cleveref sentence starts, a bare number only from a link and not in a list; the quote scanner (`\Verb`, minted), the .bib parser, the inner capitals, the accounts (Hugging Face without an owner and its pages, placeholders, case, `/~./`, subdomains, lookalike hosts, owners after users/ and collections/, a cut name); the notes' words, their hidden and own commands, a margin note's run outside the column and joined across a hyphen and a dash, no page without synctex, numbers named by hand in a range, an item's label before a sentence, the anonymity options by class and package).

UI v4 data, 2026-10-31 (`design/ui-v4/IMPLEMENTATION.md` §2 D1–D3; `mac/UX.md` §40.2). Additive: ledger schema stays 1, no new file, no extra I/O; an app before it ignores the new keys, and the app reads their absence (the official app's frozen engine) as an empty ring, never a guess.
- **`summary.answered` and `summary.handled`** (in `state`, `summaries` and `overview`'s `summary`): in the latest round while it is open and built, `answered` counts the carried comments with a verdict (the same comments as `to_judge`, with a verdict) and `handled` the unrequested changes no longer to check (checked, asked about by a comment of the round, or read: the unrequested changes − `to_check`). Both are 0 when the round is closed or its build failed, as `to_judge` and `to_check`; after Resume they are the open round's again. Against the overview's open round, `answered` = `comments.resolved + follow_up + kept` and `handled` = `changes.unrequested − changes.to_check`. The app's status ring fills with (answered + handled) ÷ (answered + handled + to_judge + to_check).
- **`students[].time`**: replaces "A closed last round (with the student, or finished) has no row" (amendment "Overview"). A closed last round whose build did not fail has a row `{"path", "round": n, "next": null, "open": false, "last": true, "you": closed_at − created, "student": today − closed_at (the days so far), "received": null}`, with `student` null when the ledger's `paper.finished` is set (the app also drops it for its own Finished mark not yet adopted). Only these rows carry `last`. A failed last round still has none (a closed round before it keeps its trip, an open failed round its open row).
- **Asking for the hierarchy** (the app's choice, amendment "App, hierarchy"): `marks` true, for the thumbnails' highlights. Measured on the synthetic promo-demo set (four papers, 8 pages, 2–3 rounds): the reply grows from 74 KB to 85 KB, 7.2–22.5 KB a paper (+0.5–4 KB, 8–73 mark rows); under the 60 KB a paper at which the app would keep only the latest round's marks, so every round's marks are kept.

Shell escape per paper, 2026-11-01 (the advisor's report: a student's paper built with "preamble.tex:28: Package minted Error: You must invoke LaTeX with the -shell-escape flag." and a PDF showed; decided with the advisor: per paper, off by default, offered from the build's error, remembered for the paper, no global setting; `mac/UX.md` for the app). Additive: ledger schema stays 1; an app before it never sends the parameter and ignores the new keys; an engine before it (the official app's frozen one, until `release.sh`) ignores `ingest`'s parameter (it builds without, as always), refuses an `edit_paper` given only it ("Give the paper's title or the student's name.") and sends neither state key, which the app reads as nothing to offer. Where §5 (`compile`'s command) or §7 (`ingest`'s parameters) conflict, this wins. Measured with TeX Live 2023, latexmk 4.79, minted 2.7, Pygments 2.21.
- **`project.compile(src, build, main, engine, progress, shell_escape=False)`**: off, the command and its environment are exactly as before (§5), save the fixed `-e` Perl both modes now carry (§5 and Security below; `SAFE_AUX`, new 2026-11-01). On: `-shell-escape` in place of `-no-shell-escape`; the folder of the engine's own Python (`os.path.dirname(sys.executable)`, not resolved: the venv's `bin`, which has Pygments' `pygmentize`, there for pytest) last on latexmk's PATH, as an app launched from the Finder has no other (without it minted says "You must have `pygmentize' installed"); and `-usepretex=\PassOptionsToPackage{outputdir=../build}{minted}`, the build folder relative to src from the engine's own paths, only when it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+$` (latexmk puts it in `"…"` in a shell command line), so minted 2 finds what pygmentize wrote with `-outdir` and keeps its `_minted-<job>/` in build (without it: "Missing Pygments output; … you may need the outputdir package option", and `_minted-main/` written into src). The `-g` call after LaTeX errors runs the same command. The jobname, the PDF's and the log's names, and synctex's files and lines are as without (tested; main file names with a space, `#`, `'` and Chinese checked by hand). Progress: "Compiling with pdflatex and shell escape…". The build result, and so the round's `build` in the ledger, gains **`shell_escape`**: true or false (a round built before has none: false).
- **`project.needs_shell_escape(build)`**: true when the build was made without shell escape, has errors, and either a package or class asks for it, in an error or in a warning other than a notice it prints as it loads, or epstopdf's figure is missing. Asking: `(?:Package|Class) \S+ (?:Error|Warning): .*(?:shell[ -]escape|write18)`, case aside, once the names the message quotes in `` `…' `` are taken out (the student's: natbib's "Citation `shell-escape' on page 1 undefined"; svg's own `` `shell escape' `` stays). The notices, said whether anything needs it: `Package (?:shellesc|epstopdf|ifplatform|gnuplottex) Warning: ` (shellesc's "Shell escape disabled", which standalone, svg and minted load; epstopdf's "Shell escape feature is not enabled"). epstopdf's figure: `` File `…-eps-converted-to.pdf' not found ``. So minted's "You must invoke LaTeX with the -shell-escape flag", standalone's "Shell escape needed to create graphic!", tikz's external figure ("… this is 'pdflatex -shell-escape'"), svg's "You didn't enable `shell escape' (or `write18') so it wasn't possible to launch the Inkscape export for `figure.svg'" with the export it misses, and epstopdf's missing conversion ask. A build without errors never asks (a template loading standalone, svg or epstopdf, built clean; gnuplottex's graphs left out, which is no error), nor one whose only errors are its own beside such notices (an undefined command, a figure not sent). LaTeX's own messages never count: they quote the student's labels and file names (an undefined `\ref{sec:shell-escape}` asks for nothing). Derived, never stored. (The review, the same day: any package's warning counted, so a clean build that loads standalone or epstopdf, or natbib's warnings about the keys `shell-escape` and `write18-attack`, offered shell escape.)
- **The paper's choice**: **`paper.shell_escape`** in the ledger, true or absent (absent is off; off is never written as false, so a paper never turned on stays byte for byte as before). **`ingest`** takes **`shell_escape?`**: given, it is stored on the paper and used for this build (saved with the new round only: a refused or failed ingest keeps the one before); not given, the stored one is used. **`edit_paper`** takes **`shell_escape?`** the same way, without a build: for the next round when the open round cannot be built again (it has comments), and to turn it off; given alone it is enough. Both refuse anything but JSON true or false, null too, changing nothing: "shell_escape is true or false.". CLI: `ingest P SRC [--shell-escape | --no-shell-escape]`, `edit-paper P [--shell-escape | --no-shell-escape]`; with neither, nothing is sent.
- **State** (additive): `paper.shell_escape`; `round.build.shell_escape` (how the round shown was built) and **`round.build.needs_shell_escape`**, derived for whichever round is shown, so a round built before this (the advisor's minted paper) asks too. The app offers shell escape from a build's errors while `needs_shell_escape` is true.
- **Trust does not travel**: a paper that comes in from a file starts with it off. `archive_unpack` (Add, Put back, Keep Both) takes `paper.shell_escape` off its new folder (in the ledger write of a fork, else in one of its own: a new version), and `swap_in` (Use This Copy) off the incoming folder before the renames (`archive._distrusted`); a paper without it comes in byte for byte as before. A copy made here (`fork`) keeps what its ledger says. A version put back (`put_back_ledger`) keeps the paper's choice as it is now, as it keeps the paper's id, never the version's: one in .history from before an import took it off would turn it on without the advisor (off when the ledger cannot be read). An export holds the ledger as it is. A review folder the app lists where it is, new to its list (Add Existing…, Import of a folder), goes through no engine call that takes it off: the app sends `edit_paper` `shell_escape: false` when the folder's state has it on (`mac/UX.md` §41). The engine's one call of `compile` is ingest's, for the paper's own round; nothing else builds with shell escape.
- **Security**: shell escape lets the student's source run any command as the advisor (`\write18`): read, change or delete their files, or send them anywhere. So it is off by default, per paper, turned on only by the advisor's `ingest` or `edit_paper`, never global, never brought in from a file. Off, LaTeX runs with `-no-shell-escape` (stricter than TeX Live's default, which allows a list of programs). `-norc` keeps a student's `latexmkrc` (Perl, run before anything else) out either way (tested: one that asks for shell escape and leaves a file never runs). The engine adds no student string to the command line: the main file stays one argv item, and the pretex holds only the engine's own path. latexmk reads an argument that starts with `-` as its own option, and runs TeX through `/bin/sh` with the main file's name in `"…"` (it refuses `$`, `\`, `%`, `~` and an unbalanced `"` there, not a backquote), so `compile` now refuses such a main file before anything runs, with or without shell escape: "The main file's name -draft.tex starts with - or has a `, which latexmk would misread. Rename the file." (before, such a name went to latexmk as it was). It puts the output folder in `"…"` in the same command lines, where the shell reads a `$`, backquote, `"` or `\` of the review folder's path, with shell escape or without (probed: a build folder under a folder named `o$(touch MARK)o`, or one with backquotes, left MARK in src), so `compile` also refuses a src or build path with one, before anything runs: "The folder path /…/Why `make` hangs/r1/build has a $, `, " or \, which latexmk would run as part of a command. Rename the folder that has it." (the command for any other path is as before). New folders' names drop the four: `archive.safe_name` (an export's folders, and so an imported paper's) as the app's `DataFiles.name` (a new paper's folder, from its title). latexmk also builds the command lines for bibtex, biber and makeindex from the names of auxiliary files the student's LaTeX chooses (imakeidx's `\makeindex[name=…]`, multibib's `\newcites{…}`), and runs them through `/bin/sh` with the name in `"…"`, where a backquote or `$(…)` runs, off or on and whatever `-no-shell-escape` otherwise stops. These names exist only after TeX has run, so `compile` cannot screen them beforehand. Instead it passes a fixed `-e` Perl snippet (`SAFE_AUX`, no student string in it) that makes latexmk run those three as its own `internal` commands calling one sub, which runs the program as an argv list (`system { $p } $p, @_`), never a shell; latexmk splits the substituted arguments itself (`parse_quotes`). The rest of the command, and the `*latex` run, are unchanged (probed: imakeidx and multibib documents naming `` x`date>MARK`x `` left a file MARK before and leave none now). So with shell escape off the main file, the folder paths and the names bibtex, biber and makeindex are run on no longer reach a shell, and `-no-shell-escape` stops `\write18`. Two other latexmk 4.79 paths handed such names to `/bin/sh` too (found by reading its source, 2026-11-01; both predate shell escape per paper; `BACKLOG.md` D17): its kpsewhich sub, which looks up `\bibdata` and `\bibstyle` names and names read from the log through `open $fh, "$cmd|"` with the names unquoted, and `parse_aux1` and `parse_bcf`, which open an .aux or .bcf named inside the .aux with Perl's two-argument `open` (a name ending in `|` runs). `SAFE_AUX` now also replaces latexmk's `kpsewhich` with one that runs kpsewhich as an argv list (`open(my $fh, '-|', 'kpsewhich', @_)`), and wraps the two readers so a name with a `|` is refused (they return 0, as for a file they cannot open) and any other goes to latexmk's own sub. Tested: in plain Perl with latexmk's subs stubbed, a name with a space and a `;` reaches kpsewhich whole and nothing else runs, and names with a `|` are refused; real builds with a bibliography in an `\include`'d chapter (kpsewhich and `parse_aux1`'s recursion) and with biblatex and biber (`parse_bcf`) still resolve. On, `\write18` runs as the advisor intends, and a package such as imakeidx may itself run makeindex through it; `SAFE_AUX` keeps only latexmk's own bibtex, biber and makeindex calls off the shell, in both modes.
- **Tests** (synthetic: `tests/fixtures/shell-escape`, temporary folders): `tests/test_shell_escape.py`: the minted paper without it (minted's error in preamble.tex, the PDF, `needs_shell_escape`) and, on an app's PATH, with it (no errors, the listing printed, `_minted-main/` in build, src as the snapshot; skipped, with the reason, when pygmentize is neither beside the tests' Python nor on that PATH); the choice stored, used by the next ingest, changed by `edit_paper` (no new version when unchanged) and by `ingest`, both ways; "yes", "true", 1, 0, null, [true] and {} refused by both, the ledger unchanged; a `\write18` that leaves `ran.txt` only with it; a latexmkrc never run, off and on; the command and environment as before when off, with the pretex and PATH when on, the `-g` call the same, no pretex for "../a b/build"; main files named "-draft.tex" and "notes`v2`.tex" refused (nothing run); src and build paths with `$(…)`, a backquote, `"` or `\` refused, off and on (nothing run, nothing made), ingest into a review folder named "Why `make` hangs" refused with the paper as it was, `safe_name` dropping the four; `needs_shell_escape` for minted (also from a file outside src), standalone, svg and epstopdf failing for want of it, not for the notices of packages loading (with an error of the build's own or none), svg's warning alone, natbib's keys (with an error or none), LaTeX's own messages, a figure not sent, nor a build made with it; real builds: a paper loading graphicx, epstopdf, standalone, svg and natbib with `\citep{shell-escape,write18-attack}`, clean and with an undefined command, not asking, and svg's and epstopdf's missing conversions asking; Checks to the student, 2026-11-01 (`design/checks-ui/PROPOSAL.md`, `design/checks-ui/mockup.html`; the advisor's decisions of that day: a problem the program finds goes to the student to verify, every check goes out unless the advisor marks it OK; checks are told apart from comments by their ids, marks, summary section and email group; a check no longer found closes itself). Replaces "nothing reaches the student unless the advisor makes it a comment" (amendment "Checks", `mac/UX.md` §39). Additive: ledger schema stays 1; an app before it never asks `check_items` and finishes without `checks`, so its rounds send no check, byte for byte as before.
- **Check items** (ledger, new top-level map **`check_items`**: id → item; and per round **`last_check_seq`**, as `last_seq`): a check that is or was going to the student. Item: `{"id", "key", "code", "group", "created_round", "rounds": {"<n>": Record}}`. Record: `{"state": "new" | "still" | "fixed" | null, "text", "detail", "note", "places", "student_note"?, "advisor_note"?}`. Ids **`A<n>-<seq>`** (`A2-03`: the round it was first found in, its number in that round, never reused: `last_check_seq` only grows, and is inherited as `last_seq` when an open round is replaced).
  - **`check_items`** (new RPC; paper, round?, groups? = all five) → `{"round": n, "items": [Item view…], "closed": {"fixed": [ids], "ok": [ids]}}`, without `state`. Item view: the item's fields and its round-n record's, with `ok` (its key in `checks_ok`), `history` (its earlier records, `{"round", "state", "student_note"?, "advisor_note"?}`) and `carried` (created before n). For the **latest round while open**, it first **syncs** round n with the round's `checks.json` (refused "The round's checks are not built yet." when it is missing or stale: the app asks `checks` first). Syncing, in the ledger lock:
    - Each current check of the `groups` asked matches an open item: the same key; else, for a check whose key holds `|r<k>` (known by its place only), an open item of the same code whose first place's text is the same, else whose text is. **Open**: an item whose latest record with a state, before n, is `new` or `still`, and whose key is not in `checks_ok`.
    - Matched: its round-n record becomes `still` with the check's text, detail, note and places (its `student_note`, if ingest put one, kept). Not matched: a new item, `new`, the next id.
    - An open item whose group was asked and that no check matched: `fixed`. An item of a group not asked keeps a record without a state (not checked this round).
    - An item created in round n that no check matches any more (the checks were rebuilt after a fix to the paper's round, a group turned off) loses its round-n record and, having no other, goes; its id is not given again.
    - Idempotent: a second sync with the same checks writes nothing.
  - A closed or earlier round is read as recorded, never synced.
- **Ingest** (`_carry`): each open item gets a round-n record `{"state": null}`, with `student_note` when the student wrote `% A2-03 …` (`notes.find` takes `A` ids as `R` ids: any case, brackets, full-width forms; a note on an id that is not an open item is a warning, `unknown` or `closed`, as for comments).
- **`check_note`** (new RPC; paper, id, text): the advisor's words on a carried item in the open round (its round-n `advisor_note`; empty text removes it), the follow-up of a check. Refused for an item not open in the open round. Returns the item's view.
- **`check_ok`** stays: Mark as OK is the key in `checks_ok`. Before it is sent, an item marked OK never goes out; a carried item marked OK in round n closes as "no change needed" (`closed.ok`: its key's round in `checks_ok` is n).
- **`finish`** takes **`checks?`** (default false): true when the app's Checks setting is on. True: the round's items are synced once more when `checks.json` is current (else as last synced) and go out. False: round n's check records are dropped first (nothing goes out; the open items stay open for a later round).
- **What goes out** (round n): the items whose round-n record is `new` or `still` and whose key is not in `checks_ok`; `fixed` and `closed.ok` as closed. final.pdf (`render.py`):
  - **Marks**: each place's rects burnt in as a highlight in the slate `CHECK_FILL` (#6E8092 at 0.30), never one of the six comment colours; the chip square (radius 0) in slate `CHECK_INK` (#4E5D6C) with white text and the status sign (`+` new, `○` still); the margin box headed "A2-03 · new · automatic check" (or "· still open"), in slate, holding the note, the detail, the advisor's note when given ("Me: …"), and a muted last line "Found by Paperole and it may be wrong: fix it, or reply % A2-03 with why it is right."; boxes share the margin and the band with comments by their place.
  - **Summary**: `REPLY_RULE` names both ids ("% R1-03 your note, or % A2-05 for an automatic check"); To do: "Comments (k): …" then "Automatic checks (k): …" (each line only when it has chips); the Key adds the check chip, "an automatic check, found by Paperole, not by me"; after "New comments in this round" a section **"Automatic checks (k)"**: the muted sentence "Paperole found these, not me, and it can be wrong. Fix each, or reply with % A2-03 why it is right.", then "Still open" and "New", each by group (References, Bibliography, Consistency, Typesetting, Submission), one line a check: chip, page, text; then "Fixed since Round m: A2-01, A2-04." and "No change needed: A2-05." when there are any. The outline adds "Automatic checks (k)".
  - **email.txt**: after the comments' groups, "Automatic checks (k)" with entries `A2-03  p.12  <text>`, the note indented, "Me: …" for the advisor's note; the closed line adds "Fixed: …" and the items closed OK under "no change needed".
- **`state`** (additive): `round.checks_sent`: the number of items that went out with the round when closed (else null), for the round card.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_check_items.py` (synthetic, temporary folders): ids and their numbering across a replaced round; sync matches by key and by place text, idempotence, fixed, groups not asked, a rebuilt round dropping its own items; ingest's records and an `% A2-03` note (and the warnings); `check_note`; `check_ok` before and after sending; finish with and without `checks`; final.pdf's chips, highlights, margin boxes (PDF words), the summary's section, To do, Key and outline, and email.txt.
- **As built** (2026-11-01, `check_items.py`, new; decisions where the above was silent):
  - The round keeps the groups `check_items` was last asked for (`check_groups`), and finish syncs with them (all five when never asked), so a kind turned off never comes back at Finish.
  - Syncing matches the open items and those created in round n (marked OK or not), so a second sync is idempotent. A check marked OK that matches nothing makes no item. A matched item takes the check's current key (a place-known key's `|r<n>` changes each round). A place-known check falls back to the place's words or text only among items whose key is place-known too.
  - A fixed record copies the latest earlier record's words, with no places. A record of a group not asked is `{"state": null}` with any notes.
  - Finish with checks drops the items created in round n and marked OK (never sent: a later Not OK must not send them as still open), so a note on one is `unknown`. `closed.ok` lists only carried items marked in round n.
  - Warnings for A ids: "A1-05 was closed in Round 1 (no change needed)" (or "(fixed)"); "There is no automatic check A1-09, so this note is ignored." A note on a check attaches no change.
  - Replacing an open round is refused, as for comments, when a check item has an advisor note in it; otherwise its records go and `last_check_seq` is kept.
  - final.pdf: one chip and one box per check per page with places, at its first place there, every place of that page highlighted; places without rects only in the summary. The box: header, note, detail, "Me: …", the muted line. The reply rule's and the intro's example is the first id that goes out; To do drops its "(k)" when checks go out; "Still open" / "New" only when some is still open; "Fixed since Round n−1"; the section only when something went out, was fixed or marked OK.
  - email.txt's closed line keeps its form: "Closed: R1-03, A1-02 (no change needed); A1-04 (fixed)".
  - `checks_sent` (ledger, on the round) is written by a finish with checks (0 allowed) and removed by every finish first. `email_preview` takes `checks` too.
  - CLI: `check-items P [--round N] [--groups a,b]`, `check-note P ID [TEXT]`; `finish` and `email-preview` take `--checks`.
  - Review fixes (2026-11-01): a view's `ok` holds only when its key was marked in round n or before (an earlier round never shows a later Mark as OK). A check known by its place that matches an item closed as OK in an earlier round (same code, its place's words or text) makes no new item, so the student is not sent again a slip whose reason the advisor accepted. `round.checks_fixed` (ledger and state, like `checks_sent`): how many were closed as fixed in the round, for the round card.
  - Tests: `tests/test_check_items.py` (16), and an identical final.pdf and email for a finish without checks (checked against the code before).

References, 2026-11-01 (`design/pdf-reader/PLAN.md` P1.3 as rewritten that day; the advisor: check that each cited work is real, cited right, cited in its published form, and that the list is written one way; the program only points at problems, the student fetches any official BibTeX by hand; online look-ups only with their settings on, Google Scholar slowly in the background). Adds to the amendments "Checks" and "Checks to the student"; ledger schema stays 1.
- **Entries** (`refs.py`, new): the cited entries (as `checks._Paper` finds them for `same_work`, `no_year`: the .aux's citations, the .bib and .bbl, each entry's box in the PDF from the link table, else its bibitem block), each `{"key", "label" (as printed, "[12]" or "Dosovitskiy et al., 2021"), "type" (bib type or null), "title", "authors" [as written in the .bib, "Last, First" or "First Last"], "year", "venue" (journal | booktitle | howpublished … as written), "arxiv" (the id from eprint with archivePrefix arXiv, a journal or note "arXiv preprint arXiv:…", "CoRR abs/…", or an arxiv.org url), "doi", "pages", "url", "preprint" (Paperlib's rule: venue empty, or containing arxiv, biorxiv, medrxiv, chemrxiv, openreview, corr), "printed" (the entry's PDF words joined, as the PDF shows it), "place" (page, rects, text)}`. Without a .bib the fields come from the .bbl's blocks where they can be told, else null.
- **Format checks** (group bibliography; judged from what the PDF prints, so a style that makes a field uniform reports nothing; a minority is reported only when the entries compared are 4 or more and the minority is at most a third; places: the minority's entries; idents in brackets, stable while the slip is):
  - `venue_forms` "One venue written 3 ways · NeurIPS · NIPS · Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems" (venues folded: case, years, ordinals "34th", "Proceedings of (the)", "In", punctuation; a small alias table `VENUES` of the common CS and ML venues joins their short and long names; ident: the venue's folded name). `venue_style` "Venues: 9 short, 14 in full" (a venue printed as its short name, an all-capitals token of 2–8 letters, possibly with a year, against one printed in words; ident "venues").
  - `author_names` "Authors: 12 entries with full first names, 5 with initials" (an entry whose first names are all initials against one whose are all words; ident "first names"); `author_case` "Author name in lower case · kaiming he · [14]" (a printed name all in lower case; ident: bib key).
  - `title_case` "Titles: 14 in title case, 4 in sentence case" (printed titles: the PDF's words of the .bib title; title case when most words of four letters or more begin with a capital, sentence case when none after the first does save acronyms and names the .bib protects; ident "titles").
  - `etal_by_hand` "Author list cut to “et al.” · [12]" (a .bib author field ending in "and others", while every other entry of more than three authors prints them all; ident: bib key).
  - `leftovers` "Stray “{” · [8]" (printed `{`, `}`, `\`, "..", ", ,", "()" or a spacing accent ¨ ´ ` ˆ ˜ ¸ alone; ident: key and the leftover).
  - `field_mix` "DOI on 6 of 31 entries" or "Pages missing on 4 of 18 conference papers" (per type, inproceedings and article apart, for doi, pages, url, publisher, volume, number; a field prints when its value's words are in the entry's printed words; ident: type and field).
  - `page_dash` "Page ranges: 12 with a dash, 3 with a hyphen" (printed ranges `\d+–\d+` against `\d+-\d+`; ident "pages").
  - `arxiv_forms` "arXiv written two ways · arXiv:2010.11929 · CoRR abs/2010.11929" (ident "arxiv").
- **`references`** (new RPC; paper, round?) → `{"round": n, "entries": [Entry…], "results": {"<source>": {"<key>": Result}}}` without state, read only: the entries (built with the round's checks, kept in `rN/refs.json` with the results) and the results stored for them.
- **`ref_results`** (new RPC; paper, round, source: "semantic_scholar" | "google_scholar", results: {key: Result}) → `{"stored": k}`: writes them into `rN/refs.json` (a cache outside the round's checksums, atomically; merged by key, a later result replacing an earlier; any round, open or closed), so the round's checks are stale and built again. Result: `{"found": bool, "title", "year", "venue", "authors": [first author first], "citations": int | null, "url", "doi", "arxiv", "record" (the source's id of the work), "published": venue | null (the venue when the work is not a preprint), "closest": {"title", "year", "authors"} | null (when not found)}`. Unknown keys are refused: "A result for reference x, which round 2 does not cite.".
- **Online checks** (group bibliography, built from `refs.json`'s results only, nothing fetched by the engine):
  - `published_preprint` "[12] arXiv preprint · published at ICLR 2021" (a preprint entry whose Semantic Scholar result has `published`; note "Cite the published version (ICLR 2021)."; ident: bib key).
  - `no_record` "[19] · no record" (every source looked up found nothing, Semantic Scholar at least; detail the closest match "Closest: <title> · <year> · <first author>", note "Check that this work exists and that its title is right."; ident: bib key).
  - `record_differs` "[7] · year 2020, published 2021" (and "title differs", "first author differs": titles compared after folding case, TeX, accents and punctuation; the year by more than one, except a preprint's; the first author's last name; ident: key and what differs).
  - `same_work` also when two keys have one `record`.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_refs.py` (synthetic papers compiled in the test): the entries' fields from a .bib (arXiv forms, DOI, preprint), each format check positive and negative (a style that hides a field reports nothing), the RPCs, results merged and refused, the online checks from stored results, checks rebuilt when results change.
- **As built** (2026-11-01; decisions where the above was silent):
  - The alias table is `refs.VENUES` (`checks.VENUES` already names each type's venue field). The format and online checks live in checks.py.
  - The minority rule applies to every "written several ways" check, `venue_forms` included (that venue's printed entries); `author_case`, `etal_by_hand` and `leftovers` are per entry. Texts say "two ways" for 2.
  - `venue_forms`: venues are joined only through `VENUES` (with "long name (SHORT)"), a workshop is not its conference, preprint venues are skipped. NIPS (before 2018) against NeurIPS is reported, as asked.
  - `venue_style`: short is one word, all capitals of 2–8 letters or a `VENUES` short name; full is three words or more.
  - `author_names` is judged per author: an entry mixing the two forms is not counted. A .bib name is read as printed in initials only with "J." beside its last name.
  - `title_case` needs two words of four letters or more, not counting the first, a subtitle's first or a word with an inner capital.
  - `etal_by_hand` needs another entry of more than three authors that prints them all.
  - `leftovers`: url words are skipped; an accent is alone only when its glyph overlaps no letter, so TeX's `\"o` extracted as "Sch¨olkopf" is not reported.
  - `field_mix`: the type's noun ("conference papers", "journal articles"); `conference` is inproceedings; preprints are left out; volume and number for articles only.
  - `page_dash`: BibTeX styles turn "-" into "–", so it fires on lists written by hand.
  - `arxiv_forms` tells three forms apart ("arXiv:…", "CoRR abs/…", an arxiv.org url alone).
  - `published` is used as given; the app puts the year in it. The text names the server ("arXiv preprint", "bioRxiv preprint"), else "preprint".
  - `record_differs` uses Semantic Scholar's found result only, and the year rule literally (more than one apart).
  - `same_work` by record is skipped when the keys already share a title.
  - Entries:
    - only cited keys with a .bib entry or bibitem;
    - authors without "others"; year a string (biblatex `date` as fallback); venue the first of journal, journaltitle, booktitle, howpublished, school, institution, publisher;
    - arXiv ids without their version; text in NFC;
    - without a .bib, the fields come from the bibitem's blocks.
  - checks.json's `"refs"` is the digest of the results it was built from (`CHECKS` 5), so after `ref_results` `check_items` refuses until `checks` builds again (the app asks `checks` first); identical results keep it current.
  - `ref_results` builds the entries first when refs.json has none and validates `found` only; an empty map stores nothing. An error building the entries leaves them empty and the other checks run.
  - The final.pdf box's last line is now "Fix it, or reply % A2-03 with why it is right." (the header says "automatic check"; the summary's intro keeps the disclaimer).
  - Review fixes (2026-11-01):
    - an entry is a preprint only when its venue names a server or it has an arXiv id (an empty venue alone made a hand-written list's plain "In ICLR 2021", or a @misc page, a preprint);
    - `published_preprint` is left out when the entry already prints that venue;
    - `no_record` is left out for a misc / online / software / dataset entry with a url;
    - `venue_style` compares conference papers only (short conference names beside journals in full is one usual style).
  - Tests: `tests/test_refs.py` (11).
